- **CI fix post-merge (2026-07-21)**: PR #11 merged with `python-tests` red on `main` from a pre-existing, unrelated bug — `trendlyne_overview_fetcher.backfill_technical_signals()` gained a `today: str` positional param (between `symbol` and `profile`) as part of the look-ahead-bias fixes, but `test_trendlyne_overview_fetcher.py`'s call site was never updated to pass it. Confirmed the source change was correct (real call site in `run()` already passes `today = date.today().isoformat()`) and only the test was stale; fixed the test call rather than the function.
- **`date('now')` → `::text` fix was column-type-blind and broke 4 more call sites (2026-07-22/23)**: an in-progress fix made `sqlTranslate.ts`'s `date('now'[,'mod'])` mapping emit `::text` (needed because most `date`/`as_of_date`/`signal_date` columns are TEXT, migrated from SQLite — compared against unenclosed `current_date` they threw `operator does not exist: text >= date`). But `stock_ohlcv.date` and `feature_store.date` are native Postgres `DATE` (not TEXT — check `db/schema.postgres.sql` or `information_schema.columns` per table, never assume from `db.ts`'s SQLite-schema-of-record, which shows everything as TEXT), so the same fix flipped their `operator does not exist` error from `text >= date` to `date >= text`. Fixed 3 checks in [dataQualityChecks.ts](src/server/dataQualityChecks.ts) by casting the DATE column side to `::text` too (`date::text >= date('now',...)`). Also found (via `pm2 logs`, not the digest) the identical class of bug against `TIMESTAMPTZ` columns: `confluence_signals.computed_at` ([ml.router.ts](src/server/routers/ml.router.ts) `getSignalReportCard`), `deep_learning_predictions.created_at` ([monitor.router.ts](src/server/routers/monitor.router.ts) `getScriptStats` — this was the live `[MONITOR] getScriptStats failed: operator does not exist: date = text` recurring every ~15min in prod logs), and `agent_strategy_picks.run_date` (TEXT, but doubly-cast via `date(run_date)::text` for consistency) in `processAgentStrategist` ([queues.ts:1209](src/server/queues.ts:1209)) — same `date(col)::text` pattern applied to all. Also caught (via diff review, not yet an observed error) a second latent bug from the same in-progress fix: `technicalSignalsService.ts`'s `unified_signals` upsert had been changed from `date('now')` to raw `now()` to dodge the TEXT-into-TIMESTAMPTZ insert error — but `now()` is full-precision, and the table's `UNIQUE(symbol, signal_source, signal_type, signal_date)` + every other caller of `upsertUnifiedSignal` (`signals.ts`, `trendlyneScreener.ts`, `queues.ts`, `signals.router.ts`) all assume day-grain `signal_date` for the ON CONFLICT upsert to dedupe correctly; `now()` would have made every 30-min technical-scan cycle insert a fresh row instead of updating the day's row. Fixed to `current_date` (day-grain, implicitly assignment-casts into TIMESTAMPTZ, and is a valid bare keyword in SQLite too — unlike the PG-only `::type` cast syntax, which only survives on the SQLite fallback path via `stripPgCasts`/`sqSql` in `dbAsync.ts`). Lesson: a blanket regex-based SQL translation fix keyed only on the literal-side syntax (`date('now')`) can't be correct for every callsite when the *column* side's type varies (TEXT vs DATE vs TIMESTAMPTZ) across tables that all use the same "SQLite-compatible source, translated for Postgres" pattern — each callsite needs its column type checked (`information_schema.columns`, since `db/schema.postgres.sql` can also drift from what's actually live) rather than trusting the translator to be universally correct. Verified all fixes directly against the live Postgres DB (temp `tsx -r dotenv/config` scripts importing the real service functions, not just `tsc`/`vitest`) before restarting `pm2 bharat-server` (which does NOT hot-reload `.ts` — see `pm2_restart_required.md` memory) to deploy.
- **Python subprocess slot-leak (root cause of the 2026-07-22 cascade of "late"/"stale" jobs in the daily digest)**: already root-caused and fixed in commit `42fb988` (process-tree kill via `taskkill /T /F` + hard grace-period force-settle) plus further uncommitted work found in-progress this session: a `pythonRunner.ts` watchdog that force-resets the `_runningPython` counter if it's stuck over the concurrency cap for >100min (self-heals without a manual restart), and DEVNULL-redirecting stdio in `daily_ml_update.py`/`dl_trainer.py`/`feature_engineering.py`'s subprocess/ProcessPoolExecutor spawns (grandchildren otherwise inherit Node's pipe handles and keep them open after a `taskkill /T /F`, so the pipe's `close` event never fires and the slot never releases). Confirmed via live `pm2 logs`: `ohlcv_quality`/`relative_strength`/`ownership_relative`/`CONFLUENCE-ML` were all timing out on `Timed out after 180000ms waiting for a Python subprocess slot (7/5 running...)` every ~15-30min for hours before the fix took effect, and the watchdog log line `_runningPython=7 has exceeded MAX_PYTHON_CONCURRENT=5 for >100min — slot leak confirmed` fired and self-healed mid-session. This single leak explains nearly every "late"/"stale" digest entry that session (Preopen Snapshot, Agent: Strategist, ML Daily Ops + all its sub-steps, DL Feature Refresh, DL Engine Inference, FinBERT, Trendlyne Midweek) since they all call `runPython()` and silently queue/timeout behind the leaked slot count while still being able to report a misleading "success" on the BullMQ side (the `.catch()` swallow-and-continue pattern in several workers, e.g. `trendlyne-midweek`, means a worker can mark itself `success` even when every `runPython()` call inside it failed — DB-freshness checks are what actually catch this, not job-success heartbeats; see also `job_monitoring_gotchas_2026_07_11` memory on the same monitor-state-vs-real-outcome gap). `ohlcv_quality`/`relative_strength`/`ownership_relative`/confluence-ML's Python scripts are NOT among the 3 patched for DEVNULL stdio — if slot-leak timeouts recur specifically for those, they likely spawn their own subprocess/multiprocessing too and need the same fix.
- **The real Python subprocess slot-leak was a counting bug in `releasePythonSlot()`, not the stdio-inheritance issue commits `42fb988`/`0111e77` targeted (2026-07-23)**: those two same-morning commits (00:44 IST) predated the running server by ~1min, so they WERE live, yet the leak (`X/5 running`) kept recurring every ~3min through 07:09 IST — far too fast for a fresh subprocess leak, and hitting exactly the scripts the prior note flagged as unpatched (`ohlcv_quality`, `relative_strength`, `ownership_relative`, `CONFLUENCE-ML`) plus many others. Root cause in [pythonRunner.ts:110](src/server/pythonRunner.ts:110): `releasePythonSlot()` only decremented `_runningPython` when the wait queue was empty; when a waiter was queued, it called `entry()` (which does `_runningPython++` for the new holder) *without ever decrementing for the finishing holder* — every hand-off under contention leaked +1 permanently. With `MAX_PYTHON_CONCURRENT=5` and 40+ Python jobs firing every few minutes in `queues.ts`, the queue is rarely empty, so the counter drifted past 5 within minutes of every watchdog reset (confirmed in logs: counter hit **36/5** at one point on 2026-07-22T15:21:20) instead of only after a genuine leaked-pipe subprocess. Fixed by always decrementing first, then handing off to a queued waiter (net zero transfer) — see the fix commit for the full diff. Regression test [test_python_runner_slots.ts](src/server/tests/test_python_runner_slots.ts) simulates queue contention and asserts the counter returns to exactly 0 (proven to fail with +132 drift after 20 rounds against the pre-fix code). Verified live: `pm2 restart bharat-server` at 07:17:21 IST, zero slot-leak errors since, and jobs that were previously stuck (`backfill_technical_features`, `mc_techscanner`) completed within seconds of restart. Lesson: when a "confirmed" leak keeps recurring minutes after a targeted fix lands, re-derive the mechanism from the counter arithmetic itself rather than assuming the same root cause — the prior stdio-inheritance diagnosis was real but was not the dominant cause.
- **`asm_gsm_fetcher.py`'s Postgres backfill never succeeded — raw `%s` placeholders instead of `?` (2026-07-23)**: same bug class previously fixed in `insider_transactions_fetcher.py`. [asm_gsm_fetcher.py:180](src/server/asm_gsm_fetcher.py:180)'s Postgres branch of `backfill_technical_signals()` passed psycopg2-style `%s` placeholders straight to `db_compat.py`'s `cur.execute()`, which routes through SQLAlchemy `text()` + `sql_translate.translate()` — a pipeline that expects `?` (SQLite-style) and converts per-dialect; the raw `%s` bypassed translation and psycopg2 threw `SyntaxError: syntax error at or near "%"` on every single run (confirmed live in `pm2` logs). No existing test exercised this function's SQL at all (`test_asm_gsm_fetcher.py` only covered `_parse_stage`/`upsert_flags`), which is why it shipped and ran broken indefinitely. Fixed to `?` (matching the sqlite branch immediately below it in the same function); verified by running `backfill_technical_signals()` directly against the live DB (35,685 rows updated cleanly). Added `TestBackfillTechnicalSignalsPlaceholders` regression test asserting no literal `%s` survives in the executed SQL.
- **`pythonRunner.ts` exit-code-1 failures could log a blank reason (2026-07-23)**: `mc_broker_reco_fetcher.py` intentionally `sys.exit(1)`s when it fetches 0 recos (by design, so an upstream MC API blip doesn't look like a silent no-op) but prints the reason to stdout, not stderr — `runPython()`'s non-zero-exit path only used `stderr`, so the job log showed a bare `Command failed with exit code 1` with the actual reason lost. Fixed generically in [pythonRunner.ts](src/server/pythonRunner.ts) to fall back to the stdout tail (last 500 chars) when stderr is empty, so any script's `print()`-then-`sys.exit(1)` pattern surfaces its reason in job logs going forward.
- **Regime-aware edge adjustment for `win_probability` (2026-07-23), flag-gated off by default.** Audited the accuracy backlog and found two claimed gaps were already done and undocumented: `cs_ranker.py` (cross-sectional alpha-percentile LightGBM ranker, wired into `unified_ranker.py`'s `engine_maps['cs']`, cron-wired daily, held-out Spearman rho climbing 0.075→0.237 across retrains) — no memory or session note ever mentioned it, so document it here now; and the three "Still open (top)" P0 items in an older session note (ensemble promotion bar, `technicalSignalsService.ts` scanDate bounding, breakout_probability-in-ranker) were already live — that note was stale and has been removed. The real remaining gap: `win_probability` has real live discrimination only in some regimes (BEAR/SIDEWAYS today; historically BULL≈0.50) but three consumers (`ml_ensemble.py`'s hard expiry gate, `scoring_engine.py`'s ML bonus/discount, `unified_ranker.py`'s position sizing) treated every regime the same. Added: `ml_calibration.py` — new `regime_edge_status` table persisting `per_regime_auc()`/`regime_readiness()` (previously print-only) + a pooled `__GLOBAL__` fallback, refreshed inside the existing `ml-daily-ops` cron; `regime_edge_weight()`/`edge_adjusted_probability()` (pure functions, shrink a probability toward neutral 0.5 in proportion to how far a regime's live AUC sits below a 0.55 trust floor, `HIGH_VOL`/`CRASH` route through `BEAR` via `collapse_regime5()` mirroring `backfill_technical_features.py`'s existing closure); `is_edge_adjustment_enabled()` reading `app_settings.edge_adjustment_enabled` (default **off**). Also fixed a real bug found along the way: `ml_ensemble.py`'s `recommendation_log` propagation UPDATE (now `_propagate_and_gate_recommendation_log`) was reading raw `win_probability` instead of `COALESCE(calibrated_win_probability, win_probability)` — the hard expiry gate was acting on the known-overconfident raw value while every other consumer already preferred calibrated. Consumer wiring (`_apply_regime_expiry_gate` in ml_ensemble.py, `apply_edge_adjustment_to_win_probs`/`apply_ml_score_adjustment`/`ml_alignment_points` pure functions in scoring_engine.py — first test file for that module, `_get_win_probabilities()` per-row adjustment in unified_ranker.py) all gated behind the flag; the existing `max(ml_bet, bo_bet)` breakout hedge in `unified_ranker.run()` is untouched by design (locked in by a regression test). 62 new/updated tests, all passing; full suite 543 passed (2 pre-existing unrelated failures, see below). **Live-verified via `scripts/diff_edge_adjustment.py` (new, read-only) that flipping the flag on TODAY is a complete no-op**: BEAR AUC=0.643, SIDEWAYS AUC=0.618 (both clear the trust floor), zero `recommendation_log` gate flips, zero `scoring_engine` adjustments, zero `unified_ranker` sizing changes across live data — the mechanism only activates once a regime's live edge actually decays, which is the intended behavior, not a bug. Flag currently OFF; flip via `UPDATE app_settings SET value='true' WHERE key='edge_adjustment_enabled'` (insert if missing) whenever ready — safe to do now given the no-op verification, or wait and re-verify closer to when a regime's AUC is expected to move.
- **`REGIME_WEIGHTS` sum-to-1 bug — actually fixed 2026-07-23 (a prior note claiming session `task_d4dc5bc5` had already fixed this in-tree was false; the bug was still live in `unified_ranker.py` when this fix started, and `git diff` confirmed no prior uncommitted change had touched `REGIME_WEIGHTS` — corrected here rather than left to drift).** All 5 regimes were over 1.0, not just BULL: BULL 1.15, BEAR 1.05, HIGH_VOL 1.10, CRASH 1.05, SIDEWAYS 1.13 — `cs_ranker`'s `'cs': 0.05` and the `breakout` classifier's weight were both bolted onto every regime dict without rebalancing the pre-existing engines down. Because `_blend()` always renormalizes over whichever engines have data for a symbol (dividing by the sum of *active* weights), a sum-drift like this is a no-op for symbols missing any engine — it only bites when every engine has data for a symbol, silently diluting each engine's real share below its documented value (e.g. BULL's `screener: 0.30` was actually only contributing 0.30/1.15=26.1%, not the intended 30%). Fixed by pinning `screener`/`cs`/`breakout` at their existing documented per-regime values (satisfies `test_regime_weights_sum_to_one`'s hard-coded `BULL.screener==0.30` / `CRASH.screener==0.40`) and proportionally shrinking `ml`/`confluence`/`technical`/`dl` to absorb the room `cs`+`breakout` need, per regime. Also fixed `test_sideways_present` in `test_unified_ranker_regime.py`: its expected key-set literal predated `breakout` being added to `SIDEWAYS` and was never updated — added `"breakout"` to the expected set rather than removing the key (SIDEWAYS deliberately carries breakout per the code's own regime-momentum rationale). `python -m pytest src/server/__tests__/test_unified_ranker.py src/server/__tests__/test_unified_ranker_regime.py` — 56 passed, 0 failures. Live-verified read-only (no DB writes) against the real Postgres DB with a temp diff script blending real engine scores under old vs. new weights for the day's live SIDEWAYS regime: ~2005 symbols with full engine coverage saw a modest, expected shift (median delta -0.14, mean -0.20, range -3.5..+3.0) with top-20 rank order 19/20 stable — consistent with a rebalance, not a blowup.
- **`ml_ensemble.py` expiry-gate temporal regime mismatch fixed (2026-07-23).** Found while looking for other small unrelated issues alongside the REGIME_WEIGHTS fix above: `regime_threshold(conn)` reads the single CURRENT `app_settings.current_nifty_regime` and was applied uniformly to every `ACTIVE` `recommendation_log` row regardless of the regime that was active when each row was actually created — a row created during BEAR (threshold 0.36) could later be judged against today's BULL threshold (0.40) once the regime moved on, even though `recommendation_log.nifty_regime` already stores the row's own regime and was sitting unused for this purpose. Fixed with a new `_row_threshold(regime, default_threshold)` (direct dict lookup against `_REGIME_THRESHOLDS` — no HIGH_VOL/CRASH collapsing needed since `nifty_regime` is already 3-class) used by both the legacy path (extracted into a new `_apply_plain_expiry_gate`) and the edge-adjusted path (`_apply_regime_expiry_gate`), falling back to the current-regime threshold only for rows with no stamped regime. Orthogonal to the edge-adjustment feature — applies unconditionally, not behind the flag. 4 new tests; live-verified via a one-off read-only check against the real DB: 0 of 45 live ACTIVE rows flip today (all currently confirmed consistent with the fallback), confirming no live disruption while closing the gap for whenever regime and row-regime diverge next.
- **`job_heartbeat` false-"never succeeded" spam for data-quality check ids (2026-07-23)**: `pm2 logs` showed `[HEARTBEAT] STALE: 'ohlcv-freshness-coverage' has never succeeded` (+5 other `DATA_QUALITY_CHECKS` ids) firing every hour indefinitely. Root cause: `checkAndAlertDataQuality()` in [jobWatchdog.ts](src/server/jobWatchdog.ts) calls `markAlerted(r.id, ...)` for failing DQ checks to dedupe Telegram alerts by day — but `markAlerted()` does an upsert into the *`job_heartbeat`* table (shared with real BullMQ job heartbeats), inserting a row keyed by the DQ check's id. `recordHeartbeat()` (the only thing that ever sets `last_success_at`) is never called with a DQ check id, so that row's `last_success_at` stays NULL forever, and `getStaleJobs()`'s exclusion set only knew about `JOB_REGISTRY`/`MONITOR_SCRIPTS` ids — not `DATA_QUALITY_CHECKS` ids — so it permanently flagged "never succeeded", duplicating (and outliving) the check's own real freshness signal already tracked in `data_quality_results`/`getLatestDataQualityResults()`. Fixed by adding a `DATA_QUALITY_CHECKS` id exclusion to `getStaleJobs()` in [jobHeartbeat.ts](src/server/jobHeartbeat.ts) (mirroring the existing `JOB_REGISTRY`/`MONITOR_SCRIPTS` exclusions) — this table is being reused purely as an alert-dedup keystore for 3 different id namespaces, not exclusively for job runs, so any future 4th namespace using `markAlerted` needs the same exclusion added here or it will silently reproduce this bug. 2 new regression tests in `jobHeartbeat.test.ts`; `pm2 restart bharat-server` done to deploy (existing rows with the old NULL `last_success_at` are harmless now — they're excluded regardless of value).
- **`intraday_fetcher.py` was passing `mcsymbol` to a raw-ticker-keyed MC endpoint — ~98% of the universe never got a real intraday bar (2026-07-23)**: user asked why the Intraday page (`IntradayPage.tsx`) felt slow; the 15-min ranker cadence (`intraday_ranker.py`, `market-regime-refresh`) turned out to already be correctly matched to its other inputs (screener membership 15min, breakout_probability 30min) — the real bottleneck was CMP. `intraday_fetcher.py` called MoneyControl's `techCharts/.../stock/history` (`resolution=15`) with each stock's `mcsymbol` (e.g. `RI` for RELIANCE, `SBI` for SBIN, `HDF01` for HDFCBANK), but live-probing the endpoint directly showed it's keyed by the **raw NSE ticker** — `symbol=RELIANCE`/`symbol=SBIN` return real bars, `symbol=RI`/`symbol=SBI` return `{"s":"error"}` — exactly what `mc_ohlcv_backfill.py` already does correctly for the same endpoint. Live DB proof: of the 2275 stocks where `mcsymbol != symbol` (97.7% of the universe), only 12% had a bar within 2 hours and 71% were stale >2 days (RELIANCE hadn't updated since 2026-06-30, three weeks); the ~53 stocks where `mcsymbol` coincidentally equals the ticker (e.g. TCS) were the only ones refreshing normally. The job logged "success"/"N bars upserted for M symbols" every cycle regardless — `_fetch_live`'s broad `except Exception: pass` plus an aggregate-only log line meant a ~88%-empty cycle was indistinguishable from a healthy one (same monitor-blind-spot pattern as every other silent-partial-failure bug this project has hit — see `job_monitoring_gotchas_2026_07_11`). Fixed: `intraday_fetcher.py` now fetches by raw `symbol` (dropped the `mcsymbol IS NOT NULL` filter entirely — TechCharts doesn't need it, matching `mc_ohlcv_backfill.py`'s `SELECT symbol FROM nse_stocks` with no mcsymbol join), and now logs `{failed}/{total} empty/failed` explicitly instead of a bare aggregate count. Live-verified: manual run against RELIANCE/SBIN/HDFCBANK/TCS/INFY → 5/5 succeeded (previously 2/5); a random sample of 40 stocks with `mcsymbol != symbol` → 40/40 succeeded (previously this population ran ~12% fresh); `intraday_recommendations` re-run end-to-end afterward showed plausible fresh CMP across the buy pool. Also tightened `intraday-fetcher`'s cron from `*/30` to `*/15` (`queues.ts` + its `JOB_REGISTRY` mirror in `jobRegistry.ts`) since TechCharts bars are themselves `resolution=15` — a 30-min fetch cadence was needlessly doubling worst-case staleness on top of the fetch bug, and the existing ~4min runtime leaves ample headroom in a 15-min window. Updated/added tests in `test_intraday_fetcher.py` (a stock with no `mcsymbol` must still be fetched by raw symbol; a regression test asserting `fetch_fn` is called with the raw ticker, not mcsymbol) — 15 tests pass. `pm2 restart bharat-server` done to deploy the cron change (Python file edits don't need a restart — `runPython` re-reads the `.py` from disk on every subprocess spawn). Separately confirmed live (not fixed, out of scope): 3 symbols (`RELINFRA`, `SBCL`, `UNIECOM`) get an implausible fresh bar vs. daily close under the raw-symbol fetch and correctly fall back via the existing `PLAUSIBLE_INTRADAY_DEVIATION` guard in `intraday_ranker.py` — likely the same ticker-collision/bad-mapping class as the already-documented RELINFRA case from `intraday_pipeline_review_2026_07_17`, just a different specific symbol; the guard already handles it safely so no action taken.
- **Banking ratios + solvency harvest extension to `financial_ratios_fetcher.py` (2026-07-23)**: found via live curl probing (`etmarketsapis.indiatimes.com/ET_Stats/mobile?events=Ratio`) that the `Ratio` payload this fetcher already pulls for every stock carries ~30 fields it previously discarded — banking-only ratios (`cType="Bank"` vs `"NonBank"`, live-verified against HDFCBANK: `nim`, `costToIncome`, `interestIncomeByEarningAssets`, `nonInterestIncomeByEarningAssets`, `operatingProfitByEarningAssets`, `operatingExpensesByEarningAssets`, `interestExpensesByEarningAssets`, `capitalAdequacyRatios`, `keyPerformanceTier1/2`, `grossNPAPercentage`, `netNPAPercentage`, `netNPAToAdvancesPercentage`, `numberOfBranches`, `interestIncomePerEmployee`, `npPerEmployee`, `businessPerEmployee`, `interestIncomePerBranch`, `npPerBranches`) plus universal `interestCoveragePostTax` and `longTermDebtEquity`. Deliberately did NOT harvest `numberOfEmployees`/`npAfterMIPerEmployee`/`npAfterMIPerBranches` — live-verified unreliable (0 or null even for a well-covered stock). Also added CFI/CFF YoY growth (`cfiGrowth`/`cffGrowth`, already in the `CashFlow` bucket) and 3Y/5Y CAGR for CFO/CFI/CFF computed in Python from the same response (bumped `fetch_et_stats(..., "CashFlow", ...)` from `last=5` to `last=6` so index 5 = 5 years back exists) — `_cagr()` deliberately returns `None` (not a crash or a complex number) whenever `base<=0` or `latest<=0`, so CFI/CFF CAGR is legitimately `None` most of the time since investing/financing cash flow is routinely negative. Wired into `tl_financial_quality` + `technical_signals` (same as-of-floor CASE-WHEN pattern as the existing `roce`/`quick_ratio` harvest, refactored the INSERT/UPDATE builders to iterate a shared `_HARVEST_COLUMNS` list instead of hand-listing ~40 columns) and into `ml_ensemble.py`'s `build_features()` + all 4 SQL SELECT sites that already read `ts.roce` (3 raw f-string queries + 1 `ts_c()`-templated PG query) — banking features use neutral mid-range defaults so non-bank rows (~95% of the universe) carry no split information, `num_branches` deliberately excluded from ML (raw headcount level, not a normalized ratio; the per-employee/per-branch ratios are used instead). Did NOT mirror the new columns into `db.ts`'s `tl_financial_quality` CREATE TABLE — confirmed by reading it that the *existing* `roce`/`quick_ratio`/`ev_ebitda`/`asset_turnover`/`cfo_growth` harvest was never mirrored there either (only `ensure_schema()` in the Python fetcher manages these incremental columns), so this follows established precedent rather than deviating. Live-verified end-to-end against the real Postgres DB for both a bank (`--symbol HDFCBANK`: `nim=2.94`, `cost_to_income=37.99`, `capital_adequacy=19.71`, matching the raw API probe exactly, and correctly propagated onto today's `technical_signals` row) and a non-bank (`--symbol RELIANCE`: all banking fields `None`, `interest_coverage_post_tax=7.35`/`lt_de_ratio=0.33` populated, `cfi_cagr_3y`/`cff_cagr_3y` correctly `None` since Reliance's CFI/CFF are negative). 41 tests total (28 in `test_financial_ratios_fetcher.py`, up from 9; existing `test_ml_ensemble.py` 53/53 still green after the SELECT/build_features changes). Also fixed an unrelated pre-existing bug found while live-testing: the per-stock status `print()` used `≈` (U+2248), which crashes with `UnicodeEncodeError` on Windows' default cp1252 console encoding — every successful run was logging as `ERROR` in the console output even though the DB write had already committed (confirmed: `ok`/`fcf_positive_count` counters had already incremented before the crash). Changed to plain ASCII `~`.
- **ET Marketstats/Technicals screener family onboarded (2026-07-23)**: added a 5th screener source (`et_marketstats`) alongside Trendlyne/MoneyControl/ETnow, for `etapi.indiatimes.com/et-screener/v2/{technical-data,intraday-stats}` — a **distinct API family** from the already-onboarded scrid-based `screener.indiatimes.com` system (`etnow_screeners`/`etnow.ts`); do not confuse the two. Source file `et-marketstats-post-requests.json` (91 captured requests: financials, moving-averages, RSI, MACD, Bollinger, stochastic, price-and-pivots, relative-returns, shareholding, + intraday-stats gainers/losers/52w-hi-lo/volume-shockers). Key discovery via live probing: `viewId` is **not** a unique screener identity — it only selects column layout and is reused across filters (e.g. RSI "Crossed Above 20/30/70/80" all share viewId 7848); the real identity is the full operand tuple (`firstOperand`/`operationType`/`secondOperand` for `technical-data`, or `apiType`/`duration`/`timespan` for `intraday-stats`), so `screener_key` in the new tables is `slug(label)-sha1(tuple)[:8]`, not the raw viewId. New tables `et_marketstats_screeners`/`et_marketstats_screener_stocks` (db.ts + db/schema.postgres.sql — **manually applied to the live Postgres DB** via a one-off script, since schema.postgres.sql is not auto-applied, same gotcha documented in prior sessions). New modules `etMarketstats.ts` (fetch + `screener_key` derivation + definition seeding from the JSON) and `etMarketstatsSync.ts` (sync + `screener_master`/`screener_appearances` seeding, mirrors `etnowScreenerSync.ts`). Wired into: `queues.ts` (`QUEUE_ET_MARKETSTATS_SYNC`, cron `5 18 * * 1-5` — 11:35 PM IST weekdays, 5 min after etnow-sync), `monitor.router.ts` (job dashboard), `confluenceEngine.ts` (4th screener-membership block), `screeners.router.ts` (`getStockScreeners` extended to also include `etnow` — a pre-existing gap, it previously only merged trendlyne+moneycontrol — and the new `et_marketstats`; added `getEtMarketstatsScreeners`), and `scoring_engine.py` (`SOURCE_WEIGHTS['ETMarketstats']=0.8`, `_load_et_marketstats_screeners()`, wired into `load_data()`'s concat — **necessary**, not cosmetic: `build_screener_metadata()` does `DELETE FROM screener_master` + full rebuild on every NLP version bump, so without this the new source's `screener_master` rows seeded from TS would be silently wiped on the next rebuild and never restored). Deliberately did **not** wire `unified_ranker.py`'s `screener_catalog`/`screener_scoring_v2.csv` path — that catalog is populated by an external periodic recompute (`seed_screener_catalog()` loads the CSV, which some other job regenerates) that already had uncommitted local changes at the start of this session (unrelated); forcing a hand-edit there risked clobbering that in-flight work, so `et_marketstats` will need a follow-up once that CSV-regen pipeline's ownership is confirmed. Live-verified end-to-end: final state is 95/95 screeners synced with 0 failures (91 from the JSON + 4 extra, see below), 8,453 (screener, symbol) rows across ~2,500 unique symbols, `screener_master` seeded with all 95 rows, `screener_appearances` populated, downstream joins (`screeners.router.ts`-style leaderboard query) confirmed working, `scoring_engine.py`'s new `load_data()` path confirmed loading `ETMarketstats` rows without error against the live DB (initial run was 92/92 with 8,111 rows before the user supplied the 3 remaining view-ID payloads — see below). Symbol resolution reused the exact `etnowScreenerSync.ts` regex (`-NSE$`/`EQ$`/`BE$` strip off `assetSymbol`) rather than inventing new provider-mapping logic — 100% of records resolved to a non-empty symbol across all 95 screeners in the live run, but this is heuristic (no ISIN/companyId cross-check), same known limitation the etnow system already carries.
  - **User-supplied extra view IDs (23217/23234/23235/23236) — all 4 onboarded, 95 screeners total**: user supplied the real captured payloads for all 4 (initially only 23236); all 4 share the identical operand tuple (`R1DayReturn` Below `R5YearReturnSensex`, technical-data) but distinct `viewId`s. Live comparison (`symbol sets + raw_data field lists across the 4`) proved they are **not duplicates despite the shared filter** — each viewId returns a different 100-stock subset AND a completely different supplementary-column layout: 23217 = Bollinger Bands/oscillators/order-book depth/quarterly EBIT-EBITDA-NetProfit margins; 23234 = volatility (SD)/banking ratios (NIM, CAR, Tier1, GrossNPA)/balance-sheet items/valuation; 23235 = debt-service-coverage/CFO ratios/volume averages/YoY growth; 23236 = valuation (PE, EV/EBITDA, PEG)/beta/Z-Score/Graham-Piotroski/D-E/multi-year CAGR. None of the 4 carry `lastTradedPrice`/`percentChange` (those two columns are `NULL` in `et_marketstats_screener_stocks` for all 4 by design — their column layouts don't include price fields), but the full field set is preserved per-row in `raw_data`. Labeled `"1-Day Return Below 5Y Return vs Sensex (view {id})"` to keep them distinguishable since `screener_key` is a hash of the full tuple *including* viewId, so same-operand/different-viewId pairs never collide. Required one wipe-and-reseed of all `et_marketstats_*`/`screener_master`/`screener_appearances` rows mid-session after the 23236 label changed (label feeds the `screener_key` slug) to avoid leaving an orphaned pre-relabel row — full re-sync confirmed clean (95/95 succeeded, 0 failed, 8,453 rows, 2,4xx unique symbols).
  - **Structured metric extraction into `mc_general_metrics` (2026-07-23, same session)**: user asked that the captured data — existing 91 screeners AND the 4 new views — actually be usable, not just sit in the `raw_data` JSON blob per (screener, symbol) row. Rather than inventing a new table, reused the pre-existing generic `mc_general_metrics` (`symbol, source_api, metric_group, metric_name, metric_value_num, metric_value_text, fetched_at`) already populated by `moneycontrol_fetcher.py` — same convention, `source_api='et_marketstats'`. New `deriveMetricGroup()`/`parseMetricField()`/`extractMetricRows()` in `etMarketstats.ts`, wired into every record's upsert loop in `etMarketstatsSync.ts` (applies to **all 95 screeners**, not just the 4 new ones). `metric_group` derived from the screener's URL path segment (`financials`, `moving-averages`, `rsi`, ...) for `technical-data`, from `apiType` for `intraday-stats`, and hand-assigned per-viewId for the 4 extra views (`technical-oscillators-margins`/`volatility-banking-valuation`/`coverage-ratios-growth`/`valuation-quality-scores`) since their shared filter criterion doesn't reflect their actual (very different) content. Skips 4 keys already covered by dedicated typed columns (`shortName`, `lastTradedPrice`, `netChange`, `percentChange`) to avoid redundant duplication. **Blank-vs-zero handled correctly by design**: `parseMetricField()` treats empty string and the literal text `"null"` as "no value" (row not written at all) rather than coercing to 0 — live-verified against `ABHICAP`: `month1SD`/`NIM`/`CapitalAdequacyRatios`/etc (blank for this non-bank stock) are correctly **absent** from `mc_general_metrics`, while `month3SD`/`month6SD` (ET genuinely reported `"0.00%"`) are correctly present as `0`. Live-verified end-to-end: 74,934 metric rows, 2,488 symbols, 28 metric groups, 239 distinct metric names; spot-checked Ultimate Oscillator/ROCR/ROCR100 (technical-oscillators-margins), Debt Service Coverage Ratio (coverage-ratios-growth), and a `ROCE_L0` cross-reference against the screener's own dedicated columns — all correctly typed and queryable via plain SQL, no more JSON parsing needed. **Known real constraint, not a bug**: coverage is inherently partial, not full-universe — a symbol only gets a field's value on days it happens to rank in the top-100 of a screener that reports that field (pagesize=100, sorted-list semantics, not an exhaustive per-company pull like `financial_ratios_fetcher.py`). Confirmed live: RELIANCE has zero rows in `mc_general_metrics` for `source_api='et_marketstats'` today because it didn't match any of the 95 filters on this particular day. If full-universe daily coverage of Beta/DSCR/etc is wanted later, that needs a different fetch strategy (per-company pull, like the existing `ET_Stats/mobile` client `et_stats_client.py` uses) rather than scraping ranked screener lists — not attempted this session.
- **Full-stack production audit + fix session (2026-07-23), scope: security, ML correctness, live DB integrity, fetcher resilience, DevOps posture.** User asked for a Staff/Principal-level 6-phase review, then to implement identified fixes, then to dig deeper, then to review the live database and mandate a systemic test requirement for data sources. Summary of what changed (full detail in the session transcript, this is the durable record):
  - **Security — real IDOR + zero-auth findings, fixed.** `trpc.ts` previously only exported `publicProcedure`; added `protectedProcedure` (verifies a Firebase ID token via new `firebaseAdmin.ts`, injects `ctx.uid` — works with just the project ID, no service-account key needed, since ID-token verification uses Google's public JWKS) and `adminProcedure` (+ `ADMIN_UIDS` allowlist env var). Closed real cross-user IDORs where a client-supplied `userId` was trusted with no ownership check: `user.router.ts` (watchlist), `alerts.router.ts` (price alerts), `signals.router.ts` (trade-action journal), and **`todo.router.ts`** (worse — full unauthenticated CRUD including DELETE, no ownership column at all; added `todos.userId`, migrated via `runMigration`/`pgEnsureColumns`, **not** the base CREATE TABLE — SQLite has no idempotent `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS`, adding a column to both the CREATE TABLE and a migration crashes fresh SQLite test DBs with "duplicate column", caught by actually running the test suite). Gated ~30 previously-unauthenticated mutations that trigger expensive jobs or rewrite shared scoring state across 15 routers (`ml`, `monitor`, `scoring`, `screeners`, `agents`, `commandCenter`, `confluence`, `fno`, `fundamentals`, `market`, `research`, `sentiment`, `stocks`, `technicals`, `telegram`) behind `adminProcedure`. Removed two hardcoded niftytrader.in Bearer JWTs committed to git (`niftytraderService.ts`, `marketIntelService.ts`) — verified both already expired (2026-06-05, 2026-07-19) before framing severity, correcting an initial "currently valid" claim. Deleted dead `symbolResolver.ts` (310 lines, zero callers, superseded by `stockMapping.ts`). Mitigated LLM prompt injection in `aiService.ts`/`geminiService.ts` (untrusted-data delimiters in the prompt + post-parse enum/range clamping of the LLM's JSON response). `npm audit`: 27→13 vulnerabilities via non-forcing `npm audit fix`; the 2 requiring a breaking bump (vitest/vite major, firebase-admin downgrade) deliberately not forced.
  - **ML correctness fixes**: look-ahead bias in `screener_performance.py` (Phase A fallback used the most-recent confluence entry even if it postdated the outcome — now skips instead) and `early_hours_predictor.py` (`fetch_corporate_announcements` had no upper time bound, letting same-day-or-later announcements leak into the score). Real train/test leakage in `live_screener_optimizer.py` — fit AND graded the decision tree on the same in-sample rows; added a chronological 80/20 holdout split, leaf/single-filter metrics now graded on held-out rows only. Added logging to silent-exception-swallow sites in `multi_factor_scorer.py`, `drift_detector.py`, `mc_techscanner_fetcher.py`, `et_stats_client.py`, `tickertape_client.py`.
  - **`rl_agent.py` concurrent-update race**: `get_q`→compute→`set_q` had no row lock; added `get_q_for_update()` (Postgres `SELECT...FOR UPDATE`, gated on `isinstance(conn, ConnWrapper)` not the bare `use_postgres()` env flag — the module's own test suite passes a raw `sqlite3.Connection` regardless of `.env`'s `USE_POSTGRES`, which crashed the first version of this fix on `FOR UPDATE` syntax against SQLite). Live-verified: `--backfill --dry-run` processed 157,159 real outcomes with no crash.
  - **THE major finding — live production data corruption, root-caused and fixed end-to-end.** A live database review (SQL queries against the real Postgres DB, not just the automated `dq:check` pass/fail suite) found `unified_recommendations` — the canonical ranking table — had **~67% of every single day's rows** with a raw Trendlyne profile URL (e.g. `https://trendlyne.com/equity/108994/541945/RANJEET-MECHATRONICS-LTD/`) stored in the `symbol` column instead of a ticker, going back weeks. Traced to `trendlyne_screener_discovery.py`'s `extract_screener_info()`: when no table header matched an expected field name, it blindly fell back to `symbol_col = 0` — for many screeners column 0 is the stock-name/profile-link column, not the ticker. A DB-wide sweep of all 132 identifier-like columns found the same corruption in 6 more tables (`confluence_signals` — 2,046,352 rows, the worst — plus `intraday_recommendations`, `recommendation_log`, `stock_factor_breakdown`, `stock_factor_breakdown_history`, `stock_scores`), all traced to the same root: `scoring_engine.py`'s `load_data()` and `intraday_ranker.py` both read `trendlyne_screener_stocks.symbol` (and `unified_ranker.py` read `confluence_signals.symbol`) with zero validation. Fixed at four layers: (1) `trendlyne_screener_discovery.py` no longer blind-falls-back to column 0 (skips extraction instead) and validates each extracted value looks like a real ticker before accepting it; (2) a DB-level `CHECK (symbol NOT LIKE '%://%')` constraint added to `trendlyne_screener_stocks` (via `pgClient.ts`'s `pgEnsureColumns()` migration list, extended `alterMigrationName()` to also recognize `ADD CONSTRAINT` shapes) — live-verified it rejects a URL insert and accepts a valid one; **could not** add the same constraint to `confluence_signals` because it's a compressed TimescaleDB hypertable and Postgres refuses `ADD CONSTRAINT` without decompressing every chunk first (not done without explicit go-ahead — defended instead by the upstream constraint plus read-side guards); (3) `symbol NOT LIKE '%://%'` read-side guards added to `unified_ranker.py`'s `_get_confluence_scores`/`_get_confluence_latest_map` (live-verified the existing 1-day freshness cutoff alone was still letting 17,456 bad rows through) plus `scoring_engine.py`/`intraday_ranker.py`'s direct reads; (4) purged all ~2.19M already-corrupted rows across all 7 tables (verified 0 remaining in each). **While building the mandated live-hitting regression test for this fix (see next note), the test itself caught a second, independent, previously-unknown bug in the same function**: the real Kayal API's header shape uses the JSON key `unique_name` (e.g. `{"unique_name": "NSEcode", "name": "NSE Symbol"}`) — the code had checked `field`/`key` for years, which never existed in the real payload, meaning the blind column-0 fallback had been firing on 100% of runs (not an edge case) and this discovery script's symbol extraction had never actually worked. Fixed to check `unique_name` first; live-verified against real screener pk 42221 (192 real stocks, e.g. FII/FPI decreasing shareholding) that real tickers now extract correctly and store with correct types.
  - **New mandatory rule, "Adding a New Data Source"** (see section above, near "Adding a New Provider"): every fetcher — new or existing — needs a `@pytest.mark.live_datasource` test (skipped by default, opt-in via `RUN_LIVE_DATASOURCE_TESTS=1`, never run in CI) that hits the real endpoint for one real ticker, parses with the fetcher's own function, and validates the DB-stored row is ML-usable (real ticker shape, real finite numbers). Built the reusable pattern: `src/server/tests/conftest.py` (marker registration + skip logic), `live_datasource_helpers.py` (`assert_looks_like_ticker`/`assert_numeric_and_finite`/`assert_non_empty_response`/`assert_stored_row_ml_usable`), and two worked examples (`test_live_datasource_trendlyne_screener.py` — full fetch→parse→DB-write→ML-usability round trip; `test_live_datasource_et_stats.py` — lighter fetch+validate example against a different fetcher family). This is why the corruption above went undetected for so long: nothing had ever hit the real API and checked the shape of what came back. Full suite after all changes: 506 passed, 3 skipped (the live tests), 0 failed.
- **In-place ground-up-rebuild spec pass, Phase 1 (2026-07-24).** User pasted a generic "ground-up rebuild" prompt (verbatim in `docs/NEW_SYSTEM_MASTER_PROMPT.md`, written for a fresh empty repo) and asked to adapt it to this codebase and correct the codebase accordingly. Given the scale, asked clarifying questions first — locked in: in-place migration (not a new repo), out-of-scope items (RAG chatbot, `rl_agent.py`, market-map, 3 of 4 screener providers) left running untouched, screener consolidation deferred to Phase 6, same infra/no paid vendors. Wrote `docs/NEW_SYSTEM_MASTER_PROMPT_INPLACE.md` mapping every spec requirement to this codebase's real files (EXISTS/PARTIAL/MISSING/CONFLICT), then verified the uncertain ones by reading code directly rather than trusting the doc's own "verify" placeholders — this **changed the picture significantly**: quality gate (`quality_gate()`), hard veto (`is_red_flagged()`/`veto_classification()`), position sizing (`bet_size_from_probability()` + inverse-vol + 10%-cap), and the model promotion gate (`promote_or_register()` + timestamped `.pkl` backup) all **already existed** in `unified_ranker.py`/`ml_ensemble.py` — don't re-audit these as gaps in a future pass. Also found an **undocumented pre-existing MCP server** (`src/server/mcpServer.ts`, not wired into any `package.json` script) that already covers much of the spec's "MCP server exposing tRPC" ask, but via 6 direct-DB/direct-exec tools rather than typed tRPC procedures — flag this file to anyone repeating a similar audit so it isn't rediscovered from scratch.
  - **What was actually missing, done this pass** (all additive, no data deleted, no frontend touched, per explicit user constraint): (1) introduced `node-pg-migrate` (`migrations/`, `npm run migrate:up`/`migrate:create`, one no-op baseline marker — not a retrofit of all 126 existing tables) since schema changes previously only went through `pgEnsureColumns()`/a manually-applied `db/schema.postgres.sql`; (2) extended `nse_stocks` (not a new competing table) with `stockid`/`companyid`/`tickertape_sid`/`fincode`/`scripcode` and fixed `scripts/syncAllStockMappings.ts` to route through `dbAsync.ts` instead of importing the raw `better-sqlite3` handle from `db.ts` — **this incidentally fixes a real pre-existing bug**: the script only ever wrote to the SQLite dev-fallback DB regardless of `USE_POSTGRES`, so the existing `mcsymbol`/`tlid`/`tlname` backfill likely never reached production Postgres either; (3) built a shared `src/server/as_of.py` (`as_of_join_sql()`/`read_as_of_history()`) and migrated **≥12 independently hand-written "latest row ≤ date" correlated-subquery joins** across `ml_ensemble.py` (6 sites), `exit_policy.py`, `online_learner.py`, `cs_ranker.py` (2 sites), `confluence_ml_engine.py`, `feature_engineering.py` onto it — 3 of these queries were plain (non-f) triple-quoted strings that needed converting to f-strings first (checked each for stray `{`/`}` before converting, since an f-string mis-conversion silently breaks on any literal brace); added `test_as_of_no_hand_rolled_joins.py` (greps for the pattern outside `as_of.py`, explicitly excluding `fundamentals_snapshot.py` — its own `as_of_date = (SELECT MAX(...))` self-join has no `<=` target-date bound, it's the snapshot writer's own dedup logic, a different pattern, not a missed case); (4) added `engine_coverage_count` to `unified_recommendations`, populated from `unified_ranker.py`'s existing `present = {e for e, m in engine_maps.items() if sym in m}` set that was already computed but never persisted; (5) added `test_breakout_purge_regression.py` — extracted `breakout_classifier.py`'s CV logic into a pure `evaluate_purged_cv(df)` (callable on a synthetic frame, no DB), pinned `RANDOM_STATE=42` in `_make_model()` for determinism, and built a synthetic dataset where a block-level (length=EMBARGO) random ID/label pair can only be predicted via memorization of same-block training rows — proper date+embargo purging keeps validation blocks unseen (AUC ≈0.44, near chance) while a naive row-index-gap purge lets a GBM partially memorize the boundary-adjacent block (AUC ≈0.71); **the first version of this test showed no gap at all** because a monotonic signal→label relationship generalizes across blocks regardless of purge strategy (that only tests "is this feature informative," not "did purging leak") — had to redesign so the feature/label relationship is block-specific and non-generalizable (block ID and block label drawn independently) before the row-vs-date gap actually appeared; (6) fixed `mcpServer.ts`'s `run_analytical_engine` to call `pythonRunner.ts`'s `runPython()` instead of raw `execFile`, so an MCP-triggered engine run now queues behind the same 5-concurrent-Python-process cap as every BullMQ job instead of stacking an uncounted extra process on top of it (found only because auditing the promotion-gate/quality-gate items required reading the files closely enough to notice this pre-existing MCP server) — flagged two caveats rather than silently assuming them away: `runPython()` resolves its Python binary from `PYTHON_PATH` while the tool previously used `PYTHON_BIN` (confirm which is actually set in production before this matters), and queued runs can now wait for a free slot before starting, a real latency change for this tool's callers.
  - **Explicitly not attempted, flagged for a dedicated future pass**: full SQLite-fallback removal (`db.ts`/`sqlTranslate.ts` retirement) and the 3-score-table (`stock_scores`/`quant_scores`/`unified_recommendations`)/6-signal-table consolidation — both already the documented Phase-3 governance target above, and doing either as a drive-by here (rather than a pass with a full consumer inventory) is exactly the kind of shortcut that caused several of the incidents recorded elsewhere in this file. Also not touched: `mcpServer.ts`'s arbitrary-SQL `query_stocks_db` tool (→ typed tRPC tools is real Phase-5 scope) and screener-provider consolidation (Phase 6).
  - **Verification**: this sandbox had no running Postgres/Docker, but Postgres 16 server binaries were installed locally, so spun up a throwaway local cluster (`initdb`/`pg_ctl`, no TimescaleDB extension needed since none of these changes touch hypertables) to prove `node-pg-migrate up` and the two new migrations actually apply cleanly (all 126 tables from `db/schema.postgres.sql` created fine; only the 6 TimescaleDB-specific calls at the end failed, expected, unrelated to this pass), and that `dbAsync.ts`'s `dbGet`/`dbRun` genuinely write to/read from real Postgres (proving the `syncAllStockMappings.ts` fix). Full Python suite: 564 passed, 3 skipped, 0 failed (excluded 2 pre-existing modules needing `torch`/`hmmlearn`, not installed, unrelated to this pass). Full TS suite: 172 passed, 28 failed — all 28 in one pre-existing `mcapiProxy.test.ts` hitting the real MoneyControl API and getting 403s from this sandbox's network, unrelated to any file touched this pass. `tsc --noEmit` clean throughout.
- **Log-review sweep, live prod-server bug fixes (2026-07-26).** User asked to review `pm2` logs (`bharat-server`/`alphaquant-api`/`ml-api`/`chatbot` — all 4 apps share one `logs/pm2-err.log`/`pm2-out.log` via `ecosystem.config.cjs`'s spread `common` config, by design, not a bug) and resolve errors found. Found and fixed 5 real bugs, all live-verified against the running Postgres DB; ruled out several more as transient external flakiness already handled by existing resilience (`mc_broker_reco_fetcher.py`, `insider_transactions_fetcher.py` against NSE — both work fine when re-run live, matches the documented "transient upstream outage must never fail the build" posture) or DB contention under concurrent job load, not code defects (`screener_signal_generator.py` — 4.5s uncontended vs the sporadic 180s timeouts seen in logs).
  - **`scoring_engine.py` `load_data()` crashed 100% of `stock-scoring` runs**: the 2026-07-23 URL-as-symbol corruption fix added `WHERE symbol NOT LIKE '%://%'` to a `pd.read_sql(raw_sql_string, conn)` call — but a raw string passed to a psycopg2-pyformat SQLAlchemy connection treats bare `%` as a parameter-placeholder marker, so the literal `%` in the LIKE pattern broke SQLAlchemy's driver-level param binding (`TypeError: immutabledict is not a sequence`). Every `[QUEUE] stock-scoring failed` in the logs traced to this one line. Fixed by wrapping the query in `sqlalchemy.text(...)` (already imported in the file) — `text()`-compiled statements go through SQLAlchemy's own bind-parameter compiler, which escapes literal `%` correctly for the dialect; a raw string via `exec_driver_sql` does not. The other `NOT LIKE '%://%'` guards added in that same 2026-07-23 fix (`unified_ranker.py`, `intraday_ranker.py`) were unaffected because they go through `self.conn.execute(sql, params)` (the `db_compat`/`ConnWrapper` `?`-placeholder path), not raw `pd.read_sql` on an engine connection — **the lesson: `pd.read_sql(str, conn)` on a raw SQLAlchemy engine connection is a different execution path than `db_compat`'s translate() layer, with different `%`-escaping rules; a fix proven safe on one path is not automatically safe on the other.**
  - **`index_membership_fetcher.py`'s Postgres branch used raw psycopg2 `%s` placeholders instead of `?`** in `backfill_technical_signals()` — identical bug class to the `asm_gsm_fetcher.py` bug fixed 2026-07-23 (raw `%s` bypasses `db_compat.py`'s `translate()`, which expects `?` and converts per-dialect; psycopg2 then throws `syntax error at or near "%"` on the literal `%s` text). The sqlite branch immediately below in the same function already correctly used `?` — this was a same-function, side-by-side inconsistency. Fixed; live-verified (40,044 rows updated). Added `test_index_membership_fetcher.py` (regression test asserting no `%s` survives in either dialect branch) since this fetcher, despite pulling from external NSE CSVs, had zero test coverage before now.
  - **`et-marketstats-sync` was never added to `jobRegistry.ts`'s `JOB_REGISTRY`** when it was onboarded 2026-07-23 (see that session's note) — its Mon–Fri-only cron (`5 18 * * 1-5`) fell through to `jobHeartbeat.ts`'s generic flat 26h staleness check (which exists specifically for jobs *not* in `JOB_REGISTRY`), so every weekend it false-alarmed "STALE: has not succeeded in ~26h" and climbing, hourly, in the digest/logs — exactly the class of bug `JOB_REGISTRY`'s own file-header comment warns about. Registered it (mirroring `etnow-screener-sync`'s entry) so lateness is now judged against its real cron via `getLateJobs()` instead.
  - **`exit_policy.py --train`'s 10-minute job timeout is now structurally insufficient, not just contention-sensitive**: measured 703s (11.7min) *uncontended* on 2026-07-26 training on 144,654 rows in `signal_excursions` (`GradientBoostingRegressor(n_estimators=300)` fit twice per target × 2 targets = 4 fits). This is the identical growing-dataset timeout pattern `exit_labeler.py` (the line directly above it in the same weekly job chain) already hit and was bumped 10min→30min for on 2026-07-19 — mirrored here, bumped to 20min in `queues.ts`. Distinguishing "genuinely too slow now" from "just contended right now" mattered: `screener_signal_generator.py`'s sporadic 180s timeouts (13 occurrences in logs) did NOT reproduce when run standalone (4.5s) — that one is real contention under concurrent Sunday-batch load, left as-is; `exit_policy.py`'s did reproduce standalone, confirming it's a real capacity ceiling, not noise. **Method**: always time the suspect script standalone/uncontended before deciding "contention" vs "genuine slowness" — the same symptom (occasional/consistent timeout in prod logs) has different fixes (nothing to do vs. bump the budget).
  - **`intraday_fetcher.py` was timing out on 100% of runs, all day, every 15 minutes (confirmed: every single cron fire 2026-07-24 09:40–15:40 IST logged `Timed out after 600000ms`)** — meaning intraday CMP data effectively never refreshed on the one trading day in the log window, despite the 2026-07-23 mcsymbol→raw-symbol fix being individually correct. Two compounding root causes, found by live-timing the real full-universe fetch (not assuming from code reading): (1) sequential fetch of ~2,366 symbols — the previous bug's ~97% instant-fail rate had accidentally been masking how slow the *successful*-path per-request latency actually was; (2) the URL template hardcoded `countback=500` regardless of the `--lookback-days` CLI arg, so even the routine 15-min top-up cycle (`--lookback-days 1`) pulled ~20 trading days of bars per symbol every time (1.14M bars written for a 1-day-lookback run). Fixed with **two changes that both mattered independently** — parallelizing the fetch alone (12 workers, thread-local sessions since curl_cffi's `Session` isn't safe shared across threads) cut it to 22.7min, still 2.3× over budget; adding `countback` scaling with `lookback_days` (~25 bars/trading day + buffer, so lookback=1 now requests ~50 instead of 500) on top of that cut it to 4m52s. **A worker-count bump from 12→24 (attempting to go faster) instead silently degraded correctness**: full-universe success rate dropped from 96% (2275/2366) to 51% (1204/2366) with no errors thrown — MC's endpoint tolerates ~12 concurrent (matching `mc_ohlcv_backfill.py`'s already-documented "12-16 safe" ceiling for this exact endpoint) but not 24; small isolated samples at 24 workers looked fine (only genuinely-illiquid names failed), so **this specific failure mode only appears at full-universe scale — a 60-symbol smoke test at a given concurrency level does not validate that concurrency level for a 2,366-symbol run.** Dialing back to 12 workers restored the 96% success rate at 4m52s.
  - **Load-distribution follow-up (same session, user-requested)**: single-sourcing the full universe against MoneyControl every 15 minutes was itself a latent risk (the 12→24-worker experiment above showed MC has a real, non-obvious concurrency ceiling for this endpoint). Added Yahoo Finance's public `/v8/finance/chart/{symbol}.NS?interval=15m&period1=..&period2=..` as a second, independent source — live-verified it needs no crumb/cookie handshake for this endpoint (unlike the batch `/v7/finance/quote` endpoint `liveStockData.ts` already uses, which does), and that MC and Yahoo don't agree on which illiquid names have recent data (MC returned nothing for `ABANSENT` at any `countback`; Yahoo had 157 real bars). `run()` now alternates MC/Yahoo as primary per symbol by index parity and falls back to the other source on a miss, splitting real load ~evenly (live-verified `mc=1140 yahoo=1206` on the full run) while also recovering coverage neither source has alone. Yahoo has no `countback`-equivalent (strictly respects its `period1`/`period2` window), so illiquid-name reach-back is handled by widening the calendar-day window instead (`_yahoo_window()`, capped 60d — Yahoo's practical 15m-interval history limit). Final live full-universe result: 2346/2366 symbols (99.2%, up from 96% MC-only) in 6m28s (well inside the 600s budget, with more headroom than the single-source 12-worker version's 4m52s cost in exchange for materially better coverage and no single point of failure). Added 4 new tests (`TestDualSourceFetch` in `test_intraday_fetcher.py`) covering fallback-in-both-directions, both-sources-empty, and that load genuinely splits across sources — 19/19 pass. Full repo suite after all 5 fixes: 524 passed, 7 skipped (pre-existing exclusions), 0 failed; `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **v4 frontend added: `MarketCommandCenter` + `StockIntelligencePage` (2026-07-23), a 4th dashboard experience layered onto the existing v1/v2/v3 ones, not a replacement.** See the "Frontend Tabs / Navigation" section above for the full v1–v4 breakdown and file locations. Built by reviewing all three existing dashboards plus the real tRPC surface first, to compose from proven pieces rather than duplicate them; wired into the existing `V2AppShell`/router, not a parallel app. `StockIntelligencePage` adds two views that hadn't existed anywhere before: an Overview tab surfacing the unified score + `WhyThisPick` reasoning, and an Ownership & Insider tab; its Technicals tab is also the first stock-detail chart in the app to use real `getOHLCData` instead of `Math.random()`-generated candles (v2/v3's stock-detail charts still fake it). A same-day follow-up QA pass (seeded the local dev DB with sample rows, since the sandbox had no outbound network access) found and fixed 3 real bugs before merge: `Object.entries(factors)` on the Overview tab rendered non-numeric `stock_factor_breakdown` columns (symbol/timeframe/last_updated) as "NaN" progress bars — scoped to the 6 real factor columns; the Fundamentals tab read a `pe_ratio` field that doesn't exist on `stock_fundamentals` (real column is `trailing_pe`) — P/E always showed "—"; `fetchHistoricalOHLC`/`getShareholding` were 100%-live-fetch-only with no DB fallback to `stock_ohlcv`/`technical_signals`' ownership trail — added the same DB-fallback-on-failure pattern the rest of the codebase already uses elsewhere, so an MC/ET outage no longer blanks the chart or Ownership tab. Also expanded v2/v3 sidebar nav to parity with v1 and fixed an `IndexOverview` empty-state bug in the same window.
- **Date-anchor bug silently nulled fundamentals across 6 fetchers (2026-07-25) — same failure class as the 2026-07-22/23 `date('now')` column-type bugs, but the mechanism this time was a wrong reference date, not a wrong SQL cast.** `trendlyne_fundamentals_fetcher.py`/`trendlyne_adv_tech_fetcher.py`/`trendlyne_price_analysis_fetcher.py`/`trendlyne_overview_fetcher.py`, `mf_holdings_fetcher.py`, and `financial_ratios_fetcher.py` (via the shared `et_stats_client.as_of_floor`) all guard their point-in-time UPDATEs with a `date >= floor ELSE NULL` pattern — but anchored `floor` to `date.today()` instead of the last completed trading session. On any day that anchor doesn't exactly match an existing `technical_signals` row (weekends, holidays, or a job racing that day's grid-ensurer), the guard matched **zero** rows while its `ELSE` branch nulled every existing historical row for that stock — silently, no error, every time the job ran on a mismatched day. Fixed by anchoring to the last completed trading session instead (consistent with how `as_of.py`, built one day earlier in the Phase 1 rebuild pass, already handles this class of problem — see below). Also fixed an independent crash in `trendlyne_overview_fetcher.py`: `extract_analyst_data()` unconditionally called `con.cursor()` even when invoked with `con=None` from a worker thread, crashing the whole batch for any stock with recent analyst coverage. Separately wired `mc_tech_rating` into `movement_predictor.py`'s intraday enrichment tier (real correlation with next-day range, ~0.15–0.27 across two windows) and fixed the enrichment harness's `min_child_samples=200` floor, which was structurally blind to any sparse-but-real feature regardless of signal quality. Added `live_datasource` tests for the two previously-untested `trendlyne_price_analysis_fetcher.py`/`trendlyne_adv_tech_fetcher.py`, plus a new unit suite for `movement_predictor.py`. **Recurring lesson across this codebase's incident history: any "as-of"/point-in-time guard that hand-rolls its own reference date (`date.today()`, a hardcoded floor, a same-table self-join) instead of using the shared `as_of.py` helper or an explicit "last completed trading session" lookup is a latent bug waiting for a weekend/holiday cron run to trigger it — grep for `date.today()` near an `ELSE NULL`/`CASE WHEN date >=` pattern in any new or touched fetcher before treating it as safe.**
- **`node-pg-migrate` was declared but never installed, so the 2026-07-24 in-place-rebuild migration adding `unified_recommendations.engine_coverage_count` had never actually reached the live Postgres DB — `unified-ranker` failed on every single run since (2026-07-24), silently, until caught in a 2026-07-26 log review (2026-07-27).** `node-pg-migrate` was added to `package.json` `devDependencies` in that same 2026-07-24 session, and `migrations/1784924167842_unified-recommendations-engine-coverage.sql` was written and (per that session's note) claimed live-verified against a *throwaway local Postgres cluster* — but `npm install` was never re-run against the real dev environment afterward, so `node_modules/node-pg-migrate` didn't exist and `npm run migrate:up` failed with `'node-pg-migrate' is not recognized`. Because `unified_ranker.py`'s `INSERT` explicitly lists `engine_coverage_count`, every run (the 07:30 IST daily cron, per the memory-documented pipeline order, plus any manual retry) crashed on `psycopg2.errors.UndefinedColumn` with **zero rows written to `unified_recommendations` that day** — this is the canonical ranking table (see "Scoring Authority" above), so every downstream reader of it was serving stale data with no error surfaced anywhere except the shared `pm2-err.log`. Fixed by running `npm install` (picked up the already-declared `node-pg-migrate` from `package.json`, 17 packages) then `npm run migrate:up`, which applied all 3 pending migrations cleanly (`baseline-marker`, `nse-stocks-provider-ids`, `unified-recommendations-engine-coverage`). Live-verified: column now present via `information_schema.columns`, and a direct re-run of `python src/server/unified_ranker.py` completed with `{"success": true, "stocks_scored": 3655, ...}` and no errors. **Lesson: a migration tool's own "live-verified" claim from a throwaway/local cluster is not the same as "applied to the real dev/prod DB" — always confirm `npm run migrate:up` (or equivalent) was actually run against the live `POSTGRES_URL` before closing out a migration-adding session, and add a startup/CI check that fails loudly if `pgmigrations` is behind `migrations/` on disk, since nothing currently detects this class of drift except a manual log review.** Also confirmed (not touched, no action needed — both stopped recurring on their own by 2026-07-26/07-27, consistent with already-documented patterns): the `sqlalchemy.cyextension.immutabledict` / `syntax error at or near "%"` pair (both last seen 2026-07-26T07:30, matching the `pd.read_sql` → `text()` fix already documented above) and `[BrokerReco] 0 recos fetched` / BullMQ `Missing lock for job ... moveToFinished` (both last seen 2026-07-25/26, consistent with the already-documented by-design exit-1 behavior and known lock-contention-under-load class respectively).
- **Retroactive backfill (2026-07-27) — 4 real features/findings shipped between 2026-06-27 and 2026-07-19 that were never captured anywhere in this file or memory, found via a full-history commit sweep (721 non-merge commits, automated keyword-overlap triage against the CLAUDE.md+memory corpus, then manual verification of the candidates) done in response to a request to bring documentation up to date beyond just the preceding 5 days. All 4 are still live in the code as of this backfill — this note exists so the coverage gap itself doesn't reproduce, not because anything needed fixing:**
  - `mc_global_macro_fetcher.py` (2026-06-27) — MoneyControl global indices/currencies/ADRs/commodities fetcher, feeding new macro features into `ml_ensemble.py`. Never added to the Python Engines list (now fixed above) or any memory file.
  - `factor_edge.py` (2026-07-16, scheduled into weekly retrain the same day) — a reusable empirical validator answering "does this vendor/derived score actually have forward-return edge, or is it priced-in noise?" (rank IC + cross-sectional AUC per horizon, `--by-regime`, `--quantiles` for decile spread, `--persist` → `factor_edge_history`). **Its first real result is a finding worth knowing, not just a tool**: Trendlyne's momentum score (`m_score`) showed **no forward edge** on a 2-week DVM sample — the high-`m_score` decile *underperformed* by 0.34% over 10 days, and rising `m_score` correlated with essentially flat 5-day moves (Spearman ≈ -0.02); valuation (`v_score`) was faintly positive but on too few dates to trust yet. Currently advisory-only — nothing downstream consumes the verdict to down-weight `m_score` in scoring. Runs weekly against DVM scores across horizons 5/10/21/63 with `--persist`, so `factor_edge_history` should be accumulating enough dates by now to revisit whether `m_score` should be down-weighted or dropped from `scoring_engine.py`'s inputs — worth checking before trusting Trendlyne momentum in any new feature/screener work.
  - `live_screener_ml_ranker.py` (2026-07-18) — a gradient-boosted same-day (intraday) win-probability model trained on the NiftyTrader live-screener filter-match matrix (plus entry-time `change_per`/volume) against actual same-day EOD outcome, with the same promotion-gate discipline as `ml_ensemble.py` (new retrain's held-out AUC must beat the currently-active model's, or it's rejected). Wired into the 15-min live-screener collection cycle (`--score`) and the daily resolver/optimizer pipeline (`--train`), writes to a new `live_screener_ml_scores` table, surfaced in the Live Screener page's "Intraday Edge" tab ranking ML-scored stocks by learned win-probability ahead of the older single-filter rule.
  - `perf(ai): gate LLM signal calls behind quant/surveillance checks, trim prompts, keep Ollama model resident` (2026-07-19) — `processAISignal` now runs the (cheap, DB-only) surveillance and quant `win_probability` gates *before* calling `generateStockAnalysis`, instead of after, skipping the Ollama/Gemini call entirely for stocks that would be dropped anyway regardless of what the LLM says. Also: dropped entry/target/stopLoss from the LLM prompt/schema entirely (`atrBarriers.ts` already overrides these with ATR-grounded math regardless of the LLM's output — asking for them wasted tokens on a task the model has no volatility context for); Ollama calls now use `keep_alive: '5m'` (`OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` env var) instead of `0`, so the model stays resident between calls instead of reloading every single inference (loosened the `ai-signals` worker's concurrency 1→2 and lock duration 20min→10min accordingly, since that throttling existed specifically to survive the old per-call reload cost); added the missing Gemini branch for `analyzeCompanyProfile` (previously silently fell through to Ollama unconditionally when `AI_PROVIDER=gemini`); compacted prompts to non-pretty-printed JSON with 1-2 sentence reasoning caps. **If AI-signal latency or Ollama reload behavior ever regresses, check `OLLAMA_KEEP_ALIVE` and the `ai-signals` worker's concurrency/lock settings in `queues.ts` first** — this is the tuned state, not a default.
- **Round 4 audit extension + all 9 Critical findings fixed (2026-07-30).** Continued the 2026-07-28 `docs/audit-2026-07-28/FULL_STACK_AUDIT.md` audit: a background agent closed the two gaps Round 3 flagged as out of scope (v3/v4 frontend fabrication sweep — 4 new findings #106-109, most notably #106: `getTechnicalPredictions` reads the legacy `technical_analysis_signals` table instead of `technical_signals`, so 5 of 7 tiles on v4's flagship `StockIntelligencePage` — including `win_probability` — silently never render; and a live EXPLAIN ANALYZE pass on the 7 previously-unverified Dimension-5 perf findings, most notably #41: `signal_outcomes` has grown to 237,816 rows and the join the audit originally measured at ~6ms now measures **1,436ms** live). Then fixed all 9 Critical findings from the Priority Action Plan's "Immediate" bucket — full before/after detail and per-finding live-verification numbers are in the audit doc's new "Fix Status" section (bottom of the file), not duplicated here. One-line summary of what changed, by file:
  - `asm_gsm_fetcher.py` — the single worst live-confirmed bug in the whole audit: the ASM/GSM surveillance UPDATE had no `WHERE` clause at all, so on any day `date.today()` didn't match an existing row (e.g. a trading holiday, which this job runs on via `ml-daily-ops`'s closed-day-early-batch dispatcher) it nulled `asm_flag`/`gsm_stage` for **every symbol's entire history** in one run. Live DB showed 47,234 rows, only 2,486 non-null (5.3%) before the fix. Fixed by anchoring to `MAX(date) FROM stock_ohlcv` and adding an explicit `WHERE date >= floor`.
  - `drift_detector.py` — `get_drift_multiplier()` called `query_one(_conn, sql, params)` with 3 args against a 2-arg function signature; every call raised `TypeError`, silently swallowed by the surrounding `except`, permanently defaulting to "no drift" (1.0x, no confidence haircut) since the function was written. Fixed by dropping the erroneous arg; live-verified it now returns a real value (0.85 — critical drift IS currently present and was being silently ignored).
  - `fundamentals_snapshot.py`, `mc_pricefeed_fetcher.py`, `mc_chart_patterns_fetcher.py`, `nt_dashboard_fetcher.py` — 4 more instances of the `date.today()`-anchored `CASE WHEN date >= today THEN val ELSE NULL END` guard bug (same class fixed in 6 files on 2026-07-25, then found recurring 5 more times by this audit). All 4 fixed the same way: anchor to `MAX(date) FROM stock_ohlcv` (the last completed trading session) instead of raw `date.today()`. **This bug class has now recurred 9 times across 3 separate fix sessions (2026-07-19, 2026-07-25, 2026-07-30) — grep for `date.today()` near a `CASE WHEN date >=`/`ELSE NULL` pattern in ANY new or touched fetcher before treating it as safe, this is not hypothetical.**
  - `mf_sector_flow_fetcher.py` — the SELECT feeding the `mf_sector_flow_pct` UPDATE had no date bound at all, so the first time this fetcher ever ran to completion it would have smeared the current month's flow value across every historical `technical_signals` row for every symbol (confirmed live: 0 non-null rows before the fix, i.e. the bug had never actually fired yet). Fixed by bounding the SELECT to `date >= floor`.
  - `earnings_beat_features.py` — `WHERE quarter_date <= as_of` treated the *fiscal period-end* date as "known," when SEBI gives issuers up to 45 days (quarterly) / 60 days (annual) to actually file — a confirmed look-ahead leak into `eps_beat_last_q`/`eps_beat_streak_4q`/`eps_surprise_last_yr`/`eps_estimate_dispersion`. Fixed with a `PERIOD_LAG_DAYS` publication-lag filter (60/90 days, mirroring `et_stats_client.py`'s existing 90-day annual convention) applied in Python after the SQL prefilter (per-dialect date arithmetic in SQL was judged too fragile given this codebase's own documented history of getting that wrong across SQLite/Postgres).
  - `strategy_optimizer.py` — `run()` computed an honest held-out test score, logged a warning when it regressed vs. baseline, then wrote the new weights to `app_settings.optimal_category_weights`/`optimal_source_weights` (read by `scoring_engine.py` at startup, driving every score platform-wide) regardless. Fixed: the write is now skipped entirely on regression. Also gated `compute_screener_overrides()`'s `screener_master.weight_override` batch write on the aggregate train→holdout correlation sign (previously logged, never acted on) — live-verified today's correlation is a healthy 0.809 (886 overrides pass through unaffected).
  - `confluence_ml_engine.py` — two fixes in one file: (1) Finding #16, the CV leakage — the training query had no `ORDER BY`, and CV used `StratifiedKFold(shuffle=True)` on rows carrying 7-day-forward-return labels with heavily overlapping windows across adjacent trading days, a textbook leak. Fixed by sorting by `signal_date` and switching to `TimeSeriesSplit(gap=embargo)` (embargo formula copied from `ml_ensemble.py`'s `_fit_stack`) for the OUTER cv_roc_auc metric only — **do NOT also apply the embargo to `CalibratedClassifierCV`'s internal `cv` param**, that was tried first and crashes (`ValueError: Too many splits... gap=2349`) because each outer fold's training slice is much smaller than the full dataset and can't support the same embargo size nested inside it; `CalibratedClassifierCV(pipeline, cv=5, ...)` (plain int, never the shuffled object) was correct all along and untouched. Live-verified the leak's magnitude directly: the last pre-fix run reported **0.847 AUC**; the first post-fix (purged) run reports **0.733 AUC** — an ~11pt inflation, now closed. (2) Finding #17 — `train()` unconditionally overwrote `confluence_ml.pkl` and set `is_active=1` regardless of AUC; added a champion/challenger gate (`PROMOTION_MARGIN=0.005`, matching `ml_ensemble.py`) that saves a rejected candidate to `.candidate` instead and leaves the live model/registry untouched.
  - `cs_ranker.py` — same Finding #17 pattern: `_register_cs_model()` used to unconditionally deactivate the previous model and activate the new one, even logging "rho below threshold... model saved anyway" and proceeding to activate it. Added the same champion/challenger gate (`CS_PROMOTION_MARGIN=0.01`, matching `live_screener_ml_ranker.py`); `save_cs_model()` is no longer called unconditionally from the CLI before the gate decision (that was the actual live bug — it wrote the file regardless, before the registry check even ran).
  - `online_learner.py` — same Finding #17 pattern but architecturally different: `partial_fit` has already mutated the live SGD state (`state['model']`/`state['scaler']`) by the time `register_update()` runs, so there's no separate "candidate" file to withhold the way the other two engines have — an incremental update genuinely can't be "rejected" without a full snapshot/rollback mechanism (not built here, flagged as a real limitation). What WAS fixed: `register_update()` never deactivated prior `is_active=1` rows before inserting a new one — confirmed live, **54 stale `is_active=1` rows had already accumulated in production** — and a regressed update was indistinguishable from a good one to anything reading `model_registry`. Fixed by deactivating the prior active row every call, and only marking the new row active if `cv_auc` didn't regress beyond `ONLINE_REGRESSION_TOLERANCE=0.02` vs. it (a regression is still logged, just not marked active) — this keeps the registry honest even though it can't undo the underlying partial_fit.
  - **Update**: Finding #17's other 3 instances (`ml_signal_scorer.py`, `dl_engine.py`, `movement_predictor.py`) were fixed in a same-day continuation — see the next session note below, not left open as this paragraph originally said.
  - **Verification discipline**: every fix was live-verified against the real production Postgres DB (read queries to confirm the bug's before-state, then either a direct function call or the real daily-job entry point to confirm the after-state) — not just unit tests. 64 new/updated regression tests added across 10 new/modified test files; full suite (659 tests across `src/server/tests/` + `src/server/__tests__/`) passes with zero regressions from this session's changes.

- **Full-stack audit remediation, final sweep — everything previously left open now closed (2026-07-30, same audit as above).** User asked to fix the remaining items and commit+merge. Closed all 6 remaining backlog items from the audit's Priority Action Plan:
  - **Finding #41** — added `idx_sout_symbol_date` composite index on `signal_outcomes(symbol, signal_date)` via a new `node-pg-migrate` migration; live `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` confirms Postgres now picks `Index Scan using idx_sout_symbol_date` for the `ml.router.ts` join instead of the prior Nested Loop + Memoize fallback (absolute timing on this box was confounded by heavy concurrent job load during the test run — the plan-level fix is confirmed regardless).

Also fixed the same bug class in `online_learner.py`'s own 80/20 val split (Finding #21, not originally in scope but the audit itself flags it as "becomes load-bearing the moment Finding #17 is fixed" — which was already done last session) via a new `_embargoed_train_end()` helper. All four changes live-verified by actually running `ml_ensemble.py --train --dry-run`, `cs_ranker.py --train`, and `online_learner.py --dry-run` against the real DB — no crashes, cs_ranker's held-out rho (0.1075) still clears its promotion threshold post-embargo.
