Every fix in this pass was either live-run against the real production DB or had its real data-source shape independently verified via `curl`/direct DB query before being wired into frontend/backend code — no fix in this pass was accepted on code-reading alone.
- **Fifth full-stack audit pass (2026-07-30): fresh ground-truth inventory + 2 new Critical/High fixes, everything else re-verified clean.** User asked for a comprehensive 6-phase re-audit (inventory, endpoint/bias correctness, strategy design, backtest framework, multi-agent architecture, tooling). Full detail (findings #110-116, Phase 3-6 verdicts) is in `docs/audit-2026-07-28/FULL_STACK_AUDIT.md`'s "Fifth pass" section — summary here:
  - **Real ground truth, corrected estimates this file had been carrying**: ~140 Python files write to the DB (not the ~30-40 this file's task prompts had been assuming), 139 Postgres tables (not ~126, the figure quoted in the 2026-07-11 "Docs refreshed" note above — schema growth via migrations since then, not an error at the time). Neither the "Key File Locations" Python Engines list (a curated subset of ~30 primary engines, not every DB-writing file) nor the table count elsewhere in this file needed correcting — they were describing a narrower, still-accurate thing; only the assumption that those lists were exhaustive was wrong.
  - **Finding #110 (Critical, the 10th recurrence of this repo's worst bug class)**: `index_membership_fetcher.py`'s `backfill_technical_signals()` anchored its `date >= ? ELSE NULL` guard to bare `datetime.now()` — this job runs weekly on **Sunday** (`nse-sync`, a non-trading day), so the anchor matched zero `technical_signals` rows and silently NULLed `is_nifty50`/`is_nifty100`/`is_nifty200`/`is_midcap150`/`is_smallcap250`/`nifty_tier` on every run since the guard was added 2026-07-19. Fixed: anchored to `MAX(date) FROM stock_ohlcv`, matching the fix already applied to 9 other fetchers hit by this exact bug class. Live-verified: non-null `nifty_tier` count corrected from 9,078 (stale residue from past mismatched runs) to 2,490 (exactly today's row count, the correct by-design state). 4 new regression tests.
  - **Finding #111 (High)**: `breakout_classifier.py` — the single model in this platform with genuine validated live edge (purged-OOF AUC ~0.61) — had **no promotion gate at all**, unlike all 7 sibling ML files (all already fixed in earlier passes). `train()` unconditionally overwrote `ml_models/breakout.pkl` on every run. Fixed with `_load_baseline_test_auc()`/`_breakout_promotion_decision()` mirroring `movement_predictor.py`'s exact pattern (`BREAKOUT_PROMOTION_MARGIN=0.005`, rejected candidates go to `.candidate` instead of overwriting). Live-verified against the real production model file (baseline test_auc=0.6656; correctly refuses a worse candidate). 6 new regression tests.
  - **Finding #112 (Medium, same bug class as #110/#111 but a rare-path variant)**: `working_capital_fetcher.py` called `as_of_floor(year_ending)` with no `fallback=` — on unparseable `year_ending`, this silently degrades to bare `date.today()` per `as_of_floor()`'s own docstring warning (sibling `financial_ratios_fetcher.py` already passes `fallback=`). Fixed: threaded a `MAX(date)`-anchored `today` through `process_stock()` → `update_technical_signals()`.
  - **Re-verified clean, not re-fixed**: all previously-fixed CV/embargo mechanisms (`ml_ensemble.py`, `cs_ranker.py`, `online_learner.py`, `confluence_ml_engine.py`, `breakout_classifier.py`'s purge), all 7 promotion gates from earlier passes, `REGIME_WEIGHTS` summing to 1.0, `multi_factor_scorer.py`'s scheduling+wiring, `getTechnicalPredictions`'s table-name fix (Finding #106) — none had regressed.
  - **New informational findings, not fixed this pass**: `chart_patterns`/`institutional_rankings` tables are dead (no writer anywhere, also missing PK/UNIQUE — fix if ever revived); `job-digest` cron has no `JOB_REGISTRY`/`MONITOR_SCRIPTS` entry and never calls `recordHeartbeat` (the monitoring job is itself unmonitored); `company-profiles-sync` runs 7 days/week during market hours unlike every sibling sync; `preopen-snapshot` has only a 5-min buffer before market open; `live_screener_ml_ranker.py`'s `TimeSeriesSplit` has no explicit embargo `gap=` (assessed as likely fine since its label is same-day, not multi-day-forward, but inconsistent with every sibling file's convention).
  - **The single largest open gap, quantified**: only **9 of ~140** DB-writing Python files have a `@pytest.mark.live_datasource` test (~6% coverage) — the exact control this repo adopted specifically because of the 2026-07-23 URL-corruption incident. None of `moneycontrol_fetcher.py`, `mc_pricefeed_fetcher.py`, `fii_dii_fetcher.py`, `pcr_fetcher.py`, or any `nt_*` fetcher have one. Flagged as the top follow-up priority rather than attempted in this pass (writing ~130 live-network tests properly is a multi-session effort on its own).
  - **Phase 3-6 verdicts (no code changes, synthesis only)**: no new model needed — `unified_ranker.py`'s existing regime-weighted blend already implements the multi-source fusion asked for; the real remaining question is whether `factor_edge.py`'s finding (Trendlyne `m_score` has no forward edge) should now drive down-weighting `m_score` in `scoring_engine.py`, once `factor_edge_history` has enough accumulated dates. Backtesting framework verdict: sound, no change needed — its former inputs were the leakage problem, and those are now fixed across five audit passes. Multi-agent architecture recommendation: **do not** move to specialized trading-decision agents — keep quant engines authoritative with one LLM layer narrowly for narrative/explanation (the current design), since a supervisor/multi-agent layer would reintroduce the non-determinism this platform has spent five audit passes removing, for a problem a deterministic weighted blend already solves; the one place LLM judgment has genuine edge is unstructured-text interpretation (e.g. earnings-call free text) with no parser equivalent, and if built, its output must land as one more bounded component score into `unified_ranker.py`, never a free-floating verdict. Tooling verdict: keep Ollama+Gemini as-is (already free); NSE/BSE direct bhavcopy download for *daily incremental* OHLCV (not deep-history) is a worthwhile follow-up to reduce scraping fragility, but out of scope for this pass as a genuinely new fetcher.
  - **Verification discipline**: both Critical/High fixes live-verified directly against the real production DB/model file, not just unit tests; full Python suite re-run after all changes.
- **Sixth pass — data/statistical bias audit + quant-strategy design (2026-07-30/31). No code changed; this pass was measurement, and it invalidates several things earlier passes assumed were fine.** Full report: `docs/audit-2026-07-30/DATA_BIAS_AND_QUANT_STRATEGY_AUDIT.md`; durable summary in the [data_bias_audit_2026_07_30](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/data_bias_audit_2026_07_30.md) memory. All numbers from read-only queries against the live Postgres DB (174 public tables, 161 populated, 13 empty) and backtests on the real `stock_ohlcv`.
  - **THE finding — look-ahead lives inside the feature, where no CV fix can reach it.** `screener_appearances.return_5d/return_20d/outcome_20d` (realized forward returns) → `screener_performance.py` computes `bayesian_score` → used as the **weight** in `screener_features_fetcher.py`'s `screener_momentum_score` → consumed by `ml_ensemble.py:829` and `unified_ranker.py`'s screener block, whose **0.30–0.40 weight is the largest single engine weight in most regimes**. `bayesian_score` is one *current* value per screener (upserted, `last_computed`), not a time series, so a feature row dated May carries knowledge through July. Purged CV / embargo / holdout are all blind to this because the leak is identical in train and test — the five earlier passes' CV fixes were necessary but address a different defect. Measured: `screener_momentum_score` shows **+3.76% net alpha per 5 days** (top-20, next-day-open entry, 0.60% round-trip cost) while a plain `mom21` control **in the same window lost 0.86%** — so it is not a regime artifact. **Decisive test not yet run** (≈1 day): recompute `bayesian_score` point-in-time (only appearances resolved strictly before date D), store as a time series, rebuild the feature, re-run. Until that runs, the ranker's largest weight is unsupported and no screener-derived performance number should be trusted.
  - **The ML feature matrix is 71% empty and 55 days deep.** `technical_signals` has 306 columns; **216 are below 50% coverage and ~150 sit at exactly 0%** (all fundamentals, all ownership, all analyst, all DVM, all six `screener_cat_*`, every banking ratio from the 2026-07-23 harvest, and `news_sentiment_score` at 0.5%). Mechanism verified by tracking coverage across days rather than inferring: each enrichment fetcher UPDATEs only the row matching its own as-of anchor, so a feature is populated **only on the day its fetcher ran** (`roce` 74% on 07-10 and 0% every other day; `dvm_momentum`/`eps_ttm` 75% on 07-24 only; `mc_cagr_3y` 93-96% on 07-20..24 then 0% on 07-27..29). `ml_ensemble.py:1157` then reads them via a lateral `ORDER BY ts2.date DESC LIMIT 1` bounded to a **3-day window**, which always lands on the newest — and emptiest — row. Net effect: ~150 of ~200 selected features are NULL for essentially the whole training set, so the reported ensemble AUC is achieved on technicals alone; and because live inference reads *today's* row (which on a fetcher-run day *does* carry enrichment — `is_nifty50` 100% on 07-30), there is real **train/serve skew**. This is a better explanation of the documented CV-0.75→live-0.50 gap than any CV-splitting issue. Fix = per-column LOCF, or stop denormalising slow data into the daily grid and as-of join each source table at build time (the `as_of.py` pattern already correct for `fundamentals_history`). Also: only **55 distinct dates** exist (2026-05-16→07-30), so nothing using screener/fundamental/ownership/sentiment features can be validated over more than one regime. `feature_store` (390 dates, 86 cols) is healthy but price-derived only.
  - **`stock_ohlcv` voids any mean-based statistic.** 512 bars exceed the ±20% NSE circuit limit, 62 exceed ±100%, 35 exceed ±500%, `close` outside `[low,high]` on 65, 19,669 zero-volume bars — and **RELIANCE 2022-06-16 shows +127,900%** (close 1280 vs a prior bar near 1.0). On the liquid universe the raw mean 5-day return is **6.49% vs a 0.00% median and 0.65% winsorised**. I produced an 850%-annualised phantom edge before catching this and had to discard the first two backtest passes. **Never compute a mean on this table without winsorisation** — `backtester.py` and `performance_tracker.py` currently do, so every historical performance figure this platform has produced is unverified until re-run with bad-bar filtering. Related: `adjustment_basis` is NULL on 2,608,191 of 2,608,478 rows (tagged going forward only), so that mitigation delivers nothing today.
  - **`signal_outcomes` pools two incompatible label definitions with no `signal_source` column.** Read straight off the data: h3/7/14/30 use a fixed **±2% terminal-return** barrier (NEUTRAL band exactly [-2.00,+2.00]); h1/5/15 use **path-based MFE/stop** labels whose NEUTRAL band (~±15%) *overlaps* their WIN (≥+1%) and LOSS (≤−0.78%) regions. Class balance inverts across adjacent horizons (h5 WIN:LOSS 2.5:1, h7 0.62:1) — a definition change, not a market effect. `return_pct` reaches **+27,399%**; `is_plausible_return()` is not applied here. Any model trained across horizons is learning a mixture of two different questions. **RESOLVED 2026-08 — see the "Scoring Authority & Signal Model" section near the top of this file**: the real mechanism was three colliding writers with no source discriminator (a third, `signalOutcomesService.ts`, was found during that fix and wasn't known at the time this finding was written), not a designed per-horizon labeling scheme.
  - **Survivorship bias is structural, not a bug.** 2,436 of 2,450 symbols across 5.5y of history are still trading and exactly **1** stopped >12 months ago — a universe pre-selected on "survived to 2026." Cause: `mc_ohlcv_backfill.py` iterates the *current* `nse_stocks` master. Fix = rebuild the historical universe from **NSE daily bhavcopy archives** (free, official, point-in-time, also supplies official delivery data and a cleaner OHLCV source than scraping). Separately, `unified_recommendations` ranked **3,959 symbols today of which 2,362 are absent from `nse_stocks`**, including a raw numeric ID `13510368` in the symbol column — same defect class as the 2026-07-23 URL-as-symbol corruption; they also have no `stock_ohlcv` history, so backtests silently drop them and the backtested universe ≠ the live recommended universe.
  - **Empirical result that contradicts the platform's design: short-horizon momentum is significantly NEGATIVE.** `mom21` top-20, ≥₹1cr ADVT, next-day-open entry, winsorised, 0.60% round-trip cost: **−0.53% net alpha per 5 days, IR −1.38, t=−3.21 across 272 non-overlapping rebalances, negative in 5 of 6 years**; 12-1 momentum is flat (t=−0.94). Both return tails underperform the universe (lottery/high-vol discount). `REGIME_WEIGHTS`, `relative_strength.py`'s skip-month momentum and the screener weighting all lean into a factor that loses money after costs at this horizon. The supported direction is short-horizon **reversal + low-vol + delivery-quality gate, monthly** (weekly turnover costs ~15%/yr, monthly ~4%). Note `calibrated_win_probability` produced **negative** net alpha even in the contaminated window at 87% turnover.
  - **Architecture verdict (user asked directly about specialist agents + a supervisor): do NOT build it for signal generation.** An LLM in the decision path cannot be replayed over history, so the strategy becomes unfalsifiable — the opposite of five passes spent removing non-determinism; a supervisor combining N opinions is a weighted ensemble with *unknown* weights when `unified_ranker.py` already is one with known, testable, optimisable weights; and this codebase already ran the experiment (the AI/LLM signal path was 74% of signals at a 2.3% win rate and was demoted to explainer-only behind a quant gate). The one place an LLM earns its place is **unstructured text** (earnings calls, MD&A, filings) emitting a *bounded component score stamped with the source document's timestamp*, entering the ranker as one more gated engine — never a free-floating verdict. MCP is tool-access, not accuracy; `src/server/mcpServer.ts` is worth finishing for the **research loop** (it would have surfaced most of this audit months earlier), after its arbitrary-SQL `query_stocks_db` becomes typed procedures.
  - **Free tooling worth adopting** (nothing here is caused by data *availability* — buying a feed fixes zero findings): NSE bhavcopy archives (survivorship + adjustment basis + delivery), NSE/BSE corporate actions (validates the bad-bar quarantine's split filter), AMFI (replaces 60-day-stale `mf_stock_holdings`), SEBI/BSE XBRL (true publication dates, removes the estimated `PERIOD_LAG_DAYS` guess), RBI DBIE (macro regime inputs), GDELT (the `gdelt_sentiment` table already exists and is **empty** while `ml_ensemble.py:1032` COALESCEs it as the historical sentiment fallback — so that fallback resolves to NULL for every training row). Libraries: `quantstats`/`alphalens` (would have made this whole audit a one-liner), `vectorbt` or `bt`, `river`, and **`pandas-market-calendars`** — which fixes the empty `market_holidays` table and attacks the recurring `date.today()`-anchor bug class at its root. Already optimal: LightGBM/CatBoost, Optuna, FinBERT, Ollama, Postgres/Timescale, BullMQ.
  - **Other integrity defects found:** `insider_trades.date` is TEXT holding `"22 May, 2026"` — **44,981 of 44,985 rows non-ISO**, so every `date >=` comparison on it is a lexicographic string compare (this is why `insider_buy_pct_90d` has 0.2% coverage); `market_holidays` is empty; news is 26% symbol-unlinked, 39% AI-scored, skewed **2.6:1 bullish** (must be cross-sectionally demeaned before use) and stored as a JSON blob with no indexed symbol column, which is the practical reason it never reached the feature matrix.
  - **Remediation shipped + a MAJOR self-correction (2026-07-31).** All Phase-1/Phase-2 items from the report are implemented and live-verified; full suite **796 passed, 51 skipped, 0 failed** (36 new regression tests in `test_data_integrity_repair.py` + `test_screener_pit_scores.py`). Fix-status table with per-item verification numbers is in the audit doc's new §8b.
    - **THE CORRECTION — the `bayesian_score` look-ahead is real but explains ~none of the alpha, and my attribution above was wrong.** I built the PIT fix, backfilled it, and ran the decisive test the report demanded. Result: **corr(full-sample weight, PIT weight) = 0.9946**, and rebuilding the feature both ways over the same window gives −0.94% (full-sample) vs −1.12% (PIT) net alpha. The weights barely move. I had traced a leak path of the right *shape* and attributed an anomaly of the right *size* to it **without measuring the link**. **Standing lesson: a mechanism that could explain an anomaly is a hypothesis, not a finding, until the counterfactual is actually run.**
    - **What the test found instead is worse.** Rebuilding `screener_momentum_score` with the *production* `compute_features()` and comparing against the stored `technical_signals` values for 2026-07-20: **corr 0.672, exact match 2.1%, stored mean 5.33 vs rebuilt 1.76 (~3× larger)** — while the *stored* feature yields +4.11% net alpha in the same window the *rebuilt* one yields −0.94%. **The platform's highest-weighted input cannot be reproduced from its own source data using its own formula**, because `screener_appearances` is mutated in place (`exited_date` backfilled, rows replaced) so the state that produced any historical value is gone. Its alpha is unattributable and it cannot be recomputed for a backtest — no screener-derived backtest number means anything until that table is made append-only/bitemporal (`(screener_id, symbol, appeared_date, observed_at)` with `exited_date` as a view). **Not done — needs a schema-design decision.**
    - **Shipped:** (1) PIT screener scores — new `screener_performance_history` + `phase_f_pit()`/`compute_pit_scores()`/`--backfill-pit`, `load_screener_meta(con, as_of)` reads the newest snapshot ≤ as_of (8,239 rows / 6 dates). (2) **`densify_feature_matrix.py`** — forward-fills sparse enrichment columns across the grid, strictly forward, per symbol, capped at 120d, never backward, recording `enrichment_ffill_age_days`; model outputs and price columns are in `NEVER_FILL` so a stale prediction is never fabricated. **+628,100 non-null cells; `roce` 0%→73.3%, `dvm_momentum` 0%→75.5%, `eps_ttm` 0%→75.4%, `mf_flow_rank` 1.9%→45.3%.** (3) Feature-join window 3d→7d in `ml_ensemble.py` (both dialect branches) + `backfill_technical_features.py` — 3 days cannot span a holiday+weekend closure and returned *no* row at all. (4) **Bad-bar quarantine** — `stock_ohlcv.is_suspect`/`suspect_reason`, **425 bars flagged** (RELIANCE 2022-06-16 correctly `impossible_move`); `backtester.py`/`performance_tracker.py` now filter on it. (5) **Universe control** — `unified_ranker._restrict_to_tradeable_universe()` dropped **2,310** symbols incl. `13510368`/`1STCUS`; today's table went 3,959 rows/2,362 not-in-master → **1,566 rows / 0 not-in-master**. (6) `signal_outcomes.label_definition` (`terminal_pct2` 115,163 / `path_barrier` 130,297) + `is_suspect` — max \|return\| among clean rows now **100.0%**, was 27,399%. (7) `adjustment_basis` DEFAULT pinned + `ohlcv_adjustment_basis()` resolver. (8) `insider_trades.date_iso` — 44,985 converted, 0 unparseable. (9) `market_holidays` — 75 rows. (10) `news_symbol_link` — 25,936 rows / 2,097 symbols, master-filtered. (11) `densify-feature-matrix` + `data_integrity_repair --bad-bars` wired into `ml-daily-ops` after enrichment / before training, registered **critical** in `JOB_REGISTRY`.
    - **Two implementation gotchas worth remembering:** (a) `stock_ohlcv` is a **compressed TimescaleDB hypertable** (21 of 24 chunks) — a predicate-wide `UPDATE` makes Timescale decompress everything and dies with `tuple decompression limit exceeded` (2.2M tuples). Fix: `SET timescaledb.max_tuples_decompressed_per_dml_transaction = 0` and update by explicit `(symbol, date)` key in small batches. This is also why `adjustment_basis` was **deliberately not** backfilled — decompressing 2.6M rows to write a metadata tag permanently costs the compression; the DEFAULT+resolver approach gets the same benefit for free. (b) `stock_ohlcv.date` is a **native DATE** column while most date columns here are TEXT — `WHERE date >= (CURRENT_DATE - 30)::text` throws `operator does not exist: date >= text` (hit this in the universe filter), *and* the failed statement then poisons the transaction so every later query dies with `InFailedSqlTransaction`. Always `rollback()` in the except branch of an optional query.
    - **Deliberately not done:** survivorship (needs the NSE bhavcopy fetcher + its mandatory `live_datasource` test — real work, top Phase-2 item); `screener_appearances` bitemporality (above); Finding #31. Also note `mc_cagr_3y`/`nim` remain 0% and densify correctly does **not** fix them — they have no non-null value anywhere in the 55-day window to carry forward, which is a fetcher-cadence problem, not a join problem, and is the next thing to chase.
  - **Round 2 (2026-07-31): the previously-open items closed.** Suite **814 passed, 63 skipped, 0 failed**. Full detail in the audit doc's §8c.
    - **Survivorship: fetcher built at source — but the magnitude I first reported was wrong twice, and the corrected figure is ~7.6%, not 40%.** Probed NSE's archives live rather than assuming: `sec_bhavdata_full_DDMMYYYY.csv` works **2021-01-04 → today** with a consistent schema (the UDiFF `BhavCopy_NSE_CM_*` files only reach back to 2024-04 and the legacy `cm{DD}{MON}{YYYY}bhav.csv.zip` is 404/discontinued — don't waste time on either). New **`nse_bhavcopy_fetcher.py`** → `nse_universe_history`, the exchange's own record of what actually traded each day, plus official `DELIV_QTY`/`DELIV_PER` for free. Full daily backfill complete: **1,442 trading days, 3,389,444 rows, 4,033 symbols, 2021-01-01→2026-07-30.**
      **CORRECTION — decompose the raw gap before quoting it.** I twice reported "37.6%/40% of the traded universe is missing, systematically the ones that died". 1,622 of 4,033 symbols (40.2%) are indeed absent from `stock_ohlcv`, but the breakdown is: **1,199 SME series** (a different board) · **384 still-trading ETFs** (GOLDBEES, BANKBEES, CPSEETF — not equities) · **234 rights entitlements** (`-RE*`, expire by design, never "died") · **367 genuinely stopped**, of which only **~306 are real mainboard companies** — and even that includes ticker changes/demergers (`TATAMOTORS` is in the list purely because of the 2025 demerger). Genuine casualties: MEGASOFT, INFIBEAM, SEQUENT, JCHAC, CREATIVE, SMSLIFE, MANGCHEFER, DHANI. **So the honest number is ~306 companies / 7.6% of the universe** — still a real bias (a backtest that never sees ~300 multi-year companies that then stopped is measurably optimistic, which justifies the fetcher) but roughly a fifth of the claimed size. The decomposition took one query and should have preceded the claim.
      Wired into `ml-daily-ops` before the quality/feature steps, registered **critical** in `JOB_REGISTRY` (a missed day loses that day's delisted names permanently), with the mandatory `live_datasource` test (12 tests against the real endpoint: fetch → own parser → own writer → ML-usability, plus idempotent upsert, equity-series filtering, and weekend-404-is-not-an-error) and 18 offline parser tests. `backtester.survivorship_gap()` reports the gap on every run. **Deliberately NOT unioned into `stock_ohlcv` pricing**: bhavcopy is RAW/unadjusted while `stock_ohlcv` is split-adjusted, so merging without a split-adjustment pass would reintroduce the mixed-adjustment-basis seam this same audit flagged and fake a gap-down on every split.
    - **`screener_appearances` reproducibility closed going forward — after two more refuted hypotheses.** *"`INSERT OR IGNORE` collapses repeat episodes"* is **wrong** (the PK is already `(screener_id, symbol, appeared_date)`; 565,786 rows vs 308,253 distinct pairs, so re-entries do create rows). *"the 3-day window double-counts continuously-present symbols"* is **wrong** (42,629 rows vs 42,280 distinct pairs in-window = 0.8% duplication, nowhere near the 3× gap). Rather than invent a third mechanism, `screener_features_fetcher.py` now writes **`screener_membership_snapshot`** — the exact `(as_of_date, symbol, screener_id)` set used, written immutably *before* features are computed from it — so reproducibility is true by construction regardless of how `screener_appearances` mutates. Live: 22,131 memberships snapshotted, **1,375/1,632 screeners on PIT weights**. Pre-2026-07-31 values stay permanently unverifiable.
    - **`nim` was never a bug — it was cadence.** `financial_ratios_fetcher.py` is gated to the **first Sunday of the month**; last full-universe run **2026-07-11**, banking harvest shipped **2026-07-23**, so 1,969 of 1,978 rows simply predate the feature. Live-probed the API *first* to rule out a parsing failure (HDFCBANK `nim=2.94`, ICICIBANK `3.71`, SBIN `2.27`, KOTAKBANK `3.83`, AXISBANK `2.97`, RELIANCE `NonBank/None` — all correct), then triggered a full run; banking ratios now landing at ≈ the expected bank share. **Standing lesson: adding a column to a monthly fetcher means up to a month of total absence, and `densify_feature_matrix.py` cannot help because there is no prior value to carry forward.** `mc_cagr_3y` is the analogous case and is working as designed (current-day-only write via the `MAX(date)` anchor; forward-fill can't reach backwards, so history stays empty but every future day carries).
    - **A conflict caught before it shipped:** `data_integrity_repair.py --bad-bars` was originally scheduled in `ml-daily-ops` and would have been **silently wiped** — `ohlcv_quality.py` runs earlier in the same chain and does `UPDATE stock_ohlcv SET is_suspect=0` at the top of every run. The extra checks were merged into a new **`ohlcv_quality.flag_malformed_bars()`** (the file that owns the flag and its reset) and the queue step removed. Live-verified through a full reset+reflag cycle: 297 flagged, RELIANCE 2022-06-16 still caught, **0 malformed bars left unflagged**. Deliberately did NOT push the stricter >25%-move threshold into the daily path — `ohlcv_quality` already has a corporate-action-aware threshold someone calibrated; `data_integrity_repair --bad-bars` stays a manual sweep. **If you ever add a second `is_suspect` writer, it must go inside `ohlcv_quality.py` or run after it.**

(2) `screener_momentum_score` being a backfill *smear* of today's membership onto history — **refuted**: cross-sectional rank autocorrelation decays 0.645 (lag 1) → 0.522 (lag 5), within-symbol std 5.46 vs cross-sectional 15.0, only 0.3% of symbols constant, and `screener_streak_days` advances by exactly 1 day-over-day for most rows. - **Log/job/schedule review sweep — 4 production bugs fixed, all live-verified (2026-07-31).** User asked to review error logs, find why jobs run late, and audit job frequency/timing/bulk-fetch opportunities. Full detail in the [job_schedule_and_log_review_2026_07_31](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/job_schedule_and_log_review_2026_07_31.md) memory; durable summary:
  - **THE finding — a 60-second timeout was failing the platform's main daily ML chain on half of all runs.** `ml-daily-ops` showed `fail_count=32 / run_count=66` with `last_error='1 steps failed: drift-detector'`, and its parent heartbeat was 2 days stale while **every one of its sibling sub-steps had succeeded** (all 12 stamped 07-30 23:54). Cause: `drift_detector.py` measures **57.2s uncontended** against a `60_000` budget — a 2.8-second margin, so it only ever passed on a fully idle box. It is a `T.run()` step (not one of the tolerated `.catch()` steps), so every timeout-kill failed the whole parent. The kill message doesn't match the deliberate `exit code 1` = EMERGENCY_RETRAIN branch, so it correctly rethrew — the defect was purely the budget. Raised to 5 min. **Two more found by the same measure-don't-assume pass**: `ml_calibration.py` **119.0s measured / 120_000 budget** (a *one-second* margin) → 10 min; `rl_agent.py --backfill --lookback 5` **220s / 300_000** (27% headroom, eaten by the 19:30–00:00 contention window) → 15 min. **Standing lesson: this repo now has five separate instances of a Python step whose timeout was set by guess and then sat just under the real runtime (exit_labeler, exit_policy, drift_detector, ml_calibration, rl_agent). Always `time` the script standalone before setting or trusting a budget — and treat <2× measured runtime as under-budgeted, not "fine".**
  - **`nse_ipo_calendar_fetcher.py` had never run once**: `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'nse'`. The package **was already declared in `requirements.txt`** — it was simply never `pip install`ed into `backend-python/venv` (the interpreter `PYTHON_PATH`/`runPython` actually uses) when the fetcher shipped 2026-07-30. Identical class to the 2026-07-24→27 `node-pg-migrate` incident: *declaring* a dependency is not *installing* it, and nothing in this stack fails loudly when the two drift. Installed (`nse-3.2.1`); live-verified — 48 rows (3 current / 5 upcoming / 40 past) with real tickers, company names and price bands.
  - **`eps_surprise_fetcher.py` Pass 2 called an endpoint that does not exist.** 132 `HTTP Error 422` lines per run and a 600s timeout-kill on every run. Live-probed MC's `actual-estimate` endpoint: `scId`/`sc_id`/`scid`/`id`/`symbol` are **all** rejected with `{"success":0,"data":"\"scId\" is not allowed"}`, and `type` only accepts `[all, std, con]` — it is **list-only**, there is no per-stock variant to fix the query string to. Replaced the ~500-call network pass with a read of `eps_surprise_history`, which the *bulk* pass already upserts one row per (scid, results-date) into on every run. **Result: 600s timeout-kill → 2.5s, and 183 symbols now get `eps_surprise_q2`/`eps_beat_streak` that previously got nothing at all** (live-verified: q2 non-null went 0 → 182 on the latest `technical_signals` date, 89 stocks with streak > 1). Note `eps_surprise_history.quarter` is a **results-announcement date** ('May 30, 2026'), not a quarter label — it does NOT sort correctly as text ('Jul' < 'May' lexicographically), so `_quarter_sort_key()` parses it; a text sort would silently swap q1/q2.
  - **`insider_transactions_fetcher.py` never got past the letter "E".** 27 timeout-kills at the 30-min budget. The tell was in the failure distribution, not the message: **all 160 failures in one run were symbols starting with E (96), F (37), G (27)** — consecutive alphabetically, zero successes after. The NSE session is warmed **once** at startup and never rebuilt; NSE expires those cookies partway through a ~2,400-symbol sequential walk, so the run died around "E" and **H–Z was never fetched on any run**. Measured per-symbol latency is fine (0.42s → 16.9 min projected for the full universe, inside budget) — it was never a speed problem. Fixed: `fetch_nse_insider()` now returns **`None` on failure vs `[]` on genuinely-no-filings** (previously both returned `[]`, so a throttled run was indistinguishable from a universe with no insider activity), the loop rebuilds the NSE session after 5 consecutive failures (capped at 12 rebuilds with backoff), and the summary line now prints failed-symbol and rebuild counts instead of an aggregate-only row count. Same silent-partial-failure class as the 2026-07-23 `intraday_fetcher` mcsymbol bug.
  - **Bulk-fetch probe — NSE's index-wide insider endpoint was tested and REJECTED, do not "optimise" to it.** `corporates-pit?index=equities&from_date=..&to_date=..` looks like an ideal bulk replacement (one call returned 4,458 rows / 579 symbols) but it is **silently stale**: it returns *nothing at all* for any window after early May 2026 (`Jul only`, `Jun→Jul`, `last14d`, `last30d` all → 0 rows) while the per-symbol endpoint happily returns July data for the same stocks. `to_date` is effectively ignored. Swapping to it would have quietly replaced live insider data with a 3-month-old snapshot — exactly the silent-wrong-data class this codebase keeps getting burned by. Kept per-symbol.
  - **Scheduling findings (analysed, deliberately NOT changed — reschedules need the pipeline dependency order confirmed first).** 8 heavy jobs fire inside a 95-minute window (22:00 quant-eod, 22:30 stock-scoring + dl-infer, 23:00 mc-sync + quant-scoring + confluence-outcomes, 23:30 etnow-sync, 23:35 et-marketstats), and `ml-daily-ops` starts 19:30 and runs ~4.4h so its tail overlaps all of them. **All 43 pg-pool `timeout exceeded when trying to connect` errors fall in that window** (29 in the 21:00 hour alone). The pool itself is healthy (14 of 60 connections, no leak), so raising `PG_POOL_MAX` would mask the pile-up rather than fix it — the fix is destaggering. Also flagged: `ml-daily-ops` (19:30) scores *before* the screener syncs that enrich its inputs (23:00–23:35), so it consumes a day-old enrichment; `refresh-all-daily` persists the daily OHLCV bar at **16:00 IST**, before NSE's official bhavcopy publishes (~18:00–19:00), while `nse-bhavcopy-fetcher` correctly runs at 19:30 inside ml-daily-ops; `dl-feature-daily` runs at **15:30 IST**, exactly at the closing bell; `outcome-resolver` runs 09:30 IST, *after* the next session opens and *after* `unified-ranker` (07:30) has already consumed the prior day's outcomes.
  - **Frequency findings.** `sync-company-profiles` runs **21:00 IST all 7 days** for data that barely changes (376 rows, last touched 07-26) — the only sync running on weekends. `insider_transactions_fetcher` re-fetches a **90-day window for all 2,366 ACTIVE symbols every single day** to capture ~2–52 new filings/day universe-wide, and **only 951 of 2,366 symbols have EVER had a filing** — so ~60% of every run's requests are guaranteed-empty by construction.

Full Python suite **827 passed, 65 skipped, 0 failed**; `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **Telegram stock recommendations restored + a NaN-poisoning bug in the canonical ranking table (2026-07-31).** User reported no longer receiving stock recommendations on Telegram, and asked that recommendations state their horizon (intraday vs long term), the reason for selection, and the screeners the stock appears in. Full durable detail in the [telegram_recs_and_nan_scores_2026_07_31](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/telegram_recs_and_nan_scores_2026_07_31.md) memory. Two independent silent failures, both live for weeks:
  - **Why the alerts stopped.** The only stock-rec Telegram path is `websocketService.broadcastNewSignal`, gated on `signalType === 'BUY' && confidence >= 85`. Commit `0609a49` (2026-07-12, "demote LLM from signal generator to explainer") replaced the LLM's self-reported confidence (85-98) with the quant `win_probability × 100` — **max `win_probability` across 2,187 stocks is 0.406**, so the gate is mathematically unreachable. Last alert fired **2026-07-18** (verified against `unified_signals`: 678 rows ever at conf ≥85, none after that date). The second broadcast site (`technicalSignalsService.ts:1478`) never sent either — it passes no `signal` object, so `alert.signal && …` is always false. **Deliberately did NOT lower the threshold** — that restores the ~1000-BUY/day firehose this repo removed on purpose; the gate is left in place with a comment explaining it is dead and pointing at the digest.
  - **THE finding — NaN scores were being minted into fake 'Buy' recommendations.** `ml_models/lstm_v3.pt` … `lstm_v18.pt` **all carry 100% NaN weights** (v2, 2026-06-16, is the last clean one), so every `deep_learning_predictions.prob_up_5d` row was NaN. `unified_ranker._get_dl_scores()` used `float(x or 0)` — **which does not sanitise NaN, because NaN is truthy** — so `dl_score` was NaN and propagated through `_blend` into `unified_score`. It stayed silent because **every comparison against NaN is False**: `if unified < 1: continue` doesn't skip it, `_classify`'s `score >= 66.0` degrades it to a plain `'Buy'`, and `_conviction`'s tier checks all fail to `'D_MARGINAL'`. Net effect: **1563 of 1566 rows** in `unified_recommendations` (the canonical ranking table, per "Scoring Authority" above) were NaN-scored on 2026-07-31 and all 449 "Buy" rows that day were meaningless; historically ~42% of rows (≈1,650 of 3,950/day) for weeks. Fixed with `_finite_engine_map()` (drops non-finite scores at the engine-map boundary so the engine is treated as *absent* and `_blend` renormalises, rather than poisoning every symbol it touches), an explicit `math.isfinite(unified)` guard, `dl_engine.py` refusing to persist non-finite predictions (exits 1 with a loud reason), and `train_lstm()` refusing to save a checkpoint whose weights contain non-finite values. Live-verified: 0 NaN, 427 real Buys, conviction spread S_ELITE 2 / A_HIGH 22 / B_MEDIUM 331 / C_LOW 845 / D_MARGINAL 357 (was uniformly D_MARGINAL).
  - **Two generalisable lessons.** (1) **A metric-based promotion gate cannot catch weight divergence** — `_promote_lstm_version` does correctly refuse a NaN `roc_auc`, but all-NaN weights make `walk_forward_validate` *raise*, which the surrounding handler deliberately swallows as non-fatal; the gate must check the weights themselves, which it now does. This applies to every model file in the repo, not just the LSTM. (2) **`float(x or 0)` is not a NaN guard** — grep for that idiom on any model-output column.
  - **Stale-row bug found while verifying.** `unified_recommendations`' upsert key is `(symbol, computed_at)`, so a symbol scored by an earlier run *that day* but dropped by a later one (universe filter, RL gate, the new non-finite guard) keeps its stale row and is still ranked by every consumer — re-running after the fix left 18 NaN rows behind. Added a purge of rows the run did not produce. Same class as the `intraday_outcome_resolver` stale-row issue fixed earlier.
  - **New delivery path**: `src/server/telegramRecommendations.ts` builds a digest from the two canonical tables — `unified_recommendations` (swing/positional) and `intraday_recommendations` (intraday) — with per-pick horizon label, entry/SL/target/R:R, `trade_reasoning` as the selection rationale, and the screener names from `screener_names_json.bull_screeners` (capped at 4 with a "+N more" overflow count). Wired as BullMQ queue `recommendations-digest` at `45 2 * * 1-5` (08:15 IST, 45 min after unified-ranker's 07:30 and before the 09:15 open), registered **critical** in `JOB_REGISTRY` with `recordHeartbeat` (its predecessor went silent for two weeks precisely because nothing watched it), plus `telegram.sendRecommendationsDigest` / `previewRecommendationsDigest` admin procedures. **The intraday emission gate is respected structurally**: `intraday_ranker.py` already downgrades every Buy to Hold while gated, so filtering on Buy inherits the gate rather than re-deriving it — and when gated the digest says so explicitly instead of showing an unexplained empty section. The unified ranker's own `timeframe='INTRADAY'` rows (24 today, incl. an S_ELITE) are a *different* engine and were being dropped by both queries; they now fill the intraday section with an explicit provenance note so the two engines are never conflated. **Gotchas worth remembering**: `unified_ranker._classify` emits title-case `'Strong Buy'`/`'Buy'`, NOT the SCREAMING_CASE the table name suggests — querying `IN ('BUY','STRONG_BUY')` silently returns nothing (I hit this); and Telegram's legacy Markdown parser rejects the *entire* message on an unbalanced `_`/`*`, which screener names contain, so all dynamic text is stripped of `_*`[]` before rendering.
  - **Verification**: every fix live-run against the real production Postgres (re-ran `unified_ranker.py` end-to-end; ran `dl_engine.py --mode infer` and confirmed it now drops 2,421 non-finite predictions, writes 0, and exits 1 instead of silently writing NaN), and **two real Telegram messages were actually delivered** (10 picks across both horizons, 2 chunks). 13 new Python tests (`test_unified_ranker_nonfinite.py`, **negative-controlled** — reverting the sanitiser to a passthrough fails 5 targeted assertions) + 22 new TS tests (`telegramRecommendations.test.ts`). Full suites: **955 Python passed / 69 skipped / 0 failed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, job-registry/pipeline-ordering suites green (the new cron `45 2 * * 1-5` collides with nothing).
  - **Still open, deliberately not attempted**: the **LSTM needs retraining** — `dl-engine-infer` now exits 1 daily until then, which is intended (it surfaces a genuinely broken model) but *will* show as a failing job; `dl` is simply absent from the blend meanwhile, which `_blend` handles correctly. The config still points at the NaN `lstm_v18`; rolling back to the last clean `lstm_v2` was **not** done (6 weeks stale and unvalidated — a modelling decision, not a bug fix). ~2,421 historical all-NaN rows remain in `deep_learning_predictions`, harmless now that the ranker sanitises. **`pm2 restart bharat-server` is still required** to register the new queue (`.ts` is not hot-reloaded) — not done in-session because `ml-daily-ops` was mid-run.

  - **THE ordering bug: every consumer of the screener tables was scheduled BEFORE the syncs that populate them.** `screener_features_fetcher.py` runs *inside* ml-daily-ops (19:30 IST) and builds `screener_momentum_score` — per the 2026-07-30 data-bias audit, the largest single engine weight in most `REGIME_WEIGHTS` regimes — off `screener_appearances`. But mc/etnow/et-marketstats only synced at 23:00/23:30/23:35. So that feature, and every score derived from it, was **a full day stale every single day**, silently, with nothing erroring. Fixed by moving the three syncs to **18:00 / 18:20 / 18:40 IST**, ahead of both ml-daily-ops (19:30) and stock-scoring (22:30).

Lesson: check whether a job self-satisfies its own dependency before concluding a cron order is wrong.

It runs 10 sequential Python steps (~145 min of budget). Pinned to `'0 21 * * 1-5'` = **02:30 IST**, in the empty post-ml-daily-ops window. Deliberately **NOT** moved ahead of ml-daily-ops: it writes `screener_performance_history`, which `screener_features_fetcher.py` reads **as-of**, so a lagged snapshot is the correct point-in-time input there and making it same-day would reintroduce look-ahead.

Every DL feature set was built without the current session in it. Moved to **17:00 IST**.

  - **Every `cronPattern` mirror updated in lockstep** (`jobRegistry.ts` for 6 jobs, `monitorScripts.ts` `cronPatterns` for dl-engine-infer and screener-performance) — a stale mirror produces phantom "late" alerts against a deadline the job never had, which is the failure mode `jobRegistry.ts`'s own header warns about.

The collision check parses **queues.ts source rather than `JOB_REGISTRY`**, because several real repeatables (`ohlcv-gap-fill-daily`, `dl-inference`, `screener-performance`) are monitored via `MONITOR_SCRIPTS` and have no registry entry — a registry-only check was blind to exactly the 16:15 pair it needed to catch. **Negative-controlled**: reinstating the old `'5 18 * * 1-5'` et-marketstats cron makes 3 targeted assertions fail.
  - **Verification**: `tsc --noEmit` clean; Python **838 passed, 65 skipped, 0 failed**; job/monitor vitest suites 39 + 9 passed. Schedule re-derived programmatically from source afterward and confirmed 0 real collisions across the full 24h.

- **Intraday pipeline / strategy / screener audit (2026-07-31) — measurement pass, no code changed.** Full report `docs/audit-2026-07-31/INTRADAY_PIPELINE_AND_STRATEGY_AUDIT.md`; durable summary in the [intraday_audit_2026_07_31](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/intraday_audit_2026_07_31.md) memory. All figures from read-only queries against live Postgres plus a purpose-built 15-min-bar backtest harness.
  - **THE finding — the intraday engine trades the wrong side of the effect.** Cross-sectional Spearman IC against the *remaining session*, 23 days, all liquidity tiers, 3 decision times: **every momentum/breakout feature is negative** (`vwap_dev` −0.090 t=−5.7, `or_pos` −0.086, `above_or` −0.061, `rvol` −0.054 t=−7.4) and only `below_or` is positive (+0.059 t=+6.0). Intraday is strongly mean-reverting — the same direction as the 2026-07-30 audit's daily-horizon momentum finding, one horizon down. `intraday_ranker.py` blends `W_BREAKOUT=0.45` + `W_SCREENER=0.55`, both momentum-flavoured. Re-graded honestly (entry at the next 15-min bar open after the signal's own `cycle_at`, exits only on post-entry bars) its Buy/Strong-Buy signals return **−0.099% gross vs a −0.003% universe over the same window, t=−6.55** — genuine negative alpha, not just noise. `intraday_score` quintiles are flat (−0.098, −0.087, −0.187, −0.174, +0.052) and conviction tiers are unordered (`S_ELITE` mean −0.037%, median −0.338%). It also emits **~1,430 rows/day, one per symbol** — a scored universe, not a selection.
  - **`intraday_outcome_resolver.py` is measuring the wrong thing.** It grades intraday signals against the **daily** `stock_ohlcv` bar, whose `open`/`high`/`low` span 09:15→close and therefore include price action from *before* the signal existed. That fabricates gap exits that were never trades — live: **122 `STOP_GAP` rows averaging −9.54%** and 42 `TARGET_GAP` averaging +5.21% — and lets targets/stops be "hit" pre-entry. Every downstream number inherits it (reported win rate, `intraday_strategy_lifts`, accuracy metrics). **The fix needs no new data**: `intraday_ohlcv` already holds the 15-min bars; walk bars strictly after `cycle_at`.
  - **No net-positive intraday strategy exists on this data.** Swept **256 configurations** (8 signal families × 4 decision times × 4 exit rules × 2 basket sizes) over the liquid universe (ADTV20 ≥ ₹5cr) with a cost sweep of 0.10/0.15/0.25/0.50% round-trip: **best net return at a generous 15bps is −0.004%**. The mean-reversion edge is statistically robust (quintile spread +0.20 to +0.30%, t=4–6) but its harvestable magnitude (~0.08–0.22% gross) sits below the cost floor; alpha peaks at **+0.12%** (10:15 entry, 1-hour hold) and decays by the close. India intraday explicit costs are ~0.085% (brokerage + sell-side STT + exchange + GST + stamp), so 0.15% is a fair floor *including* slippage — the conclusion holds at every cost tested.
  - **A short-side result that looked great and was a trap — always check shortability before believing one.** Fading intraday strength (short highest `vwap_dev`, N=10, T=10:15) gave **+0.838%/day gross, beta-neutral alpha +0.739%, t=2.88, 74% of days positive**. Restricted to the F&O universe — the realistic intraday-shortable set, since broker MIS short lists are essentially F&O plus select large caps — it collapses to **+0.017%/day, alpha −0.031%, t=−0.27**. The entire edge lived in the ~950 non-F&O names, which are both unshortable *and* exactly where bid-ask bounce on a thin book manufactures apparent reversal when returns are measured bar-close to bar-close. Excluding ASM/GSM changes nothing further.
  - **`intraday_ohlcv.vwap` is 100% NULL, so `vwap_deviation_pct` is a constant 0.0 while looking populated.** `intraday_features.py` falls back to `0.0` when no VWAP is present; live 2026-07-30 shows 2,140 non-null rows with min=0.0 and max=0.0. This is the worst kind of dead feature — every coverage check passes. Computed properly (typical-price × volume over the session), VWAP deviation is **the single best-ranked intraday signal in the whole dataset**. Two-line fix, high value.
  - **The fetcher writes the vendor's "last price" snapshot as if it were a bar**, timestamped at fetch time rather than on the 15-min grid (off-grid, zero-volume, `O=H=L=C`). July: 101,716 rows (5.5%) off-grid, 99.3% of them zero-volume; 18.1% of all July rows are flat. For names fetched successfully every cycle the ratio approaches 1:1 — **RELIANCE has 49 rows for a 25-bar session**. Because `intraday_features.py` selects bars **positionally** (`grp.iloc[:2]` = "opening range", `grp.iloc[:4]` = "first hour"), interleaved snapshots make "first 4 bars" cover 45 minutes instead of 60: stored `first_hour_vol_share` correlates only **0.642** with the same feature recomputed from clean grid bars, **5.9% exact match, MAE 0.060 on a 0–1 feature**, systematically biased low (0.197 vs 0.245). Same class as the 2026-07-30 `screener_momentum_score` irreproducibility finding.
  - **Screeners: informative, wired backwards, not independently tradeable — keep them, but not at this price.** 257,704 first-appearances scored over 22 days: **24 of 41 filters clear |t|>2 where ~2 would by chance**, and the pattern is coherent rather than noise — `orb5minLow` +0.162 (t=6.24) vs `orb5minHigh` −0.151 (t=−3.90) is a clean symmetric pair; `lowerHighLowerLow` +0.088 vs `higherHighHigherLow` −0.018; `todayBelow50SMA` +0.070 vs `todayAbove20SMA` +0.017; `todayGapDown` +0.060. Every weakness filter is positive, every strength filter negative or flat — so a breakout-flavoured 55%-weighted confluence score is rewarding exactly the negative-alpha ones. But **only `orb5minLow` has a positive absolute return** (+0.169% gross → +0.019% at 15bps, negative at 25bps); the +0.045% average "alpha" is largely a size artifact of benchmarking against an equal-weight universe containing the illiquid tail. Resource cost: `live_screener_appearances` **499 MB** + `live_screener_outcomes` **381 MB**, ~382k rows/day, plus a */15 collector and a 20-min-budget resolver ×3/day.
  - **`live_screener_ml_ranker.py` has no live skill: AUC = 0.4962.** Graded against actual same-day outcome (4,986 rows, 5 days), daily rank IC −0.0017 (t=−0.08), and the **highest-confidence quintile is the worst-returning** (wp 0.629 → −0.0397%). Small sample and a mid-window retrain, so suggestive not final — but its promotion gate is comparing held-out AUCs of a model whose live discrimination is nil.
  - **Binding constraint on all of the above: full-universe 15-min coverage only begins 2026-06-29 = 23 usable trading days**, over one mildly declining regime (universe drift −0.10%/day). Stated plainly in the report: the *sign* results (t up to −11, consistent across tiers and times), the production engine's negative alpha (t=−6.6) and the resolver's look-ahead are properties of the code and robust; the *magnitude* conclusions are 23-day estimates.
  - **Data genuinely required to change the answer** (nothing here is a vendor-purchase problem): longer 15-min history (the statistical ceiling); **bid-ask / order-book data** — `tick_data` and `order_book_snapshots` are both 0 rows, so slippage is assumed rather than measured and several configs sit within ±0.05% of break-even; **index intraday bars** (only `NIFTYBEES` exists, 710 bars) — without them there is no intraday relative-strength vs NIFTY and no beta hedge, which is the standard way to turn a small directional alpha into a tradeable market-neutral one; a **price-band/circuit table** (none exists) to exclude untradeable names; and 1-min/5-min bars, since 15-min is too coarse to resolve whether a stop or target filled first (the harness had to assume the stop — conservative but crude).
  - **Audit-backlog verification (checked against live DB and current source, not against the docs' own claims).** Confirmed **still open**: `nse_universe_history` → backtest path (it is referenced *only* in `backtester.py`'s `survivorship_gap()` reporting function, lines 103–133; the actual trade universe at lines 150/165 still reads `stock_ohlcv` — the gap is measured, not closed); the `adjustment_basis` follow-up (`relative_strength.py`/`ml_ensemble.py`/`breakout_classifier.py` still don't reference it, and it's moot anyway at **287 of 2,608,478 rows tagged = 0.011%**, by design); `live_datasource` coverage (**16 test files vs ~140 DB-writing Python files, ~11%**); Finding #31; pre-2026-07-31 screener feature values. Confirmed **actually done**: NSE bhavcopy backfill (3,238,963 rows, 1,377 dates, 2021-01-01→2026-07-30, 4,033 symbols, current); `screener_membership_snapshot` (24,882 rows writing as designed); weekly banking ratios (`nim` on 40 symbols ≈ the real bank share); the job destagger. **Corrected a false alarm**: `recommendation_log.entry_price` looks 97.8% NULL in aggregate but that is *historical rows only* — 07-31 is 241/313 and 07-30 is 262/505 filled, zero before 07-30, so the `scoring_engine._log_recommendations()` fix is working and the trailing-stop job now has real positions to act on. `mc_cagr_3y` being current-day-only is by design, not a defect. **Newly found**: `intraday-breadth-capture` is registered in `monitorScripts.ts` with a `job_heartbeat` row at **run_count=0** — monitored by table freshness only, never calls `recordHeartbeat` (same pattern as the `job-digest` gap closed earlier on 07-31), and its producer `intradayBreadth.ts` has written only **54 rows, last 2026-07-30**, so the breadth input to `intraday_regime.py` is usually dropped by that file's (correctly implemented) staleness guard rather than used.

  - **The 60 403/401/444 endpoints are genuine auth walls, not UA problems** — retried one per host with a warmed session + browser UA + `Referer`/`Origin`/`Accept-Language`; finology, stockedge, sensibull, ndtvprofit, tapetide, niftytrader(401), ET-subscriptions and trendlyne(444→403) all still refuse. It is thin exactly where `technical_signals` sits at 0% coverage: ownership 30, analyst 23, events 27. **Do not bulk-ingest the screener/technical families from this corpus** — they add columns to a matrix that is already 71% empty and re-derive signals already measured as unprofitable.

The standout: `investsights.in/api/v2/fundamentals/market/fiidii?days=9999` — **verified 2,584 daily rows, 2016-01-01→2026-07-30, in a single 738KB call**, with `fii_net`/`dii_net`/`fii_equity`/`fii_derivatives`/`mf_total` ~97% non-null (`fii_buy`/`dii_buy` are only 87/2584 — use the `_net` fields). This directly attacks the **55-distinct-dates** constraint that caps every screener/fundamental/ownership/sentiment conclusion this platform can currently draw. Then: MC `deals/list` + Tickertape `stocks/deals` (bulk/block with named counterparty and **`pctTransacted` = % of float, already cross-sectionally normalized, the most ML-ready field in the corpus**); the smartoptions per-strike IV/Greeks/buildup surface; `investsights.in/api/v2/investors/` (31,001 investors with new-entry/exit/increase/decrease counts — the platform has aggregate shareholding % only, no per-investor conviction tracking); and `frapi.marketsmojo.com/market_events/getData` (forward-dated event calendar, for conditioning and for *excluding* pending-binary-event names, not as alpha).

Every captured URL in the corpus carries a stale hardcoded date. (2) The header key in this response is **`name`**, NOT the `unique_name` documented elsewhere in this file for the Kayal screener API — the convention differs per Trendlyne API family, so resolve columns from `tableHeaders` at parse time and never index positionally (the 2026-07-30 max-pain call/put swap and the 2.1M-row corruption were both positional-indexing bugs).
  - **Nothing in this pass has a measured forward-return edge** — it measured availability, shape, freshness and orthogonality only. Per the standing lesson from the 07-30 bias audit (*a mechanism that could explain an anomaly is a hypothesis, not a finding, until the counterfactual is actually run*), each of the 5 candidates carries a stated test rather than a claimed edge. Preconditions recorded in the report before any of it ships: a mandatory `live_datasource` test per new fetcher (coverage is **16/~140**, ~11%, and this corpus is exactly the class that control exists for), a live-value re-test for any endpoint with a date/expiry in the URL, header-name column resolution, and disclosure-date (not event-date) stamping joined via `as_of.py` for all ownership/deal/analyst rows.
  - **FIXES IMPLEMENTED same session (2026-07-31).** All P0–P3 recommendations plus the two actionable §6 backlog items. Full before/after table in the audit doc's §8. Suites after: **892 Python passed / 65 skipped / 0 failed**, **333 TypeScript passed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Every fix live-run against the real production Postgres, not just unit-tested.
    - **`intraday_outcome_resolver.py` rewritten** to walk `intraday_ohlcv` 15m bars strictly after each signal's own `cycle_at`, entering at the first bar that *opens* after the signal and testing stop/target only on later bars (the entry bar's own high/low cannot decide the outcome — its intra-bar path relative to the fill is unknown). Deliberately does NOT fall back to the daily bar when 15m bars are missing; that fallback *was* the bug. Re-resolved all 11 history dates: **phantom gap exits 164 → 0**, PnL range **−100.04%…+6.32% → −3.56%…+4.52%**. The corrected figure cross-validates against my independent research harness at the same cost model (−0.658% vs −0.632% for 07-30). Also added a **stale-row purge**: rows this run did not produce are deleted, because re-resolution silently left daily-bar-era records behind for any symbol that could no longer be graded. Separately deleted **1,387 rows for 07-13/07-15/07-16** — dates predating `intraday_recommendations_history`, so without `cycle_at` they can never be honestly resolved and were poisoning `intraday_strategy_learner.py`.
    - **`intraday_fetcher.py`**: `_is_on_grid()` drops the vendor's in-progress "last price" quote (stamped at request time, zero volume, `O=H=L=C`); `parse_bars()` now computes and stores **session VWAP** (cumulative typical-price × volume, reset per IST session). Critically, VWAP is emitted as **NULL unless the session-open bar is present in the payload** — the earliest day of any lookback window is partial, and accumulating from an arbitrary mid-session bar produces a confidently wrong VWAP that the upsert would write over a previously-correct one. An honest NULL is recoverable; a wrong VWAP is not.
    - **`intraday_features.py`**: windows are now resolved in **minutes from the session open against each bar's own timestamp**, never by row position (`iloc[:2]`/`iloc[:4]` were being eaten by interleaved snapshot rows). It also **derives session VWAP from the bars themselves when the column is NULL** — which repairs *all historical sessions immediately*, not just future ones, since `intraday_ohlcv` cannot be backfilled (see below). Live backfill of 4 sessions: `vwap_deviation_pct` went **100% zero → ~97% non-zero (sd 2.4)** and `first_hour_vol_share` **0.197 → ~0.23**.
    - **`intraday_ranker.py`**: added `W_REVERSAL` (cross-sectional rank of inverted VWAP-deviation + intraday range-position, computed from *today's* 15m bars because the session state must be current as of the cycle — `technical_signals` is post-close), rebalanced `W_BREAKOUT` 0.45 → 0.20 / `W_SCREENER` 0.55 → 0.40, with an `assert` that the three sum to 1.0 (the `unified_ranker` `REGIME_WEIGHTS` drift bug of 2026-07-23 was exactly this going unchecked). breakout is cut rather than removed: its validated edge is real but on a ~10-day forward label, not this horizon. **The load-bearing fix is the emission gate**, not the sign change: Buy/Strong-Buy is only published while the engine's OWN trailing realised net PnL (from the now-honest outcome table) is positive over ≥100 trades in 10 days, with an `app_settings` escape hatch, and it **fails closed** on a DB error. Live: gate **CLOSED** (−0.456%/trade over 3,015 trades), **338 Buys downgraded to Hold, buy_pool 0**. Scores are still computed and stored so the learner and UI keep working and the gate can re-open by itself.
    - **`live_screener_ml_ranker.py`**: added `_live_auc()`, which grades the **deployed** model's stored probabilities against realised outcomes (`live_screener_ml_scores` → `live_screener_appearances` → `live_screener_outcomes`). Live result is **AUC 0.3778 over 47,559 rows** — *worse* than the 0.4962 first estimated, i.e. actively anti-predictive, against a reported held-out `test_auc` of 0.6408. While live AUC sits below a 0.52 floor the promotion margin is raised 0.01 → 0.05, because a small gain in a metric already shown not to transfer is not evidence. Deliberately **not** a hard block — blocking promotion outright would freeze the current, worse model permanently in place. Live AUC is now written into the model-status JSON so consumers can distinguish a model with demonstrated live discrimination from one without.
    - **`live_screener_resolver.py`**: 365-day retention guard on `live_screener_appearances`/`_outcomes` (499 MB + 381 MB). Verified the DELETE genuinely matches (2.7M rows at a 5-day window) and rolled back. **It is a growth guard, not a space reclaim** — the data is only ~5 weeks old so it is a no-op today; said plainly rather than implying it frees space now. Retention never fails the resolve run.
    - **Index intraday bars added** — `NIFTY50/^NSEI`, `NIFTYBANK/^NSEBANK`, `NIFTYIT/^CNXIT`, `NIFTYMIDCAP/^NSMIDCP`, `INDIAVIX/^INDIAVIX`, 976 bars each over 2026-06-09→07-31, plausible ranges (NIFTY50 23,116–24,525; INDIAVIX 11.5–16.3). **Yahoo only** — MC's TechCharts stock endpoint was probed live and serves indices under none of `NIFTY`/`NIFTY 50`/`^NSEI` — and via a separate URL template since indices use a caret ticker with no `.NS` suffix. Their VWAP is correctly NULL: Yahoo reports index volume as 0, so a volume-weighted price is genuinely undefined.
    - **`backtester.py`**: added `traded_window()` / `filter_to_traded_universe()`, wiring `nse_universe_history` into the **point-in-time universe** — the half of the survivorship fix that needs no price adjustment, so it is safe today. Prices still come from `stock_ohlcv`; unioning bhavcopy prices remains blocked on a split-adjustment pass (bhavcopy is RAW, `stock_ohlcv` is split-adjusted). Verified live on a real casualty: MEGASOFT stopped trading 2026-02-06, so a 2026-07-01 signal is dropped while a 2021-06-01 one is kept. Symbols absent from bhavcopy entirely are **left in and counted separately** — absence there is a backfill-coverage gap, not evidence the name was untraded.
    - **`intraday-breadth-capture` moved onto the scheduled 15-min chain** in `queues.ts`, ahead of `intraday_regime.py` which consumes it, with a real `recordHeartbeat`. It previously ran only as a side effect of `liveStockData.ts`'s **cache-driven** quote refresh, so it fired only when a user request happened to miss the cache during market hours — hence 54 rows total with multi-day gaps, and `intraday_regime.py`'s (correctly implemented) staleness guard dropping breadth from the fusion most cycles.
    - **No bulk backfill of `intraday_ohlcv`** — it is a compressed TimescaleDB hypertable (47 of 59 chunks), and a predicate-wide UPDATE/DELETE would force full decompression, the same trap already documented for `stock_ohlcv`. Both defects are therefore fixed *forward* in the writer and *defended* on the read side, which is also why the `intraday_features.py` VWAP fallback matters: it repairs history that the writer never can.
    - **Tests**: 4 new/extended files, all **negative-controlled** by reverting the fix and confirming the new assertions fail — `test_intraday_fetcher.py` (+off-grid rejection, +session-VWAP incl. the partial-window NULL and the cross-session reset; fixtures moved to a real session-open epoch since arbitrary timestamps are now correctly rejected), `test_intraday_features.py` (+polluted-vs-clean window equivalence, +NULL-column VWAP fallback), `test_intraday_outcome_resolver.py` (new: entry bar cannot trigger its own stop, first-touch-wins, stop-wins-ties, **no GAP reason is ever producible**, costs charged), `test_intraday_ranker_gate.py` (new: gate open/closed/thin-sample/override/**fails-closed-on-DB-error**, weights sum to 1.0, reversal ranks weakness above strength and ignores snapshot bars), `test_live_screener_ml_live_edge.py` (new: inverted scores → AUC < floor, single-class window degrades rather than raises, strict-vs-standard margin policy incl. the real measured 0.3778).
    - **What was deliberately NOT done, with reasons**: the ranker's sign was corrected but **not inverted into a short book** (§4.3 — that edge is entirely in non-shortable names, and a flipped long score is break-even, not profitable); bhavcopy **prices** still not unioned into the backtest (needs the split-adjustment pass first); **screener aggregation** to a daily per-(symbol, filter) fact (the bigger storage win, but it changes the shape every consumer reads — deserves its own pass with a full consumer inventory); **Finding #31** (still needs regime-labeled outcome history that does not exist).
    - **The honest bottom line is unchanged by any of this**: the measurement problems are fixed and the engine no longer publishes negative-expectancy recommendations, but **none of that creates an intraday edge**. The effect that exists is real, robust in sign, and smaller than the cost of trading it. The gate re-opens by itself if that stops being true.
