Effectively-zero tail (1–21 non-null of 2,188): all `analyst_*`, all eight `tl_*`, `roe_annual`/`roce_annual`/`ebitda_margin`/`np_margin`, `block_deal_*` (6), `insider_buy_pct_90d` (4), `pledge_pct`/`pledge_chg_qoq` (21). **The family counts in the note above (ownership 30 / analyst 23 / events 27) count *endpoints*, not gaps** — mapped onto the real holes, `delivery_pct`/`delivery_trend_30d` and `sector_global_corr_21d` are **wiring gaps, not data gaps** (`nse_bhavcopy_fetcher.py` already pulls official DELIV_QTY/DELIV_PER, 3.4M rows; `mc_global_macro_fetcher.py` already exists), and the fundamentals cluster is mid-repair from the 07-31 weekly `financial_ratios_fetcher.py` change that already took it 0%→~74%, so **re-measure before building**. Genuine corpus wins are narrow — analyst revisions, bulk/block deals, MF holdings, pledge — i.e. *low tens* of columns, not hundreds.
  - (2) **The expired-`expDate` warning was under-stated, and the real failure shape defeats every check this platform runs.** Re-tested `smartoptions.trendlyne.com/phoenix/api/fno/market/filter/` on 2026-07-31 across live and expired expiries (live near = `2026-08-04`): omitted → 112 rows / IV 106/112 / real Greeks; `2026-08-04` → identical data (only the `near`/`next` label in row URLs differs, so **how you specify the expiry doesn't matter — only that it is live**); `2026-08-25` → 200 rows / IV 187/200 / real Greeks; **`2026-07-28` and `2026-05-26` (expired) → 200 rows with strike/LTP/volume/ttv/OI/OI-change *fully populated and frozen*, `implied_volatility` and `iv_change` NULL at 0/200, and `delta`/`gamma`/`rho`/`theta`/`vega` returned as 0.0 — NOT NULL**; `2021-01-01` → 0 rows. That combination passes a row-count check, a non-empty check, an OI-populated check, **and a Greeks null-coverage check at 100%**, because 0.0 is a value — and a zero delta/gamma is individually plausible for a deep-OTM strike, so no single row looks wrong. Ingesting it writes NULL into `atm_iv`/`iv_rank`/`iv_skew` (already only 9.4% covered, so it reads as "coverage didn't improve" rather than an error) plus 0.0 Greeks that `dataQualityChecks.ts`'s freshness/coverage/range checks cannot flag. **Any fetcher built on this endpoint must validate that `implied_volatility` is non-null on a majority of rows and reject a batch whose Greeks are all identically zero** — a null/coverage check alone is insufficient here.

- **Audit-backlog sweep: endpoint-corpus §5-1 and §1.5 implemented (2026-07-31).** User asked to go through every audit report + this file, identify what is still pending, and implement it. Read the four audit docs and **verified each "still open" claim against the live code and DB rather than trusting the docs' own status tables** — which mattered: `backtester.py`'s point-in-time universe (`traded_window()`/`filter_to_traded_universe()`) was already wired in the working tree despite being listed open, while the endpoint-corpus audit's `§5`/`§1.5` recommendations had no fix-status section at all and were genuinely untouched (CLAUDE.md's "FIXES IMPLEMENTED... audit doc's §8" refers to the *intraday* audit, which has a §8; the endpoint doc ended at §6). Full before/after in that doc's new §7. Suites after: **936 Python passed / 69 skipped / 0 failed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, **323 TS tests passed**.
  - **§5-1 FII/DII deep history shipped — and the audit's own recommendation for it was wrong in a way that would have corrupted the platform's macro-flow column.** New `fii_dii_history_fetcher.py` + migration `1785600000000_fii-dii-flow-segments`: `fii_dii_flow` **682 → 2,592 rows, now 2016-01-01 → 2026-07-30** (3.8× depth, 1,910 inserted, 610 gap-filled across 4,767 cells, 0 existing values clobbered), attacking the 55-distinct-dates constraint the 2026-07-30 bias audit named as capping every macro-flow conclusion. §4.1 of the endpoint audit said "`fii_net`/`dii_net` are ~97% non-null — use the `_net` fields". Measured live against all 2,584 rows and cross-checked against the 589 overlapping `tradebrains` rows already in the DB, **the endpoint reuses `fii_net` for two different quantities with no era flag in the payload**: where `fii_buy`/`fii_sell` are present it is cash equity (`buy − sell`, 82/87 exact); where they are NULL it is an **all-segment** figure (`fii_equity + fii_debt + fii_derivatives`, 2,472/2,497 exact) dominated by derivatives notional — **2024-09-26 reads 132,159 Cr against a true cash-equity 8,538 Cr, a 15× overstatement**. The cash-equity series in that era is `fii_equity`, which matches the existing tradebrains rows **exactly: 506/506 comparable overlap rows within 1 Cr, median absolute difference 0.03 Cr**. Two further traps handled: (a) **the endpoint's "DII" is not DII** — its `dii_net` equals `mf_total` (2,484/2,497), i.e. SEBI mutual funds only, while `fii_dii_flow.dii_net` means NSE's DII aggregate (banks + insurers + MFs + others); on the overlap they disagree by a **median 3,786 Cr and agree within 1 Cr on 0 of 576 rows**, so historical rows leave `dii_net` NULL and MF lands in its own `mf_*` columns (my first read dismissed this as a NULL artifact — re-testing on non-null rows only showed the mismatch is real, and both directions were checked before the mapping was fixed); (b) **21 "transitional" rows carry both the buy/sell pair and the segment breakdown** (2026-03-20…25), where `fii_net` is cash equity (2026-03-25: −1508.89 = buy−sell vs equity+debt+deriv = 4242.8) — confirming buy/sell-*presence*, not the existence of segment fields, is the correct discriminator. **That case was found by the mandatory `live_datasource` test, not by construction**; the first implementation silently dropped their derivatives data. Fetcher is **gap-fill by default** (never overwrites an existing non-NULL value or rewrites a row's `source`), so it is order-independent against the NSE/tradebrains writers; one call returns all history in ~3 s so backfill and daily top-up are the same operation; wired into `ml-daily-ops` *after* `fii_dii_fetcher.py` (today's authoritative NSE row lands first and wins) as a tolerated `T.run(...).catch(...)` step so a third-party outage cannot fail the daily ML chain, plus a `JOB_REGISTRY` entry. 17 unit tests (**negative-controlled** — reverting to the naive `fii_net` passthrough fails 3 targeted assertions) + 4 `live_datasource` tests that **re-verify both era invariants against the live response on every run**, so an upstream semantics change fails there instead of silently poisoning the column. **Standing lesson: ~97% non-null coverage told me the column was usable and said nothing about what it meant — cross-check a new source against rows you already trust before mapping any column; the tradebrains overlap is the only thing that exposed any of this.**
  - **§1.5 `verify_live_urls.py` hardened (all four changes).** Rewritten to **import** its parsing/normalization helpers from `probe_endpoint_payloads.py` rather than reimplement them — a second, drifting copy of a parser is precisely how the 2026-07-23 URL-as-symbol corruption survived undetected. It now emits a *verdict* rather than a status code: `ok` / `empty_ok` (200 with zero rows) / `needs_live_param` (URL pins a date or expiry) / `stale` / `auth_blocked` / `not_json` / transport failures. Live re-run over the real 919-URL corpus in 53 s: **`ok` 616, `empty_ok` 76, `needs_live_param` 75, `auth_blocked` 60, `not_json` 39, `http_404` 23, `stale` 14**, with **24 URLs recovered by collapsing doubled slashes** (17 of them now `ok`). **The number worth remembering: 820 endpoints returned HTTP 2xx and 204 of them — 24.9% — are not usable**; the old status-code-only script blessed all 820. All 10 `trendlyne.com/futures-options/api-filter/*` endpoints are correctly caught (200 with 123 "records" that are actually `tableHeaders`, off a hardcoded `30-sep-2021` expiry). Implementation note: an undelimited 8-digit run is genuinely ambiguous — **NSE's own bhavcopy archive uses DDMMYYYY** (`sec_bhavdata_full_04012021.csv`) while other providers use YYYYMMDD — so candidates are **parsed** rather than regex-matched, otherwise every numeric `companyid` in the corpus false-flags and the signal is worthless. 24 tests, each pinning a failure mode that actually occurred against this corpus.
  - **Confirmed still open after live verification, deliberately not attempted** (each is blocked on a decision or a dataset, not on effort): endpoint-corpus §5 items 2–5 (bulk/block deals, options surface, event calendar, superstar investors — each a new fetcher plus its mandatory `live_datasource` test, and §5 explicitly sequences them behind item 1; the options surface must not be attempted without §4.3's live-expiry discipline, since an expired `expDate` returns 200 with real OI, NULL IV and every Greek exactly `0.0`, passing row-count, non-empty *and* null-coverage checks); **unioning `nse_universe_history` prices into the backtest** (the point-in-time *tradability* half is wired and verified; prices stay blocked on a split-adjustment pass, since bhavcopy is RAW and `stock_ohlcv` is split-adjusted); **`screener_appearances` bitemporality** (needs a schema-design decision, and is mitigated going forward by `screener_membership_snapshot`); **`adjustment_basis` consumers** (moot at 287 of 2,608,478 rows tagged = 0.011%, by design to preserve Timescale compression); **screener aggregation to a daily per-(symbol, filter) fact** (changes the shape every consumer reads — needs its own pass with a full consumer inventory); **Finding #31** (still needs regime-labeled outcome history that does not exist); and **`live_datasource` coverage**, now **17 of ~140 DB-writing Python files (~12%)**, still the largest single control gap.

- **MoneyControl per-stock news wired into the stock pages, then reviewed and hardened (2026-07-31).** New `getMcStockNews` tRPC procedure → `fetchMcStockNews`/`parseMcStockNews` in `mcApiService.ts`, rendered by a new shared `src/components/McNewsCard.tsx` on all three stock surfaces (v1 `App.tsx` NewsTab, `MCStockInfoPanel`'s new "Stock News" tab, v4 `StockIntelligencePage` NewsTab). Endpoint behaviour verified by probing it directly, not by reading the code; full detail in the [mc_stock_news_endpoint_2026_07_31](../../Users/amitk/.claude/projects/D--Github-bharat-stock-intelligence/memory/mc_stock_news_endpoint_2026_07_31.md) memory. What mattered:
  - **`techmvc/mc_apis/mc_pricechart_homepage/news?sc_did=` is keyed on the MoneyControl `sc_id` (`mcsymbol`) — the exact opposite of MoneyControl's own TechCharts OHLCV endpoint, which is keyed on the raw NSE ticker** and where passing `mcsymbol` silently broke ~98% of the universe on 2026-07-23. `sc_did=RI`/`HDF01`/`TCS` return data; `sc_did=RELIANCE` returns `news: null`. Same vendor, two endpoints, inverted identifier conventions — probe each one, never infer by analogy from a sibling.
  - **It fails safe** (unknown id → `news: null`, not another stock's articles — checked against `RELIANCE`, `ZZZZ99`, `INVALIDXYZ`, empty), but `mcFetchJson` returns `null` on transport failure too, so the original code collapsed "MoneyControl is down" and "this stock has no news" into one *"No stock-specific news found"* message. Parse now returns an explicit `status: 'ok' | 'no_news' | 'fetch_failed'` and the shared empty state distinguishes them — the same silent-degradation class this file has recorded repeatedly.
  - **MoneyControl serves backslash-stripped JSON `\uXXXX` escapes**: real headings arrive containing the literal text `u2013` where an en-dash belongs (2 of 10 items on HDF01, live-confirmed). `decodeMangledEscapes()` repairs only sequences containing a digit that decode above ASCII, so ordinary hex-shaped words are untouched. **Check any other MoneyControl text field for this before rendering it.** Upstream `news.count` is also the page size (always 10), not a total — surfacing it as "N articles" over-claims; report what was actually returned.
  - **Review-found defects fixed alongside**: the whole 845-line `mcApiService.ts` had been rewritten to CRLF (2,086 changed lines across the feature → 288 after normalising, and the pre-existing missing EOF newline fixed with it); the panel fetched news while `activeTab === 'overview'` where it is never rendered, costing one of `mcSemaphore`'s 10 MoneyControl slots on every stock-drawer open; `additional_links` (Business / Earnings / Management Interviews) was parsed and never displayed; a `getStockMapping(...)?.mcsymbol` fallback beside `resolveMoneycontrolSymbol` was dead code (that function already prefers `mcsymbol` first), and the final raw-ticker fallback was inert since a raw ticker never resolves — an unresolvable symbol now short-circuits without calling MoneyControl at all; and ~210 lines of near-identical card markup existed in three files, now one component.
  - **`MCStockInfoPanel`'s tab bar renders only when `!section || section === 'all'`.** Every `App.tsx` mount passes a specific `section`, so the new tab was reachable only from `SlideOutDrawer`; added `'news'` to the `section` union and both `activeTab` mappings so it can be targeted directly. Worth remembering for any future tab added to that panel.
  - **Verification**: 19 new tests in `src/server/__tests__/mcStockNews.test.ts` (fixture captured verbatim from the live HDF01 response, including the real `u2013`), full TS suite **374 passed / 0 failed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, and the procedure exercised end-to-end over HTTP against the running server (`status=ok count=10 links=3`, en-dash correctly decoded, unresolvable symbol → `no_news` with no upstream call). The rendered DOM was **not** pixel-verified — the browser pane was blocked by an unresolving policy check in this session, same tooling limitation noted for Findings #89/#90 earlier.

- **"Fix all remaining" sweep — the whole audit backlog closed (2026-08-01).** Continuation of the endpoint-corpus session above; user asked to fix everything still pending. Five items done, two closed as won't-fix with reasons. Suites after: **983 Python passed / 78 skipped / 0 failed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. Every fix live-run against the real production DB or the real third-party endpoint.
  - **Finding #31 (regime tilts) — RESOLVED as shrinkage, not a fit, after the stated blocker turned out to be stale AND the real one turned out to be permanent.** `unified_ranker.py`'s docstring claimed no per-category history table existed; `stock_factor_breakdown_history` does exist now and even carries a `regime` column, so that claim was corrected. But measuring the actual data settled it the other way: **BULL has 3 lifetime regime episodes and CRASH 6**, and the factor history (13 distinct dates) covers **zero days of either** — while those two carry the most extreme multipliers (0.30, 1.50). Neither fitting the magnitudes nor validating their sign is possible, and the 170+ HIGH_VOL/SIDEWAYS "episodes" are day-to-day alternation, not independent samples; fitting on that reproduces exactly the overfit-then-write bug Finding #42 fixed in `strategy_optimizer.py`. So the tilts are now treated as **priors and shrunk halfway to neutral** via `shrink_tilt()`/`TILT_SHRINKAGE=0.5` — direction preserved exactly (CRASH still underweights breakout), magnitude damped (a 70% cut becomes 35%). A tilt actually *fitted* from outcomes still overrides at **full** strength, since shrinkage exists to damp intuition and a fitted value is not intuition. New `regime_tilt_fit_readiness()` reports per-regime whether enough history has accumulated to justify fitting, so the item unblocks itself instead of sitting open forever (live: every regime `ready_to_fit=False`). 6 new tests incl. one asserting shrinkage can never flip a tilt across neutral.
  - **Survivorship closed in PRICES — new `ohlcv_adjust.py`, wired into `backtester.load_ohlcv()`.** The tradability half was already done; prices were blocked on split adjustment (bhavcopy RAW vs `stock_ohlcv` split-adjusted). `corporate_actions` was checked and is unusable — 353 rows, dividends only, a six-week range — so factors are derived from NSE's own data. Live result: **delisted companies are tradeable for the first time** — MEGASOFT 1,260 bars ending exactly at its 2026-02-06 delisting, INFIBEAM 1,257, SEQUENT 1,251, all previously absent from `stock_ohlcv` entirely. 649 adjustment events persisted to a new `ohlcv_adjustment_factors` table. **Four wrong turns, each caught only by testing against reality and each of which would silently corrupt a whole price series — this is the durable lesson, not the feature:** (1) the first method assumed NSE's `PREV_CLOSE` is ex-date-adjusted. **It is NOT** (NESTLEIND: `prev_close=27116.4` alongside `close=2666.4`), so the split lives in `close/prev_close` *within one row*, not across rows. The wrong assumption made every real split invisible while surfacing clusters of artifacts — caught only by checking a known split, which is why NESTLEIND and IRCTC are now pinned in the tests. (2) Snapping off the *close* misses real actions when the ex-date has a big genuine move: IRCTC rose 10.6% that day, so `close/prev_close`=0.2212 against a true 1/5, while `open/prev_close`=0.1978. **Snap off the open.** (3) A same-date "cluster" guard added against the old method was silently discarding real events — **India averages ~3 corporate actions per trading day**, so several companies sharing an ex-date is the base rate, not an anomaly; it killed IRCTC's split *after* correctly detecting it. Removed. (4) **Circuit-locked days** trade at a single price exactly at the band, so 1.20/0.80 are indistinguishable from a 6:5 consolidation or 1:4 bonus by ratio alone — before the zero-range guard, **1.2 was the most common "ratio" in the entire history (99 events)**, i.e. the detector was mostly finding circuit locks. Also: the ratio grid must be built as two separate families (small p/q for bonuses, `1/k` and `k/1` only for deep splits) — crossing the large denominators with every numerator invented ratios like 9/25 and made the grid so dense the snap stopped filtering anything.
  - **A real data gap found along the way: `nse_bhavcopy_fetcher.py` filtered weekends out of its backfill**, so NSE's live Union Budget session on **Sunday 2026-02-01** (2,955 securities, verified fetchable) was never captured — an entire missing trading day, invisible to every row-count and freshness check. The archive 404s on genuine non-trading days (verified: Saturday 2026-01-31), so probing every calendar day is correct and costs only the 404s. **The missing sessions are NOT yet backfilled**: a parallel sweep to enumerate them got the archive to rate-limit me (403 Access Denied), so the count of affected dates is unknown — artifacts on 2025-02-03 and 2023-11-13 suggest the 2025 Budget Saturday and Diwali Muhurat trading are also missing. Re-run gently (serial, delayed) once unblocked. **Also worth remembering: `archives.nseindia.com` does not honour HEAD** — a HEAD sweep reported 0 sessions where GET returned 349KB of real data.
  - **Endpoint audit §5-2 shipped — `tickertape_deals_fetcher.py`** brings `pctTransacted` (deal size as % of float) into `block_deals`, the cross-sectionally comparable field NSE's own feed lacks (qty/value are absolute: a ₹12cr deal is a control transaction in a microcap and a rounding error in Reliance). Live: 978 rows, 966 with pct, 201 symbols — e.g. EIGHT ROADS INVESTMENTS MAURITIUS exiting **27.41% of SHADOWFAX's float for ₹852cr**. Three endpoint quirks verified and pinned in tests, each a silent-failure mode: `type=block`/`type=bulk` are **ignored** (identical mixed set returned); `skip` is ignored while `offset` paginates (a loop on `skip` re-fetches page 1 forever while looking like progress); and **the feed is not only bulk/block** — it also carries ten flavours of insider filing, so the fetcher filters to Bulk/Block rather than silently changing what `block_deals` means for every existing reader. **Follow-up worth taking: those insider rows carry `pctTransacted`, which `insider_transactions_fetcher.py` (NSE PIT filings) does not** — the same endpoint could materially improve insider features, in that fetcher's table rather than this one.
  - **`live_datasource` coverage 17 → 23 of ~140 fetchers**, prioritised by blast radius rather than count: `test_live_datasource_feature_matrix_fetchers.py` covers five fetchers that write directly into `technical_signals` (delivery_volume, nt_dashboard, preopen, mc_techscanner, trendlyne_fundamentals), plus the two new fetchers above. All verified passing against real endpoints. Writing them re-confirmed the point of the exercise in a small way: two of my own assumptions about response shape were wrong (NiftyTrader returns `{indices, stocks}`, not `resultData`; MTO fields are `deliverable_qty`/`qty_traded`/`delivery_pct`) — the fetchers were healthy, my guesses were not.
  - **Two items CLOSED as won't-fix** (recorded in the intraday audit's new §9 so future passes stop re-opening them): **`adjustment_basis` consumers** — 287 of 2,608,478 rows tagged (0.011%), and backfilling means decompressing 21 of 24 Timescale chunks and permanently losing the compression; the mixed-basis problem it was meant to detect is now handled at source by `ohlcv_adjust.py`. **`screener_appearances` bitemporality** — `screener_membership_snapshot` already makes values reproducible from 2026-07-31 forward, and a bitemporal rebuild cannot recover state that was overwritten in place, so it would cost a dual-write migration to fix nothing historical; treat pre-07-31 screener-derived numbers as void.

Commits here are therefore made **by explicit path, never `-A`**. `.claude/worktrees/` already exists — running concurrent sessions in separate worktrees avoids this class of collision entirely.

- **Second backlog sweep — four more genuinely-open audit items, found by re-reading the audits rather than my own notes (2026-08-01).** User asked again for anything still pending; re-scanning `docs/audit-2026-07-28/FULL_STACK_AUDIT.md` (not just the newer docs) surfaced items neither my task list nor the newer audits' "still open" tables carried. Suites after: **1,006 Python passed / 78 skipped / 0 failed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean. All live-verified.
  - **THE find: `insider_buy_pct_90d` was reading the wrong column and had 0.18% coverage.** The 2026-07-30 bias audit diagnosed `insider_trades.date` as TEXT holding NSE's display format (`"05 Apr, 2022"`, 46,194 of 46,198 rows) and **added a parsed `date_iso` column to fix it — but never switched the consumer.** `insider_features.py` still did `WHERE date >= ? AND date <= ?`, a lexicographic string compare of `"05 Apr, 2022"` against `"2026-05-03"`, which matches essentially nothing: the feature was populated on **4 of 2,187 rows**. One-line fix to `date_iso` → **151 symbols** (mean 0.020, sd 0.140; TATASTEEL/RELIANCE/BEL net-buying, 148 net-selling). **Standing lesson: adding a repair column is only half a fix — grep every consumer of the broken column in the same session, or the repair sits there doing nothing while the audit records it as done.**
  - **`recommendation_log`'s "residual NULL entry_price" (audit line 2346) was never a barrier gap — it was the universe gap, in a second writer.** Measured live: 87 symbols with NULL entry_price on 2026-08-01, **all 87 had no `technical_signals` row at all**, only 4 had any price history, and **84 were absent from the `nse_stocks` master** — including `13510368`, the exact raw numeric id the bias audit purged from `unified_recommendations`. `unified_ranker` gained `_restrict_to_tradeable_universe()` then; `scoring_engine._log_recommendations()` never did. Added the mirrored filter (live: drops 86 of 363 candidates) and purged **2,521** unactionable historical rows. Today's coverage **77.8% → 99.0%**. A recommendation for a symbol with no price cannot be entered, stopped, graded or backtested — the row should not exist.
  - **`_trading_days_back` was holiday-blind in TWO files** (audit line 1161). `delivery_volume_fetcher.py` and `fno_rollover_fetcher.py` each hand-rolled "step back, keep weekdays", so `--days N` silently covered fewer than N real sessions. Measured over a 90-session window: **3 wasted fetches on real holidays** (Ambedkar Jayanti, Good Friday, Eid) and 5 calendar days of lost reach — `--days 90` actually covered 87. Both now share `as_of.trading_days_back()`, which reads the exchange's own session list from `stock_ohlcv`/`nse_universe_history`. **Deliberately NOT `market_holidays`**: that table is built from observed gaps and stops at 2026-04-14, so it cannot answer questions about the recent range these callers ask about. Also fixed the same file's None-vs-`[]` contract (404-holiday now returns `[]`, a real failure returns `None`) — collapsing both to `None` made a throttled run indistinguishable from a run of holidays, the bug already fixed once in `insider_transactions_fetcher.py`.
  - **NSE weekend sessions backfilled — but only 3 of the 25 "found", and checking that is what mattered.** The rate limit had lifted, so I re-probed serially (1.2s delay) rather than with the parallel sweep that got me blocked. 25 weekend dates returned HTTP 200 with 2,500+ parseable rows. **They are almost all fakes: the archive RE-SERVES the previous session's file for weekend dates instead of 404ing** — md5 proved Sunday 2024-10-20 byte-identical to Friday 2024-10-18, likewise four *consecutive* Sundays in Oct/Nov (NSE holds Muhurat trading once a year). Only **3 are genuine**: Diwali Muhurat **2023-11-12** and the Budget sessions **2025-02-01** and **2026-02-01** — which independently match the three artifact dates `ohlcv_adjust.py` had flagged. Backfilled those (sessions 1,378 → 1,381). Crucially this is **not** corrupting even without the check, because `parse_bhavcopy` keys every row off the file's own `DATE1` rather than the requested date, so a re-serve just upserts rows that already exist — but `run_one()` would have logged a session on a day the market was shut, so it now detects and reports the re-serve and counts 0. **Had I trusted 200 + plausible row count, I would have inserted 22 days of duplicate Friday data stamped with weekend dates into the very table built to be the point-in-time universe.**
  - **Insider filings now carry `pctTransacted`.** `tickertape_deals_fetcher.py --insider` writes the feed's insider categories to `insider_trades` with % -of-float — the materiality dimension NSE's PIT feed lacks and `insider_buy_pct_90d` (a pure quantity ratio, and near-binary in practice) cannot express. Live: NOVARTIS AG exiting **70.68% of NOVARTIND's float**, against promoter sells of 0.9% and 1.0% that a ratio scores identically. Note `insider_trades.id` is a **BIGINT PK with no default** (NSE rows occupy 9..46k), so unlike `block_deals` a `tt:` string key does not fit — ids are derived deterministically from the low 48 bits of Tickertape's ObjectId plus a 10^15 offset, so a re-run upserts rather than duplicating and the two sources cannot collide.
  - **Verified already-fixed, not re-done:** Finding #43 (`mf_sector_flow_fetcher.py` historical overwrite) has its `date >= floor` bound in place.

Given this repo's extensive prior perf work (Round 4 audit fixes #33/#34/#37, job destagger, index additions — see memory), ran 3 parallel research-only audits first (frontend rendering, live tRPC request path, DB query/index) rather than re-discovering already-fixed ground, then implemented the findings. All changes `tsc --noEmit` clean, full vitest suite (275 tests) green, `vite build` succeeds. **No live Postgres in this sandbox — the new migration (`migrations/1785700000000_screener-appearances-symbol-and-options-oi-date-index.sql`) has NOT been applied; run `npm run migrate:up` against the real `POSTGRES_URL` before the index-dependent query fixes below see their intended benefit** (same class of gap as `migration_tool_verification_gotcha` memory — declaring/writing a migration is not the same as applying it).
  - **Backend query fixes**: `screeners.router.ts`'s `getScreenerBasket` had regressed the exact correlated-subquery-per-row anti-pattern Finding #33 fixed elsewhere (`WHERE ts.date = (SELECT MAX(date) FROM technical_signals t2 WHERE t2.symbol = ts.symbol)`, O(total historical rows)) — rewritten to the same `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY symbol ...)` pattern already used correctly in `misc.router.ts`. `getStockScreeners` ran 4 independent screener-table lookups sequentially (`await` × 4) instead of `Promise.all`. `commandCenter.router.ts`'s `getUnifiedScoreForSymbol` (likely the single highest-traffic per-symbol endpoint — every stock-detail page load) joined against `SELECT * FROM technical_signals WHERE date = (SELECT MAX(date)...)` — materializing every symbol's full ~300-column row for the latest date just to pick one — rewritten as two parallel indexed point lookups (`WHERE symbol = ?`) instead of a join; `getBuyRecommendations`'s equivalent subquery kept the join (needed, since it returns up to 200 symbols) but now projects only the ~40 columns actually used instead of `SELECT *`. Three "latest close per symbol" sites (`scoring.router.ts` `getStrategyPicks`, `ml.router.ts` `getSignalReportCard`'s `activeSignalGrowth`, `signals.router.ts` `getSignalTracking`) each did `JOIN (SELECT symbol, MAX(date) GROUP BY symbol)` or `WHERE (symbol,date) IN (SELECT symbol, MAX(date) GROUP BY symbol)` — full-table aggregation + re-scan — rewritten to the same `ROW_NUMBER()` pattern (chose this over Postgres's `DISTINCT ON` deliberately: `confluenceEngine.ts`'s own comment already documents `DISTINCT ON` as Postgres-only, not translated for the SQLite dev fallback, so `ROW_NUMBER()` is this codebase's established cross-dialect equivalent).
  - **Caching gap — the largest single finding.** `market.router.ts` (`getGlobalMarketData`/`getMarketOverview`/`getTopMovers`/`getBreakouts`/`getSectorPerformance`), `indices.router.ts` (`getAllIndices`/`getGlobalIndices`/`getIndexStocksList`/`getIndexConstituents`), `moneycontrol.router.ts` (11 of 12 procedures — only `getMcConsolidated` was already wired to `mcApiService.ts`'s internal 1h cache), and `fundamentals.router.ts` (`getRatios`/`getShareholding`/`getCorporateActions`/`getMFInvestments`/`getSmartMoneyFlow`) all called their live MoneyControl/NiftyTrader/ET upstream fetch functions directly on every single request with **no caching at all** — every dashboard poll tick (30-60s) from every open browser tab was a synchronous round-trip to a third-party API, and at real concurrency this both inflates p50 latency to the upstream's latency and risks the upstream rate-limiting/banning the app (exactly the failure mode `cacheService.ts`'s `fetchWithCache` — Redis/in-memory fallback + in-flight de-dup/cache-stampede protection — already exists to prevent). Wrapped all of the above in `fetchWithCache`, TTL chosen per data volatility (30s live indices/overview, 60s movers/breakouts, 900s intraday-ish MC price/vwap, 3600s quarterly-ish fundamentals/ratios/shareholding, 300s smart-money-flow). `ml.router.ts`'s `getModelRocDiagnostics` ran an **unbounded** query over the ever-growing `signal_outcomes` table (237,816+ rows) plus a synchronous Mann-Whitney rank-sort AUC computation **on every call, in the request handler** — added a `days` window param (default 365) and wrapped the whole computation in `fetchWithCache` (300s), since the underlying data only changes on batch-job cadence.
  - **DB index gaps** (new migration, not yet applied — see above): `screener_appearances` had indexes on `appeared_date`/`screener_id` but none on `symbol`, despite `getScreenerAppearanceHistory` accepting a symbol-only lookup — added `idx_scap_symbol(symbol, appeared_date DESC)`. `stock_options_oi` had `(symbol, date DESC)` but `getOptionsIntelligence` does `WHERE date = (SELECT MAX(date)...)` with `date` not the leading column — added `idx_soo_date(date DESC)`. `technical_signals` only had two redundant single-column `symbol` indexes and one on `date`, no composite — added `idx_tsig_symbol_date(symbol, date DESC)` to serve the new point-lookup queries above. **Deliberately did NOT add an index on `confluence_signals`** despite `ml.router.ts`/`confluence.router.ts`/`technicals.router.ts` all wrapping `computed_at` in `date(...)::text` (defeating both a plain index and, more importantly, TimescaleDB's own chunk-exclusion pruning on this compressed hypertable) — CREATE INDEX behavior on compressed Timescale chunks for a non-segmentby column carries real uncertainty this sandbox couldn't live-verify, matching this repo's own precedent of deferring risky operations on compressed hypertables (`ADD CONSTRAINT` on `confluence_signals` was similarly deferred in the 2026-07-23 URL-corruption fix). Fixed only the one site with a clean, low-risk rewrite instead (`ml.router.ts`'s `getSignalReportCard` outer `WHERE` — cast the *literal* side to `::timestamptz` instead of the column, keeping `computed_at` bare/sargable for chunk exclusion); left the other two sites (`confluence.router.ts`'s `DATE(cs.computed_at) = so.signal_date` — DATE compared to a TEXT column, possibly a **live correctness bug**, not just a perf one, unverifiable without a real DB — and `technicals.router.ts`'s `date(cs.computed_at) = ?`) untouched and flagged rather than blind-fixed.
  - **Frontend rendering fixes**: `DashboardPage.tsx` re-sorted the full live `stocks` list (`topGainers`/`topLosers`/`advancers`/`decliners`) on every 2-3s `getQueueStats`/`getSignals` poll tick while signal generation is running — wrapped in `useMemo([stocks])`. `App.tsx`'s `handleLogin`/`handleSelectIndexByName`/`toggleWatchlist` were plain functions recreated on every `App()` render (a ~4000-line component with dozens of `useState` calls) and fanned out through `AppShell`'s `sidebarProps` into a `React.memo(SidebarInner)` plus ~15 other route components — wrapped all three in `useCallback` (had to reorder `handleLogin`/`syncUserMutation` before `toggleWatchlist` since the latter references the former in its dep array — `const` TDZ, not a runtime issue since it's only invoked inside a later callback, but the deps array itself needed forward visibility). `App.tsx`'s `Screener` component recomputed `displayStocks`/`processedStocks` (filter+sort) on every render regardless of whether the underlying query data changed — wrapped in one `useMemo`. `LiveMarketScreener.tsx`/`EODMarketScreener.tsx` both re-sorted their result lists on every render including unrelated 30-60s poll ticks from sibling queries — memoized; `EODMarketScreener` was also missing the `isVisible`-gated-polling pattern its siblings (`LiveMarketScreener`/`MarketIndices`/`GlobalMarkets`/`Watchlist`) already use, so it polled even when its tab wasn't visible — added the same `document.visibilitychange` gate. `Watchlist.tsx`'s `watchlistStocks` did an unmemoized filter→map with a nested O(n·m) `.find()` per row against `liveQuotes`/`watchlistDetails` on every 10s poll — rebuilt as `useMemo` + `Map` lookups. **Deleted a genuinely dead ~558-line `Dashboard` component** (`App.tsx`, confirmed zero `<Dashboard` JSX references anywhere) along with 8 now-unused eager (non-lazy) imports it alone consumed (`MomentumIntelligence`, `IndexOverview`/`InstitutionalInsights`/`PennyStockIntelligence`, `TopMoversIntelligence`, `MarketIndices`, `GlobalMarkets`, `IntradayBreakouts`) — every real route uses the already-lazy-loaded `DashboardPage`/`V2Dashboard`/`V3Dashboard`/`MarketCommandCenter` instead, so this was pure eager-bundle weight with zero functional benefit. (Deletion done via the `Edit` tool rather than a shell `sed` line-range delete — the sandbox's auto-mode classifier blocks raw in-place file mutation outside the sanctioned edit path; asked the user, who confirmed the `Edit` tool route.)
  - **Deliberately NOT attempted, with reasons** (documented rather than silently skipped): `NSEStockDiscovery.tsx` renders the entire unfiltered NSE universe (thousands of rows, no virtualization) — flagged as a recommendation, not fixed, since it needs a new dependency (`react-window`/`react-virtual`) and a product decision on default filtering, not a drop-in change. `FII/DII` data is independently fetched with different `days` args across 8 components (`App.tsx` ×2, `AlphaCockpit`, `MoneyFlowPulseWidget`, `MoneyFlowPage`, `V3Dashboard`, `TradeDecisionCockpit`, `V2Dashboard`) — no cross-component cache dedup, since the varying `days` param means genuinely different query keys; a fix here means centralizing on one widest-range fetch with client-side slicing, an architectural change out of scope for this pass. The 2.2MB main JS chunk (`vite build`'s own size warning) was not addressed — code-splitting the remaining eager dependencies is a larger, separate effort.
  - **Standing lesson for this session, matching an existing pattern in this file**: writing a SQL comment (`-- ...`) *inside* a JS template-literal SQL string that itself contains a literal backtick character (e.g. `` -- Was `WHERE ...` `` ) silently terminates the outer template literal early — caught immediately by `tsc --noEmit` (`',' expected` at the comment's position), but worth grepping for on any future inline-SQL-comment edit: never put a backtick inside a comment that lives inside a backtick-delimited query string.
  - **Self-correction, same day**: user asked directly "are all identified gaps fixed?" — checking the actual code against all three original audit outputs (not against my own task list, which was already a filtered subset) found **2 real findings that had been identified by my own audits but never converted into a task or a fix**: `misc.router.ts`'s `getTradeDecisionCockpitData` (polled every 60s, zero caching) and `technicals.router.ts`'s `getTechnicalTrends` (falls back to a live, inline, 5-batch-concurrent MoneyControl fetch on a cold DB cache, also zero caching). Both fixed with the same `fetchWithCache` pattern used throughout this session (45s / 300s TTL respectively). **Lesson: a task list built by triaging audit findings can silently drop items between "the audit found it" and "a task got created for it" — after finishing, diff the fix list back against the original audit findings one more time before declaring the pass complete, don't just trust the task tracker.**
  - **"Fix all" follow-up (same day)**: user asked to close every remaining gap, including the ones previously flagged as deliberately deferred. All closed:
    - **`confluence.router.ts`'s `DATE(cs.computed_at) = so.signal_date` (2 sites) was a genuine, probably-live Postgres correctness bug**, not just a perf concern — comparing a DATE-cast TIMESTAMPTZ against a column with a *declared* TEXT type has no implicit operator in Postgres (unlike comparing against an untyped bind parameter, which Postgres contextually types — confirmed `technicals.router.ts`'s sibling `date(cs.computed_at) = ?` does NOT have this bug, since `?` there is a parameter, not a column). Fixed with the exact `::text`-cast-the-DATE-side pattern already established in this codebase's history for this exact table/column (`ml.router.ts`'s `getSignalReportCard`) — a no-op on the SQLite fallback (cast stripped, existing native `DATE()` behavior unchanged), corrects the Postgres path.
    - `getSmartMoneyFlow`'s subquery: dropped `SELECT *` to only the 5 columns actually read. Deliberately did NOT add a date bound here (unlike sibling fixes) — `fii_chg_qoq`/`mf_chg_qoq` are quarterly figures that can persist unchanged on a symbol's row for weeks, so narrowing the window risks silently dropping symbols whose latest value sits on an older row, an unverifiable behavior change without a live DB.
    - `getSystemStatus` (admin monitor page, ~30 scripts): the ~60 round trips were already concurrent *across* scripts (`Promise.all(MONITOR_SCRIPTS.map(...))`, not a true N+1 loop) but sequential *within* each script (`await getLastRunAt()` then `await getScriptStats()`, fully independent lookups) — parallelized with `Promise.all([...])` per script, plus wrapped the whole procedure in a 30s cache (well below every staleness threshold it itself checks against).
    - Leading-wildcard `LIKE` scans on `news_sentiment_items` (`getAIAnalysis`): rather than the invasive "normalized join table + backfill + rewire the news ingest pipeline" option, added a `pg_trgm` GIN trigram index (`idx_nsi_symbols_json_trgm`, `idx_nsi_title_trgm`) — purely additive, Postgres-only (matches every migration in this repo), zero query-code changes since the planner picks it up automatically.
    - `NSEStockDiscovery.tsx` (unbounded rendering of the full NSE universe): **deliberately did NOT add `react-window`/`react-virtual`** — a new dependency needing `npm install` unverified in this sandbox, and one that works against the same session's bundle-size goal. Instead: debounced the search input (300ms, was re-filtering thousands of rows per keystroke) and capped the initial render to a 60-item page with a "Load more" button, resetting on filter change. Solves the actual problem (thousands of DOM nodes on first open) with zero new dependencies and nothing needing live-browser visual verification.
    - FII/DII cross-component fetch: of the 8 `getFiiDiiFlow` call sites, 7 (`App.tsx` ×2, `AlphaCockpit`, `TradeDecisionCockpit`, `V2Dashboard`, `V3Dashboard`, `MoneyFlowPulseWidget`) are all "current flow snapshot" use cases (1–10 rows actually read) — standardized all 7 onto the `{days:10}` key already used by 3 of them, so React Query now shares one cache entry/fetch across however many are mounted concurrently, with each component slicing what it needs client-side (`.slice(0,6)`, `[0]`, etc. — verified each call site's actual downstream usage before changing its `days` value, not just bumped them blindly). **`MoneyFlowPage.tsx` deliberately excluded** — its whole purpose is deep, user-selectable history up to `days:4000`, a fundamentally different cost profile; forcing it onto the shared key would either overfetch for the snapshot cards or cap its own range. Left a comment there explaining why, so this isn't "fixed" into the shared cache by a future pass that doesn't re-derive the reasoning.
    - Main JS bundle (2.2MB, Vite's own build warning): added `manualChunks` in `vite.config.ts` splitting `firebase/app`+`firebase/auth`, `recharts`+`lightweight-charts`+`d3`, and `motion` into separate vendor chunks. **Verified, not assumed**: main entry chunk gzip dropped 567.87KB → 380.10KB (−33%), now 4 independently-cacheable, parallel-downloadable files instead of one blob. **Deliberately did NOT** attempt the deeper fix (extracting App.tsx's ~9 inline sub-components — `StockDetails`, `OptionChain`, `MFAnalysis`, `FnOSignals`, `TechnicalAnalysis`, `FundamentalInsights`, `Backtest`, `MarketMap`, `NewsTab` — into separately lazy-loaded files, which is the change that would actually reduce first-load bytes rather than just improve caching/parallelism) — real first-load-byte reduction, but a much larger refactor touching a big, complex surface with no live browser available in this sandbox to catch a visual regression.
    - Verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean, 275/275 tests still passing (incl. `monitorSystemStatus.test.ts` confirming the `getSystemStatus` parallelization is behaviorally identical), `vite build` succeeds with the new chunk split. 25 files changed total across both rounds. **Two new migrations still need `npm run migrate:up` run against the real `POSTGRES_URL`** (this sandbox has none) before their indexes exist: `1785700000000_screener-appearances-symbol-and-options-oi-date-index.sql` and `1785800000000_news-sentiment-trigram-indexes.sql`.

    - **A JSX-nesting bug caught by process, not luck**: `V1Screener.tsx`'s first draft was missing one `</div>` (4 divs needed to close before `</Card>`, only wrote 3) — caught immediately because `tsc --noEmit` treats an unclosed JSX element as a hard parse error, not a silent issue. This is why every extraction in this round was verified with a typecheck immediately after writing the file, before moving to the next one, rather than batching several extractions and checking at the end.

Caught by grepping every JSX tag used in the new file against the original's icon imports before wiring it up, rather than trusting the copy was verbatim. Required importing `Bookmark` as **both** its own name and `Bookmark as WatchlistIcon` in the same file (this codebase already does exactly this in App.tsx — importing the same lucide-react icon under two different local names for two different UI purposes is valid ES module syntax, not a bug).
    - **Measured, real result**: main entry chunk gzip **380.10KB → 352.11KB** (a further 7.4% cut on top of Round 3's `manualChunks` win), each extracted component now its own small chunk (V1StockDetails 8.90KB gzip down to V1NewsTab 1.20KB) loaded only when a user actually opens that tab — combined across both rounds, the main chunk has gone from the original **567.87KB → 352.11KB gzip, a 38% total reduction**, with zero runtime behavior change (same Suspense fallback pattern already used by every other lazy route in this app).

  - **`moneycontrol.router.ts` scId-resolution gap fixed (2026-08-02, same session), reported by the user as "lots of SWOT errors in logs."** User's proposed fix ("stop HTML-scraping, use the JSON `swot/details?scId=...` URL") didn't match the code — `fetchMcSwot`/`insightService.ts` already call that exact JSON API; the only real HTML-scraping SWOT reference in the codebase is `trendlyneService.ts:99`, a different provider entirely. Found the actual matching root cause instead: all 13 single-symbol MC procedures in `moneycontrol.router.ts` did `getStockMapping(input.symbol)?.mcsymbol || input.symbol` — and `mapping.mcsymbol` is `""` (empty string, falsy) for a real subset of `stocklist.ts` entries, not just `undefined` for symbols missing from the file. `"" || input.symbol` silently falls through to the **raw NSE ticker**, which is not a valid MoneyControl `scId` (opaque, provider-specific — this codebase's own "Ticker Resolution Strategy" section already documents never guessing a provider ID). Fixed by routing all 13 through `resolveMoneycontrolSymbol()` (already implemented correctly in `stockMapping.ts`, already proven working in `getMcStockNews` per [mc_stock_news_endpoint_2026_07_31] — hardcoded mapping → in-memory cache → MC autocomplete fallback), returning `null` instead of a broken live call when even autocomplete can't resolve an obscure symbol (type-consistent: every `fetchMcXxx` function already returns `Xxx | null`).
    - **Live-verified end-to-end, both directions, not just code-read.** `scId=BE03` (a populated `mcsymbol`) → real SWOT JSON, confirming the endpoint itself was never the problem. `scId=ASMS` (Bartronics India's raw NSE ticker, one of stocklist.ts's empty-`mcsymbol` entries) → MoneyControl returns a blank SWOT response, proving the bug fires in production today. `stocklist.ts` was also checked directly rather than trusting CLAUDE.md's stale "180 liquid stocks" figure — it actually has **2,005 entries**, of which **23 have `"mcsymbol": ""`** (illiquid/thin names: ASMS, SGIL, SAMTELTD, ZCHANAIN, NEWMKTADV, ZSANMCOM, MRUTR, COVIDH, CHEMIESYNT, ZHEMHOLD, OSWALEA, HARIGCRANK, FRONTLINE, SHENTRACON, SHRIAHIMSA, ENRIN, MUFIN, WOL3D, `13510368` — the same corrupted numeric-symbol artifact documented in the 2026-07-30/08-01 bias-audit sessions, still sitting in this file unresolved — STRATMONT, PANCHSHEEL, `GVT&D`, PRSNTIN). Two of the 23 were tested against the real autocomplete endpoint with the query the code actually sends (`query=<raw ticker>`, not the company name): `ASMS` finds no exact match (correctly degrades to `null` — Bartronics may not be indexed under that ticker in MC's autosuggest at all) but **`MUFIN` finds an exact match** (`", MUFIN,"` present in the candidate's `pdt_dis_nm`) → real scId `APMFI54277` → confirmed against the live SWOT endpoint returns real strengths/weaknesses/opportunities/threats data for Mufin Green Finance. This proves the fix's fallback genuinely recovers data for at least some of the 23 broken symbols, not just fails safe for all of them.
    - Verification: `tsc --noEmit` clean, full test suite still 275/275 passing (no existing test asserted the old broken fallback behavior). No production logs were available to cross-check against (this is a fresh worktree that never ran a server — `logs/*.log` all confirmed empty), so this fix rests on live third-party API verification rather than reproducing the user's literal reported error text; flagged to the user rather than claimed as a byte-for-byte log match.
  - **Follow-up same day ("make sure all sources are able to fetch data ... no other similar issue exists"): swept the whole codebase for the same bug class, found 2 more live sites, and separately verified every directly-testable upstream provider is actually reachable.** Grepped for `getStockMapping(...)?.<providerField> || <rawFallback>` across `src/` (the exact shape of the bug just fixed) rather than trusting the prior fix was exhaustive.
    - **`stocks.router.ts`'s `getAlphaQuantDetail`** and **`fno.router.ts`'s `getStockFno`** had the identical `mapping?.mcsymbol || input.symbol` bug, unfixed in the prior pass because they live outside `moneycontrol.router.ts`. Both now route through `resolveMoneycontrolSymbol()`. `getAlphaQuantDetail` combines MC data with independent score/tradebrains data via `Promise.all` — an unresolvable symbol now skips only the MC leg (`scId ? getMcConsolidatedData(...) : Promise.resolve(null)`, then `{...null, score, ...}` — spreading `null` into an object literal is a no-op in JS, not a crash) rather than failing the whole procedure. `getStockFno` has no non-MC data to fall back to, so an unresolvable symbol now returns `{success: false, error: 'No MoneyControl mapping found for this symbol'}` instead of silently querying MC with a raw ticker.
    - **`mcApiService.ts`'s `getMcConsolidatedData`** had its own *third*, independent copy of the same broken fallback (`const mapping = getStockMapping(symbol); const effectiveScId = mapping?.mcsymbol || scId`) — redundant re-derivation of an `scId` its caller had (in the fixed call sites) already resolved correctly. Not itself exploitable post-fix (since `"" || scId` correctly falls through to the caller's already-resolved value when `mcsymbol` is empty), but fragile: any future caller that reintroduces the naive `getStockMapping(...)?.mcsymbol || rawSymbol` pattern would have silently poisoned all ~19 of this function's fanned-out `fetchMcXxx` calls (technical, equity-cash, SWOT, essentials, insights, price/volume, analyst rating, earnings/price forecast, consensus, ratios, shareholding, chart patterns, etc. — this is the function every MC-backed procedure ultimately calls). Simplified to trust the caller's `scId` outright and removed the now-dead `getStockMapping` import from this file.
    - **Two frontend sites checked and left alone, not bugs**: `V1StockDetails.tsx:75` and `SlideOutDrawer.tsx:51` both compute `stockMapping?.mcsymbol || symbol` and pass it as a `scId` prop to `MCStockInfoPanel` — but that component's `scId` prop is declared in its TS interface and never actually read anywhere in its body (confirmed via grep: the only match for `scId` in the whole file is the interface line). Dead prop, not a live fetch bug — `MCStockInfoPanel` resolves everything itself via `symbol` through the now-fixed tRPC procedures. Left as-is rather than "fixing" working code for a cosmetic dead-prop cleanup.
    - **Checked other providers for the same falsy-empty-string trap** (`tlid`/`tlname`/`companyid` — Trendlyne/ET's opaque IDs) — only 2 other occurrences exist (`liveStockData.ts:596-598`), and both go the *correct* direction (`dbMapping.tlid || undefined`, converting an empty string to `undefined` for downstream consumers rather than using it as a fallback value) — not a bug.
    - **Live reachability swept across every core upstream provider this app depends on** — MoneyControl (`api.moneycontrol.com` SWOT/consolidated *and* `priceapi.moneycontrol.com/techCharts` OHLCV), NSE bhavcopy archives, Yahoo Finance chart API, ET Stats mobile, investsights FII/DII — all confirmed **200 with real, correctly-shaped JSON/CSV** when queried with the exact headers/params the real fetcher code sends. **First-pass naive curl (no `User-Agent`, guessed query params) falsely reported 4 of these 5 as broken (403/404/429/400)** — matching each fetcher's actual request shape (e.g. `techCharts` needs `resolution=15&countback=N&currencyCode=INR`, not a guessed `resolution=1D`; ET Stats needs `companyId=<real id>&events=Ratio&last=N&bType=all`, not invented param names) flipped every one of them to 200. **Standing lesson: a bare curl without matching a fetcher's real headers/params is not a reachability test — it reproduces the exact false-negative failure mode this codebase's `live_datasource` pytest convention exists to prevent** (see "Adding a New Data Source" above). Trendlyne (`smartoptions.trendlyne.com` and the wider Trendlyne surface) requires session auth (`fetchTrendlyneWithAuth`) and NiftyTrader requires a DB-stored rotating Bearer token (`app_settings.niftytrader_auth_token`, loaded by `getNiftyTraderHeaders()` in `niftytraderService.ts`) — neither is verifiable via a stateless curl from this sandbox (no live session/DB), but both are pre-existing, deliberate, already-documented mechanisms (the NiftyTrader path already logs an explicit warning and degrades gracefully when the token is empty, rather than silently guessing) — not a new finding, and out of scope to "fix" here since they need live credentials this sandbox doesn't have.
