  - **Round 3 ("continue")**: picked up the one item flagged as deliberately deferred (extracting App.tsx's inline sub-components into lazy-loaded files). Before touching it, re-audited the remaining `React.FC` declarations still inline in App.tsx and found 3 more genuinely **dead** ones with zero references anywhere (`IndexBar`, `SignalHistoryModal`, `MarketMap` component + its unused `extractIndexId` helper) — leftovers from the earlier `Dashboard` deletion (`SignalHistoryModal`'s only caller was the dead `Dashboard`) and from the `/market-map` route having been reimplemented as inline JSX (`MarketHeatmapWidget` + `SectorPerformance` + `SectorHeatmap`) without the old `MarketMap` component ever being removed. Deleted all 3 (871 net lines off App.tsx). **Verified honestly, not assumed**: rebuilt and the main chunk barely moved (1,514.17KB → 1,514.17KB, gzip 380.10→380.09KB) — confirms Rollup's tree-shaking was already eliminating this dead code from the bundle before the manual deletion, so the value here is source-hygiene/maintainability, not bundle size. Then hit a genuine decision point on the *live* (non-dead) inline components (`StockDetails`, `OptionChain`, `TechnicalAnalysis`, `FundamentalInsights`, `MFAnalysis`, `FnOSignals`, `NewsTab`, `Backtest`, `Screener`) — these are the real remaining lever for cutting first-load bytes, but unlike every fix so far they're not mechanical: converting them to `React.lazy()` requires a `<Suspense>` boundary at each of their (nested, non-route) usage sites, since the existing `SafeRoutes`/`PageFallback` Suspense wrapper only covers top-level `<Route>` elements, not components nested inside other components (e.g. inside `SlideOutDrawer`). That's a genuine, user-visible loading-behavior change (brief spinners where none exist today) across the app's core "click a stock" flow, and this sandbox has no live browser to catch a visual regression. **Deliberately stopped here rather than push it through unverified** — flagged to the user explicitly rather than silently completing or silently dropping it.
- **Full data audit + a new bug class found and closed across 11 files: midnight-crossing write-target dates (2026-08-01).** User asked for a complete data audit against all 4 prior audit docs plus a daily data-integrity cron. Two research agents reconciled the audit backlog and swept for recurring bug-class instances; findings led to hands-on fixes, not just a report. Full suite after all changes: **1,056 Python passed / 81 deselected (`live_datasource`) / 0 failed**, `tsc --noEmit` clean, TS job/pipeline suites **69 passed**.
  - **Audit-doc reconciliation**: most previously-"open" items across the 4 audit docs were already closed (docs were stale) — NSE bhavcopy weekend backfill, `screener_membership_snapshot` growth, Finding #31 shrinkage wiring, several `FULL_STACK_AUDIT.md` items (#113 `live_screener_ml_ranker.py` gap, #114 dead tables, #115 `job-digest` monitoring, #116 `company-profiles-sync` scheduling) all confirmed live-fixed. Genuinely still open, left deliberately: `adjustment_basis`/`screener_appearances` bitemporality (both documented won't-fix), `live_datasource` coverage (~20%, an ongoing program not a bug), 3 unbuilt endpoint-corpus fetchers (superstar investors, event calendar, the `fno/market/filter` options surface beyond what `so_option_chain_fetcher.py` already covers) — feature gaps, not integrity bugs.
  - **THE finding, caught by a spot-check the reconciliation agent did on its own initiative (not something I asked it to check): the 2026-08-01 "Second backlog sweep" entry above (`insider_buy_pct_90d` → date_iso fix, "151 symbols") never actually reached production.** Live on 2026-08-01: `technical_signals` for 2026-07-31 still showed **4 of 2,187 rows populated — the exact pre-fix symptom**, identical to the four example symbols that fix note itself cited. The fix's own verification only called `compute_insider_features()` directly and confirmed it returns real data in isolation; it never confirmed the *scheduled run's* `UPDATE ... WHERE date=?` actually matched a row in the live table. **Root cause: `ml-daily-ops`'s step chain has grown long enough that it now regularly finishes AFTER MIDNIGHT IST** — confirmed via `job_heartbeat`: a run processing the "2026-07-31" trading day completed at **2026-08-01 01:23 IST**. `insider_features.py` used `datetime.date.today()` as the exact-match write target (`WHERE symbol=? AND date=?`); once the script executes past midnight, `today` resolves to the *next* calendar day, which has no `technical_signals` grid row yet (a trading day's row only exists once created during *that day's own* market-hours scan cycle) — so the UPDATE silently matches **zero rows, every time, no error**. `bse_event_classifier.py`'s `run_daily()` had the identical pattern, deliberately documented as a fix (2026-07-19, replacing a worse `MAX(date)` bug) that itself became wrong once the schedule changed underneath it.
  - **Fix: `as_of.logical_trading_date(cutoff_hour=4, now=None)`** — before 4am local time, "today" is yesterday (the trading day whose EOD processing is still finishing); at/after it, the real calendar date. Deliberately **not** `MAX(date) FROM technical_signals` — that would reopen the exact bug the `date=?` guard was written to prevent (silently writing into an even-older stale row if a script runs before today's grid exists for an unrelated reason, e.g. a manual daytime backfill). A fixed early-morning cutoff fixes the midnight-crossing case without reopening that one.
  - **A follow-up systematic sweep (32-file grep → ~30 candidates with an exact-match `date=?` UPDATE into a daily-grid table) found the SAME defect in 8 more files, all fixed the same way**: `insider_transactions_fetcher.py`, `screener_features_fetcher.py` (2 sites — the largest single engine weight in `unified_ranker`'s regime blend), `credit_rating_fetcher.py`, `mc_earnings_fetcher.py` (4 separate functions), `analyst_revision.py`, `avwap_features.py`, `oi_delta_features.py`, `iv_features.py`. Plus 2 more fixed defensively though **not currently reachable in production** (`relative_strength.py`, `ownership_relative.py` — the vulnerable `--date today` branch exists and is each script's own documented usage pattern, but `queues.ts` currently calls both with no args) — closed anyway since it's a one-line change removing a landmine for whenever someone adds that flag. ~20 files were checked and correctly left alone: some anchor to `MAX(date)` from source data already (`eps_surprise_fetcher.py`, `mc_broker_reco_fetcher.py`, `pead_model.py`), some write per-row `signal_date`/`scan_date` from a dataframe not a wall-clock scalar (`ml_ensemble.py`, `online_learner.py`, `cs_ranker.py`, `movement_predictor.py`, `ml_signal_scorer.py`, `ml_calibration.py`, `breakout_classifier.py`), some are plain `INSERT...ON CONFLICT` upserts which self-heal regardless of which date resolves (`pcr_fetcher.py`, `reward_engine.py`, `nifty_pe_fetcher.py`, `mc_index_oi_fetcher.py`), and some only ever run inside a market-hours-gated window that structurally cannot cross midnight (`preopen_fetcher.py`, `market_regime_fetcher.py`, `intraday_regime.py`). **Standing lesson for any new post-close script: `date.today()`/`datetime.now()` used as an exact-match write target is not safe by inspection alone — check what job chain invokes it and whether that chain's own runtime has grown past the point where "today" can still mean the trading day whose row exists.**
  - **The `date_iso` gap's real root cause, fixed at the source**: `insider_features.py` reads `insider_trades` via `date_iso`, but the table's **dominant, currently-scheduled writer is `moneycontrol_fetcher.py`** (not `insider_transactions_fetcher.py`, which writes a *different* table, `insider_transactions` — easy to confuse, don't) — and it never populated `date_iso` on insert at all, so every row it wrote after the 2026-07-30 one-time backfill was invisible to the read-side fix regardless of the date-anchor bug above. Fixed by reusing `data_integrity_repair.py`'s own `parse_loose_date()` at insert time (not reimplementing it) so both the strptime-success path (MC's `%d-%b-%y` format, already ISO by the time it's inserted) and the raw-fallback path (when MC serves an unexpected format) still get a real `date_iso`. Backfilled the 1,213 rows written since the last repair run (now 46,275/46,275 = 100% coverage) and manually re-ran the fixed pipeline for 2026-07-31 (4 → 160 of 2,187 rows populated).
  - **Three more dormant-but-structurally-live bugs found and fixed, all matching failure classes this codebase has been burned by before**: (1) `unified_ranker.py`'s position-sizing leg (`bet_size_from_probability`, `_get_win_probabilities`) had no `isfinite` guard on `win_probability`/`calibrated_win_probability` — a NaN value doesn't get caught (fails `p <= neutral`, since every NaN comparison is False) and falls through to `min(1.0, nan)`, which Python's first-argument-wins NaN comparison resolves to **1.0, the MAXIMUM bet size** — same failure shape as the 2026-07-31 `dl_score`-into-fake-'Buy' incident, just in the sizing leg instead of the ranking leg. 0 NaN in production today (dormant), fixed defensively. (2) `intraday_ranker.py`'s component blend had the same `is not None`-not-`isfinite` gap; currently benign by accident (`max(0.0, nan)` happens to keep 0.0 under the same first-argument-wins quirk, so it silently zeroes rather than fakes a Buy) but fixed to match the explicit guard used everywhere else. (3) **`regime_detector.py`'s `train_hmm()` had NO promotion gate at all** — genuinely new, never previously audited across 5 prior full-stack passes — unconditionally overwriting `hmm_regime.pkl` (which drives `REGIME_WEIGHTS`, the largest blend-switch in the whole ranker) with no held-out comparison, unlike every sibling ML file in this codebase. Added a champion/challenger gate scoring the trailing 60 days under both the active and freshly-retrained model (`_log_likelihood_per_sample`, with an explicit Jacobian correction — **two GaussianHMMs fit with different StandardScalers are not comparable via raw `model.score()`**, since each scaler's own fitted std shifts the density by a constant the naive comparison would otherwise attribute to fit quality); a regression is written to `hmm_regime.pkl.candidate` instead of overwriting the live model. `--force` skips the gate for a deliberate first train or relabeling.
  - **Added the daily data-integrity cron job requested**: `data-quality-daily` (`10 3 * * *` = 8:40 AM IST, every day — a formal `JOB_REGISTRY`-registered, heartbeat-monitored wrapper around the existing 25-check suite in `dataQualityChecks.ts`). This does **not** replace the pre-existing 15-min `setInterval` poll in `jobWatchdog.ts` (still the real-time alerting path) — it exists because that poll itself was never a monitored cron job, so a silently-dead Node process would take data-quality monitoring down with it and nothing would notice (the exact class of gap `job-digest` had before *it* was registered). Sends a pass/warn/fail Telegram summary every day regardless of outcome, and fails the job itself (visible as "late" in the digest, not just a once-daily message) if any critical check is failing. Live-verified end-to-end: 23/25 pass, 0 critical failures, 2 pre-existing non-critical warns (`insider_trades`/`bulk_deals` recency — sparse data, by design).
  - **Verification discipline**: every production-data claim was checked against the live Postgres DB before and after the fix (not just unit tests) — `date_iso` coverage, `insider_buy_pct_90d` population counts, `job_heartbeat` timestamps converted to IST to prove the midnight-crossing. The 8-file systematic sweep was delegated to a background agent, then independently spot-checked (diffs read directly, NOT-VULNERABLE verdicts re-verified against `queues.ts` invocation sites, full suite re-run independently rather than trusting the agent's own reported pass count) before being accepted — this is the discipline the project's own `live_datasource`-test mandate exists to enforce, applied to agent-produced fixes too.
- **`dl-trainer` (LSTM weekly retrain) was a live incident, not just an under-budgeted timeout — found via a routine weekend-job-status check (2026-08-01/02).** `dl-trainer`'s last recorded failure was a 6h timeout on 2026-07-27, but investigating it surfaced something worse and still active: `dl_model_config.json` pointed at `lstm_version: 19`, and **`lstm_v19.pt` did not exist on disk at all**. Root cause: `dl_trainer.py`'s `retrain_models()` reimplemented its own quality gate instead of calling `dl_engine.py`'s already-correct `_promote_lstm_version()` (built for a different call path — `dl_engine.py --mode train` CLI usage — and never wired into the actual scheduled orchestrator). That gate treated **NaN acc/roc_auc as "insufficient data, skip the check, promote anyway"** — backwards, since `train_lstm()` sets `metrics["error"]` specifically when the weights themselves diverged and correctly refuses to even `torch.save()` the checkpoint (the 2026-07-31 NaN-LSTM fix), but this gate never looked at that key. Net effect: `dl-engine-infer` had been running "successfully" (exit 0) while **silently writing zero predictions every day**, indistinguishable from a healthy day in every job-monitoring signal. Checked every checkpoint's actual weights directly (not metadata): **only v2 and v3 (mid-June) are clean — every version from v4 through v18 is ~100% NaN**, and `model_registry` had **17 of 18 BiLSTM rows simultaneously `is_active=1`** forever, since the old gate never deactivated a prior row either way.
  - **Immediate repair**: rolled `dl_model_config.json` back to v3 (backup of the broken config kept alongside, matching `_promote_lstm_version`'s own timestamped-backup convention) and repaired `model_registry` so only v3 is active. Seeded `dl_model_config.json`'s `lstm_metrics` with v3's real baseline (`roc_auc: 0.58`) so the next real training run has an actual bar to clear instead of auto-promoting via the "no prior baseline" escape hatch.
  - **Fixed the gate bypass**: `dl_trainer.py` now calls `dl._promote_lstm_version()` (the one gate for this model, mirroring `ml_ensemble.py`/`cs_ranker.py`'s promotion pattern) instead of its own logic; the model_registry write was extracted into `_record_model_registry()` (con, version, auc, acc, promoted) — a small, DB-mockable unit mirroring `cs_ranker.py`'s `_register_cs_model()` / `test_cs_ranker_promotion_gate.py` pattern exactly, since it's the identical Finding #17 bug class (unconditional promotion + never-deactivated prior row) in a file that bug class hadn't reached yet.
  - **Fixed a second silent-failure mode found while patching this**: `run_inference()`'s "model file missing" and "every prediction came back non-finite" branches used to `print()` and quietly `return`/`sys.exit(1)`. Both are now a raised `RuntimeError` instead — **`sys.exit` cannot be used here** because `python_api.py`'s `/api/infer-dl` FastAPI endpoint also calls `dl_engine.run_inference()` in-process; `SystemExit` is a `BaseException` the endpoint's `except Exception` does not catch, so `sys.exit(1)` there would have taken down the whole uvicorn worker instead of just failing that one request. A raised exception fails loudly on both call paths correctly (CLI: uncaught → non-zero exit; FastAPI: caught → HTTP 500).
  - **Found and fixed the likely root cause of the divergence itself, then validated it**: no gradient clipping anywhere in `_train_one_fold()`, and no non-finite guard on features loaded from `feature_store` — a ratio-style feature (e.g. `dist_sma200_pct`, `vwap_dist_pct`) computed off a near-zero denominator or one of this codebase's documented bad OHLCV bars (see the 2026-07-30/31 bias-audit and `ohlcv_adjust.py` notes) can be ±inf, and feeding that straight into the LSTM detonates the first matmul and poisons every subsequent batch once the hidden state carries a NaN. Added `np.nan_to_num` sanitization in `load_symbol_sequences()`/`load_inference_sequence()`, a per-batch `torch.isfinite(loss)` skip, and `clip_grad_norm_(max_norm=1.0)` in `_train_one_fold()` — with the AMP-specific gotcha that `scaler.unscale_(opt)` **must** run before `clip_grad_norm_`, or clipping bounds the wrong (still fp16-scaled) magnitude and doesn't actually protect anything. **Live-validated, not just unit-tested**: a real 25-symbol/30-epoch training run (7,448 sequences) that would previously diverge now finishes with **0 of 2,299,209 parameters non-finite**.
  - **The 6h timeout was genuinely too small, and by a lot — measured, not guessed**: that same 25-symbol validation run took **870s (14.5 min)** on this machine's shared 8GB laptop GPU (RTX 5050) under typical contention (Ollama + other jobs — `nvidia-smi` showed 85% utilization, 5.6/8.1GB used, 10 concurrent `python.exe` processes at the time). Extrapolated to the full ~2,100-symbol universe plus `walk_forward_validate()`'s own several-model-copy retraining, a full run plausibly spans hours to the better part of a day depending on contention. **User chose to bump the timeout generously rather than reduce training cost.** `queues.ts`: `dl-retrain-weekly`/`dl-retrain-emergency`'s `lockDuration` and an explicit `processDLPython(...)` timeout override raised 6h→24h (the shared `processDLPython` *default* of 6h is untouched, since `feature_engineering.py`/`dl_engine.py --mode infer`/`backfill_ohlcv.py` all rely on it staying short). `dl_trainer.py`: the stale-lock threshold that lets a second trainer start when the first is presumed dead was raised from 7200s (2h) to 25h (`STALE_LOCK_SECONDS`, named and commented so it's obviously coupled to the BullMQ timeout) — at the old 2h threshold, a real 20-hour run would have looked "stale" partway through and let a second concurrent trainer start, both writing `lstm_v{N}.pt`/`dl_model_config.json` at once.
  - **Tests**: 11 new (`test_dl_trainer_registry_gate.py` — `_record_model_registry`'s promote/reject/scoping behavior, the gate-wiring check via source inspection, and a `STALE_LOCK_SECONDS > 24h` invariant test), all passing alongside the existing 28 DL-related tests. Full suite after all changes: **969 passed, 81 skipped, 0 failed**; `tsc --noEmit` clean.
  - **Still open, deliberately not attempted this session**: the LSTM has not actually been *retrained* yet (only rolled back to the last known-good v3, from mid-June — 6+ weeks stale) — the fix makes the NEXT scheduled/emergency retrain trustworthy, it doesn't itself produce a fresh model. `~2400 historical all-NaN rows in `deep_learning_predictions` (v4-v18 era) were left as-is (harmless now that the ranker/inference path sanitizes non-finite values, per the 2026-07-31 fix).
- **Telegram job-health digest audit — 6 real stale/missing `cronPatterns` mirrors in `monitorScripts.ts`, all producing false Telegram alerts (2026-08-03).** User asked to review the Telegram job-status reports and confirm all jobs are running fine. No live server/DB/Telegram in this sandbox, so this was a source-level cross-check: every `JOB_REGISTRY` (`jobRegistry.ts`) and `MONITOR_SCRIPTS` (`monitorScripts.ts`) cron mirror was diffed against its real schedule in `queues.ts` + the `src/server/jobs/*.jobs.ts` modules the 2026-08 decomposition split it into (a prior session's "every cronPattern mirror updated in lockstep" claim from 2026-07-31 turned out incomplete). Found and fixed 6 real gaps in `monitorScripts.ts`, all previously undetected because `jobPipelineOrdering.test.ts` only checks `JOB_REGISTRY`/source-cron collisions, never `MONITOR_SCRIPTS`'s `cronPatterns` mirrors:
  - `feature-engineering` and `trendlyne-midweek`: both still carried their **pre-2026-07-31-destagger** cron values (`0 10 * * 1-5` / `30 12 * * 2`) after the real jobs (`dl-feature-daily`, `trendlyne-midweek-batch`) moved to `30 11 * * 1-5` / `30 14 * * 2` that same day — the destagger session's own "6 jobs + dl-engine-infer + screener-performance" mirror-update list simply missed these two. `feature-engineering` is `critical: true`, so this was firing a real false "stale" Telegram alert every single weekday between the old and new deadlines.
  - `regime-detector` and `performance-tracker`: both had **no `cronPatterns` at all** despite being driven by Mon-Fri-only jobs (`dl-regime-daily`, and `performance_tracker.py` inside `ml-daily-ops`) — the flat `staleLimitHours: 26` fallback false-flagged 'stale' (both `critical: true`, real alerts) every Saturday/weekend.
  - `technical-scan`: same gap, but with a self-contradicting comment already in place — it explicitly reasons that a flat threshold would "false-alarm every single evening/weekend" and sets `staleLimitHours: 26` to fix it, but the underlying job (`*/30 3-10 * * 1-5`) is Mon-Fri only, so the real Friday→Monday gap is **~64.5h, not ~26h** — this was false-flagging 'stale' for ~38 continuous hours every weekend on one of the platform's most central jobs (2000+ stock technical scan), `critical: true`.
  - `financial-ratios` / `working-capital`: exhaustively computed (not eyeballed) the true worst-case gap between consecutive "first Sunday of month" occurrences across 15 years — **35 days = 840h**, next occurrence 2026-11-01→2026-12-06 — which exceeds the existing `staleLimitHours: 800`. Bumped to 900h. Both `critical: false` (digest-only, no real-time alert), so lower severity than the four above.
  - Also corrected `company-profiles-sync`'s display-only `schedule: 'Bi-weekly Sunday'` label (cosmetic — not used in any lateness computation) — the job is actually daily, all 7 days, sharding the universe by day-of-year (already correctly documented elsewhere in this file's 2026-07-31 job-destagger note; the label here had never been updated to match).
  - **Verification**: fixed values checked against the literal `repeat: { pattern: ... }` lines in `queues.ts`/`jobs/*.jobs.ts` (not against other comments, which is exactly the class of drift this catches), `computeCronLateness()`'s actual semantics in `jobHeartbeat.ts` traced by hand for each case to confirm the false-positive window rather than assumed, `npm install` run fresh in this sandbox to get a working `tsc`/`vitest` (had neither at session start), full suite **251 passed** (only the 28 pre-existing, network-dependent `mcapiProxy.test.ts` failures, unrelated — this sandbox's proxy returns 403/timeout against the real MoneyControl API, already documented as a known non-blocker), `tsc --noEmit` clean except the one pre-existing `MCStockInfoPanel.tsx` error already on `main`.
  - **Lesson, extending the already-documented pattern**: this file's own history already tracks the "stale cronPattern mirror" bug recurring across `JOB_REGISTRY` entries and MONITOR_SCRIPTS entries lacking `cronPatterns` — this session shows the fix pattern itself (add a `cronPatterns` mirror) can drift the same way its target did, and that a mirror update done "in lockstep" during a scheduling change can still miss entries, especially ones with self-confident comments (`technical-scan`) that never got re-derived numerically. **Any time a job's cron pattern changes, grep for every OTHER file that might independently encode that same schedule (`JOB_REGISTRY`, `MONITOR_SCRIPTS`, any test asserting ordering) — a comment claiming "matches the convention" is not evidence, recompute the actual gap.** Did not build an automated mirror-consistency test for this in this session (deliberately out of scope — the task was a status review, not a refactor) — worth a follow-up: extend `jobPipelineOrdering.test.ts` (or a sibling) to parse `MONITOR_SCRIPTS.cronPatterns` and assert each pattern string literally appears in the source file its job comes from, the same way it already does for `JOB_REGISTRY.cronPattern`.
- **AI-signal Ollama gate was a near-no-op — degenerate calibration, not a bug (2026-08-02).** User asked whether the `ai-signals` job for 20MICRONS was adding value and to cut unnecessary Ollama usage if not. Job logs said "completed successfully" (it did — persisted `BUY, confidence 41`), but that says nothing about value. Checked the live universe: **2,155 of 2,264 symbols (95.2%) shared the exact same `calibrated_win_probability`, 0.40640821640387675** — just above the `processAISignal` gate floor (`app_settings.ai_signal_min_win_prob`, default 0.40) — so the gate was passing 2,169/2,264 stocks (95.8%) daily, i.e. calling Ollama against essentially the whole universe rather than a quant-endorsed shortlist. 20MICRONS itself: raw `win_probability` ranged 0.18–0.71 over 5 days but calibrated to the identical 0.4064 on 4 of them. **Root cause is not a calibration bug**: `ml_calibration.py`'s own docstring already documents the base ensemble's `win_probability` is non-monotonic outside BEAR regime (0.65–0.85 raw predictions win LESS than lower ones) — consistent with the already-known live-AUC≈0.50-outside-BEAR finding from the 2026-07-23 `regime_edge_status` work. Isotonic regression is correctly pooling a genuinely non-monotonic range into one flat value; it's exposing the base model's lack of real differentiation for ~95% of the universe on a given day, not causing it — retraining/refitting the calibrator would not fix this. **Measured consequence**: AI-sourced `unified_signals` 30-day decisive win rate is **~2.0%** (134 WIN vs 6,525 STOP_LOSS + 66 LOSS of 6,725 decisive outcomes) — the same dead level as pre-[[llm-demotion-and-retrain-2026-07-12]] (2.3%); the July 12 demotion fixed *what* gets written but not the gate's near-total lack of selectivity. **Fix (user-approved)**: raised `app_settings.ai_signal_min_win_prob` 0.40 → 0.42 via a direct row upsert — single consumer (`queues.ts:335`, 5-min cache TTL), no code/restart needed — clearing the 0.4064 plateau with margin while keeping the gate open for the 14/2,264 (0.6%) stocks/day with genuinely differentiated calibrated scores. Live-verified projection: ~2,169/day candidates → ~14/day, a ~99% cut in unnecessary Ollama calls. **Deliberately not done**: didn't touch `ml_calibration.py`, didn't disable the AI-signal pipeline outright (the residual 0.6% + their `ai_reasoning` narrative may still carry value), didn't wire the existing (flag-gated-off) `edge_adjusted_probability()` regime-shrinkage mechanism into this specific gate (it already covers `ml_ensemble`/`scoring_engine`/`unified_ranker` sizing but never this Ollama gate — worth a follow-up). **Lesson**: a job's "completed successfully" log line is not evidence the signal it wrote had value — check gate selectivity (fraction of universe actually filtered) and resolved win rate before trusting job-success as a usefulness proxy; a gate reading a *calibrated* column can silently degrade to a near-no-op when the underlying model's differentiation collapses in a regime, and the calibration behaving correctly is exactly what hides this from a naive check.
- **Weekend job-status review (2026-08-03): 3 real fixes, 1 initially-flagged "critical gap" that turned out to be a measurement artifact — worth recording so it isn't re-flagged.** User asked to confirm all weekend jobs (Sat Aug 1 / Sun Aug 2) completed successfully. Cross-checked `getLateJobs()` (JOB_REGISTRY, cron-aware) AND `getSystemStatus()` (MONITOR_SCRIPTS, DB-freshness-aware) against ground-truth queries on the live tables -- the two checkers disagree with each other by design (different registries), so neither alone is sufficient; both had real gaps.
  - **`nse-bhavcopy-fetcher` (critical) had zero heartbeat instrumentation** -- `queues.ts` called it via bare `runPython(...).catch(console.warn)` instead of `T.run('nse-bhavcopy-fetcher', ...)` like every sibling step in the same `ml-daily-ops` chain, so `job_heartbeat` never recorded a single run despite the fetcher genuinely working (`nse_universe_history` was correctly fresh through 2026-07-31). Fixed by wrapping it in `T.run(...)` to match the established pattern.
  - **`regime-detector` (critical) false-alarmed "stale" every weekend** -- its `monitorScripts.ts` entry was missing `cronPatterns` (unlike its sibling `dl-engine-infer`, registered the same way via `dl.jobs.ts`), so it fell back to the flat 26h `staleLimitHours` check, which trips every Sat/Sun regardless of Friday's actual success. `market_regimes` was correctly fresh through Jul 31 (HIGH_VOL) the whole time. Added `cronPatterns: ['15 11 * * 1-5']` matching `registerDlJobs()`'s real registration.
  - **Initially flagged, then corrected: the 15-min intraday chain (market-regime-refresh -> intraday-breadth-capture -> intraday-ranker) looked like it had a critical live gap** -- `intraday_recommendations` showed only 2 executions all Friday instead of ~25, and `intraday-breadth-capture`'s new write path had never once recorded a heartbeat since being wired in. **This is not a live bug**: `git show` on the commit that added the new breadth-capture step (`d8e8c5d`) timestamps it **2026-07-31 17:35:18 IST -- after that day's 15:30 IST market close**. The code correctly has zero data since deploy because zero trading-hours minutes have elapsed since then (Sat/Sun are non-trading); the next scheduled fire was confirmed live in Redis (`bull:market-regime-refresh:repeat`) for Monday 08:30 IST, right on schedule. **Lesson: before treating "zero activity since visible in the data" as a bug, check when the instrumenting commit actually landed relative to the market session -- a fix deployed after close on a Friday will correctly show nothing until the next session, and checking again pre-market-open on the very next trading day will look identical to a real outage.**
  - **Real, currently-active bug: `exit_policy.py --train` timed out on its 20-min budget on all 3 attempts over the weekend** (Sat 13:59, Sat 19:16, and Mon 07:31 IST -- the exit-policy MFE/MAE regressor has not retrained successfully this cycle). Traced via BullMQ/Redis job history (`bull:ml-weekly-retrain:completed`) to **two overlapping `ml-weekly-retrain` catchup runs from two separate server restarts ~30 min apart**, both queued because `addJobWithCatchup()`'s "was this missed?" check only looks at `getJobs(['completed','failed'])` -- a still-running catchup from an earlier restart is invisible to a later restart's check, so both concluded "missed" and each queued its own full retrain, doubling Python-subprocess contention. Fixed generically in `src/server/jobs/registerJob.ts`: before queuing a catchup, check `getJobs(['active','waiting','delayed'])` for an existing job with the same name and skip if found. This guard applies to every queue using this shared helper, not just ml-weekly-retrain. 3 new tests in `src/server/__tests__/registerJob.test.ts` (missed+nothing-in-flight -> catchup queued; missed+already-in-flight -> skipped; not-missed -> no catchup logic touched).

- **Data-integrity report cleanup + the trendlyne-midweek gap closed (2026-08-03), continuation of the same-day weekend job-status review above.** User pasted the actual `data-quality-daily` Telegram digest (5 warnings on a Monday morning: OHLCV/fii_dii_flow/market_regimes freshness "3+ days old", insider_trades "276.4d old", bulk_deals "76.1d old") and asked to resolve all of it, plus fix the pre-existing `MCStockInfoPanel.tsx` typecheck error. All 5 warnings investigated against live data — 3 were the exact same weekend-blind-threshold false-positive class documented repeatedly elsewhere in this file, and 2 were genuine bugs this check happened to be the first thing to surface.
  - **OHLCV/fii_dii_flow/market_regimes "3+ days stale" were false alarms**: `data-quality-daily` runs at 08:40 IST, hours before that day's own stock-refresh (16:00)/fii-dii-fetcher (19:30, inside ml-daily-ops)/regime-detector (16:45) jobs fire — so on any Monday, the most recent row is still Friday's, a real ~1.1-day trading-day lag inflated to ~3.1-3.4 raw calendar days by the Sat/Sun gap. `dataQualityChecks.ts`'s own header explicitly declines an NSE-holiday-calendar dependency "for simplicity," but a plain Sat/Sun subtraction is cheap and removes the whole false-positive class. Added `tradingDaysStale()` (subtracts weekend calendar days between the row's date and now, floored at 0) and wired it into `ohlcv-freshness-coverage`/`fii-dii-flow-freshness`/`market-regimes-freshness`. A genuine multi-weekday outage still fails/warns undiminished (verified with a "one week later" test case).
  - **`insider-trades-recency` was reading the wrong column — a real bug, not a false alarm.** `insider_trades.date` is NSE's raw display text ("22 May, 2026"), not lexically sortable; `MAX(date)` returned `"31 Oct, 2025"` (alphabetically late month name) while the real latest row was `2026-07-31` — a 276-day false reading. `date_iso` (added in the 2026-08-01 midnight-crossing session, now at 100% coverage and populated at insert time) is the actually-sortable column. Fixed the check's SQL to `MAX(date_iso)`.

The live equivalent is `block_deals`, fed daily by `tickertape_deals_fetcher.py` (wired into ml-daily-ops since 2026-07-31, carries `pctTransacted`) and additively shared with `block_deal_fetcher.py`'s NSE rows. Repointed the check's SQL at `block_deals` (id kept stable; label/detail wording updated). Left the dead `bulk_deals` table itself alone (its `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` in `db.ts`/`moneycontrol_fetcher.py` is harmless dead weight, not a bug) — dropping a live-DB table is a bigger, separate decision than fixing a monitoring check, flagged rather than done unprompted.

Live-probed the endpoint directly today and it returns full, correct data (`head.status="0"`) — the incident was transient (most likely a temporary Trendlyne anti-bot block against the plain, unauthenticated `requests.Session()` this fetcher uses) and had already self-resolved, so it couldn't be reproduced. Rather than chase an unreproducible one-off, hardened both fetchers with the resilience machinery this codebase already built for exactly this class of problem (`fetch_utils.py`'s `retry_get`/`FetchTracker`, rolled out to 15 other fetchers 2026-07-30 but missed by these two): `_fetch()`/`fetch_adv_tech()` now retry through `retry_get` instead of a single-attempt `session.get()`, and `main()` in both files now tracks every symbol through a `FetchTracker` that exits non-zero once the failure rate crosses 15% — so a repeat of "100% no data" fails the job loudly (visible in `job_heartbeat`/error logs) instead of logging a misleadingly clean "execution completed" with zero rows written.

- **Data-quality coverage expanded from ~25 to 62 checks + a standing mandate added (2026-08-03), same-day continuation.** User asked to "add all live datasources" to `dataQualityChecks.ts` and make it mandatory that any future datasource gets a check too. Swept every `runPython()` call site across `queues.ts` + `jobs/*.jobs.ts` (~120 distinct scripts), cross-referenced each script's own `INSERT INTO`/`CREATE TABLE` targets against the file's existing coverage, and found the ~25 hand-written checks covered a small fraction of the platform's external-data landing tables — most fetchers had zero freshness monitoring at all.
  - **Built a declarative factory instead of hand-writing ~35 more bespoke checks**: `TABLE_FRESHNESS_CHECKS` (a `{id, label, category, critical, table, dateColumn, nativeDateColumn?, tradingDayAware?, warnDays, failDays?}` config array) + `makeFreshnessCheck()`, which generates the SQL + `evaluate()` for each entry. This directly serves the "make it a mandate" half of the ask — adding a check for a new datasource is now a one-line config object, not a SQL+evaluate() block to author and get right every time. `tradingDayAware` defaults to true (uses the same-day's `tradingDaysStale()` fix); explicitly set `false` for genuinely 24/7-cadence tables. Omitting `failDays` reproduces the existing "sparse by nature" soft-warn-only style (`insider-trades-recency`/`bulk-deals-recency`) for naturally episodic data.

Fixed by only keeping rows whose symbol matches an existing `nse_stocks` ticker — BSE-only holdings are silently dropped, not corrupted, an accepted coverage tradeoff. Wired into `queues.ts` right after `extra_endpoints_fetcher.py` (plain `runPython(...).catch(...)`, matching that whole block's existing style — no `T.run()`/StepTracker wrapping there). Live-verified end-to-end against the real DB: 15-investor sample → 374 stored / 434 skipped (expected, given the BSE-code mixing).
  - **`url_explorer`'s own `store.py` had a real bug, found by actually running it**: its DDL used bare SQLite `AUTOINCREMENT`, which is invalid Postgres syntax (`db_compat.translate()` rewrites query syntax, not DDL type syntax) — crashed the first live fetch/profile pass with `psycopg2.errors.SyntaxError`. Fixed by branching on `sql_translate.use_postgres()`, mirroring `moneycontrol_fetcher.py`'s `ensure_tables_exist()` — same "SQLite-schema-of-record assumed dialect-portable without ever running it against Postgres" lesson this file has recorded several times before (the `date('now')` incidents).

  - `src/server/__tests__/monitorScriptsCronMirror.test.ts` (29 tests): every `MONITOR_SCRIPTS.cronPatterns` string must exist as a real `repeat: { pattern: ... }` registration somewhere in `queues.ts`/`src/server/jobs/*.jobs.ts` (general sweep), plus pinned regressions tying `feature-engineering`/`trendlyne-midweek`/`regime-detector`/`dl-engine-infer`/`screener-performance`/`outcome-resolver-5d`/`-15d` to their specific driving job by name.

Fixed by checking every occurrence of the marker, searching only forward from each (verified true at every real call site), and taking the globally nearest (occurrence, repeat) pairing across all of them.
  - `src/server/__tests__/monitorScriptsStaleLimitConsistency.test.ts` (14 tests): extends the audit to `staleLimitHours` itself — monitor.router.ts's `getSystemStatus()` FALLBACK lateness check, used only when an entry has no `cronPatterns` (the rest are already covered by the cron-mirror test above). For each no-`cronPatterns` entry, locates its real driving job by the same marker technique and computes the job's true worst-case gap — via `cron-parser` for a literal cron cadence, or via an explicit first-Sunday-of-month calendar model for `working-capital`, whose real cadence (`trendlyneWeekly.jobs.ts`'s `isFirstSundayOfMonth` gate) is a conditional sub-cadence of a weekly cron, not expressible as a raw cron pattern — then asserts `staleLimitHours` clears it. **Found a real, live drift while building it**: `financial-ratios` still carried its pre-2026-07-31 `schedule: 'First Sunday of month'` label and `staleLimitHours: 900` after `financial_ratios_fetcher.py` was moved out of the first-Sunday gate that same day (`processTrendlyneRatiosMonthly` now runs it unconditionally every Sunday, only `working_capital_fetcher.py`/`mf_stock_holdings_fetcher.py` remain gated below it) — not a false-alarm risk (900h > the real 168h worst case), but a genuine staleness-detection blind spot: a real break in that fetcher wouldn't have been flagged for up to 900h instead of ~200h. Corrected to `schedule: 'Weekly Sunday'` / `staleLimitHours: 200`, matching its `ml-weekly-retrain`/`trendlyne-fundamentals` siblings' convention; `working-capital` itself is genuinely still monthly-gated and keeps its correct, now cross-verified 900h.
  - **Verification discipline held throughout**: every test negative-controlled by deliberately corrupting real entries (`et-marketstats-sync`/`dl-feature-refresh` cron values; `ohlcv-backfill`'s `staleLimitHours` below its real 168h worst case; `working-capital`'s below its real 840h worst case, specifically to prove the monthly-gate model is load-bearing and not silently equivalent to a plain-weekly read of the same cron) and confirming the exact expected failures before restoring. Full suite green (395+ passed across all three new files combined) except the pre-existing, unrelated `mcapiProxy.test.ts` network failures and a pre-existing `MCStockInfoPanel.tsx` typecheck error already on `main`.
- **Full-suite Postgres flakiness (the follow-up flagged above) root-caused and fixed same-day, 2026-08-03.** Not actually flaky — 100% deterministic given the ambient environment, and NOT caused by the mirror-consistency test files (confirmed by reproducing identically with those files removed). Root cause: `dbAsync.ts`'s `usePostgres()` reads `process.env.USE_POSTGRES` fresh on every `dbGet`/`dbAll`/`dbRun` call (`=== 'true'`, strict), and this sandbox has `USE_POSTGRES=true` baked into the process environment itself — confirmed via a fresh `bash -c` subshell (no `.env` file, no shell rc line; it's a real container/harness-level env var, not something a test could see coming from a dotfile). 10 test files (`backtestRunner`, `commandCenter`, `createSignal`, `getFiiDiiFlow`, `getRecentCloseSeries`, `getUnifiedScoreForSymbol`, `scoringService`, `signalReaders`, `unifiedSignalsKey`, `upsertUnifiedSignal`) exercise the real tRPC router (or call `dbRun`/`dbGet`/`dbAll` directly) against a real in-memory/file-based SQLite `db` they set up themselves, but never isolated `USE_POSTGRES` — so every DB call silently routed to a live Postgres pool that doesn't exist in this sandbox, failing on `ECONNREFUSED` regardless of what was actually being tested. **Confirmed empirically before touching any code**: `USE_POSTGRES=false npx vitest run <file>` made the failures disappear pre-fix; a full run under that override matched the known-clean baseline exactly (28 failures, all in `mcapiProxy.test.ts`, unrelated real-network calls). Fixed by adding `process.env.USE_POSTGRES = 'false';` to each file, ahead of any (even transitive) import of `dbAsync.ts` — 6 files already used a dynamic `await import('../db')` specifically to guarantee env vars land first (same pattern now reused, env line added alongside their existing `DATABASE_URL = ':memory:'`); the other 4 used a **static** `import { dbRun } from '../dbAsync'`, where a plain top-level env-var statement placed before it is NOT guaranteed to run first (static imports are hoisted ahead of a module's own top-level code) — converted to the same dynamic-import pattern rather than relying on an ordering guarantee that might not hold under a different bundler/transform config. **Verified, not assumed**: ran the full suite 3 times under the *unmodified* ambient environment (`USE_POSTGRES=true` still set at the shell level, no override) — consistently 407/435 passed, identical every time, only the pre-existing `mcapiProxy.test.ts` failures remain. `tsc --noEmit` clean except the one pre-existing, unrelated `MCStockInfoPanel.tsx` error already on `main`. **Lesson**: a test file that sets up its own isolated fixture (in-memory SQLite) but drives it through a real, shared routing layer (`dbAsync.ts`) is only as isolated as every env var that routing layer reads — `DATABASE_URL` alone was never sufficient, since `USE_POSTGRES` is a completely separate switch read by a different module (`pgConfig.ts`) with no connection to `DATABASE_URL` at all; a test that isolates one env var a shared facade reads must audit *every* env var that facade reads, not just the one it happens to remember exists.
- **Fourth mirror-consistency test, `JOB_REGISTRY.graceMinutes` (2026-08-03, same audit series).** `jobHeartbeat.ts`'s `getLateJobs()` waits `graceMinutes` past a cron's expected fire time before flagging a job 'late' — if `graceMinutes` is smaller than the job's own declared runtime (BullMQ `lockDuration`, or an explicit `withJobTimeout(...)` wrapper), the job gets flagged late while still running completely normally. `src/server/__tests__/jobRegistryGraceMinutesConsistency.test.ts` (59 tests) locates each entry's real driving job via the same marker-matching technique as `jobRegistryCronMirror.test.ts` and asserts `graceMinutes` covers it; the 17 `ml-daily-ops`/`ml-weekly-retrain` StepTracker sub-steps are checked against their parent's own (live-read) `graceMinutes` instead, per the already-documented "wider than the parent's grace" convention.
  - **Found real, live drift, three of it large.** `mc-screener-sync`: grace 60min vs a declared 90min lockDuration. `ml-weekly-retrain`: grace 180min vs a 6h lockDuration. `quant-eod-sync`: grace 45min vs a 2h lockDuration / **5.5h** `withJobTimeout` wrapper whose own comment records real measured runs of 153/157/239min — grace=45min was almost certainly false-flagging on every single run. `ml-daily-ops` (`critical: true`): grace 60min vs a 4h lockDuration / 3.5h `withJobTimeout` — and this file's own job-schedule-review note independently measured this job's real runtime at **~4.4h**, with a separate note recording a run completing at 01:23 IST off a 19:30 IST start (~5h53m), both exceeding even the source-declared 4h/3.5h ceiling. Being the platform's central, most complex ML pipeline job, running every weekday, a 60min grace was very likely producing a real "Job running late" Telegram alert most weekday evenings. Fixed each to its source-declared ceiling + a modest margin (`mc-screener-sync` 60→105, `quant-eod-sync` 45→360, `ml-daily-ops` 60→270, `ml-weekly-retrain` 180→390) — deliberately the mechanically source-derivable number, not stretched to match the less-precise CLAUDE.md anecdotal ~4.4h/5h53m figures for `ml-daily-ops`, which describe an evolving pipeline-growth concern already flagged elsewhere as unresolved and worth its own dedicated look, not folded into this fix. Cascaded to the sub-steps (`ml-daily-ops`'s 15 × 120→280min, `ml-weekly-retrain`'s 2 × 240→400min) to preserve the "wider than parent" relationship the fixed parents' new values would otherwise have broken.
  - **Building the extraction logic surfaced two more real, empirically-found bugs on top of the marker-matching lessons already recorded for the cron-pattern test**: (1) the lockDuration regex didn't handle this codebase's `60_000` JS numeric-separator style (only `60 * 1000`), silently dropping several real matches; (2) checking every occurrence of a marker and taking the globally nearest forward match (the technique that correctly handles cron patterns) picked up an *unrelated later job's* lockDuration for `closed-day-early-batch` — a `recordHeartbeat(...)` call reusing its name sat closer to the *next* queue's (`dl-retrain-emergency`'s) lockDuration than its own. Fixed by searching forward only from the marker's FIRST (registration) occurrence, since lockDuration always sits relative to a job's own registration in this codebase, never scattered oddly relative to a later reference call.
  - **Negative-controlled**: reverted `jobRegistry.ts` to its pre-fix state and confirmed the exact same 4 failures reproduce with the exact lockDuration/withJobTimeout numbers in the failure messages, then restored. Full suite: 466/494 passed (was 407/435 before this file), same pre-existing `mcapiProxy.test.ts` failures only, `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Fifth mirror-consistency test, `MONITOR_SCRIPTS.graceMinutes` (2026-08-03, same audit series).** `monitor.router.ts`'s `getSystemStatus()` uses `computeCronLateness(..., graceMinutes, ...)` for any entry with `cronPatterns` — the same "wait `graceMinutes` past the expected fire time" mechanism as `JOB_REGISTRY.graceMinutes`, just for the DB-freshness-check registry instead of `job_heartbeat`. `src/server/__tests__/monitorScriptsGraceMinutesConsistency.test.ts` (19 tests) locates each entry's real driving job(s) via the same marker technique and asserts `graceMinutes` covers the largest of them (`outcome-resolver-5d`/`-15d` are fed by two independent jobs via two `cronPatterns` and must cover whichever runs longer).
  - **Found a large, systematic drift**: 9 of the 16 `cronPatterns`-bearing entries are steps inside the `ml-daily-ops` chain (`cronPatterns: ['0 14 * * 1-5']`) — the exact job the `JOB_REGISTRY.graceMinutes` test above already found needed 270min, not 60 — but every one of these 9 `MONITOR_SCRIPTS` entries (`outcome-resolver-5d`, `outcome-resolver-15d`, `performance-tracker`, `fii-dii-fetcher`, `finbert-scorer`, `ml-ensemble-score`, `reward-engine`, `rl-agent-update`, `signal-type-stats`) was *also* still at `graceMinutes: 60`, completely independently: these are two separate registries tracking the same underlying jobs through different mechanisms (`job_heartbeat` vs DB-freshness), so fixing one never touched the other. Four of the nine are `critical: true`, meaning this was very likely producing real "Critical engine stale" Telegram alerts on top of the already-fixed JOB_REGISTRY "Job running late" ones for the *same* chain. Fixed all 9 to 280min, matching the corresponding JOB_REGISTRY sub-step fix for consistency between the two registries. The other 7 `cronPatterns`-bearing entries were already consistent; `intraday-breadth-capture` (setInterval-driven, no lockDuration exists) is explicitly exempted with its own reasoning documented inline.
  - **Negative-controlled**: reverted, confirmed the exact same 9 failures reproduce with the exact real-ceiling numbers, then restored. Full suite: 485/513 passed (was 466/494 before this file), same pre-existing failures only, `tsc --noEmit` clean.
- **Sixth audit: coverage completeness for JOB_REGISTRY's everyMs jobs (2026-08-03, same series).** User asked to extend the audit to whether `everyMs`-driven jobs (`news-sentiment`, `trendlyne-intraday`, `confluence-compute`) should also have a `MONITOR_SCRIPTS` DB-freshness entry, not just the `job_heartbeat` "did it run" signal already covered.
  - **Found a concrete structural gap for `confluence-compute`**: `confluence.jobs.ts`'s processor returns `{ computed: 0, ... }` — a successful, non-throwing return — on every tick where `isMarketOpen()` is true (a deliberate skip: this positional signal intentionally doesn't run during market hours). BullMQ fires `'completed'` identically whether real work happened or the run was a no-op, so `job_heartbeat` reports "success" every 30 min forever even if `confluence_signals` silently stopped updating entirely — only a DB-freshness check on the real output table can catch that.
  - **`news-sentiment`**: a different but real case — no deliberate skip, but a silent partial failure (e.g. every RSS source returning empty) would report "success" with `fetched_at` never advancing — the same "job succeeded but wrote nothing new" pattern this codebase's incident history keeps recording.
  - **`trendlyne-intraday`**: deliberately given **no** entry. `runIntradayScreenerScan()` only writes on a genuine new high-scoring breakout with no existing active signal, so zero new rows on a quiet day is normal — a naive freshness check would false-alarm. Worse, its write target (`unified_signals`, `signal_source='screener'`) is shared with several unrelated writers, so even a "recent rows exist" check would be misleadingly reassuring about this job specifically. A real check needs a scan-attempt log, out of scope here.
  - **Shipped**: two new `MONITOR_SCRIPTS` entries (`confluence-compute` — `staleLimitHours=10h`, since the real worst-case gap is the ~6h15m weekday-market-hours-skip window, not one clean 5-field cron pattern; `news-sentiment` — `cronPatterns=['*/15 * * * *']` + `graceMinutes=45`, genuinely 24/7 so an exact cron works), each wired to a real `getLastRunAt`/`getScriptStats` case in `monitor.router.ts` (without the former, the switch's `default: return null` would silently show "never run" forever — worse than no entry). Both ids added to `jobRegistryVsMonitorScripts.test.ts`'s `KNOWN_SAFE_OVERLAPS`. `monitorScriptsCronMirror.test.ts`, `monitorScriptsStaleLimitConsistency.test.ts` (new `marketHoursSkipGated` model, fixed 6.25h constant), and `monitorScriptsGraceMinutesConsistency.test.ts` all extended to cover the two new entries. New `everyMsMonitorCoverage.test.ts` (6 tests) encodes the finding directly — re-verifies `trendlyne-intraday`'s exemption reasoning against actual source (its write is still conditional, `unified_signals` still has multiple writers) rather than just asserting it, so a future refactor that changes either premise fails loud instead of leaving a stale exemption.
  - **Negative-controlled**: removed `confluence-compute`'s `getLastRunAt` case, corrupted `news-sentiment`'s `cronPatterns`/`graceMinutes`, corrupted `confluence-compute`'s `staleLimitHours` below 6.25h — all four caught, then restored. Full suite: 495/523 passed (was 485/513), same pre-existing failures only, `tsc --noEmit` clean.

- **`edge_adjustment_enabled` turned on, re-verified as a live no-op (2026-08-03).** Following the gap-analysis roadmap's #1 sequencing item, checked `app_settings.edge_adjustment_enabled` and found it **already `'true'`** — flipped by a concurrent session sometime after the 2026-07-23 no-op verification (that entry's "Flag currently OFF" is now stale; corrected here). Re-ran the read-only `scripts/diff_edge_adjustment.py` against live data rather than trusting the old check: **still a complete no-op** — BEAR AUC 0.630 and SIDEWAYS AUC 0.567 (moved from 0.618 since 07-23) both still clear the 0.55 trust floor with sufficient history (26-31 distinct days, 4 episodes each), so no shrinkage fires; 0 of 0 `recommendation_log` ACTIVE rows flip (that table now has 0 `technical_scan`-sourced ACTIVE rows, down from 45 in July — reflects normal turnover, not a bug), 0 of 21 `scoring_engine` win-probs changed, 0 of 2,259 `unified_ranker` bet sizes changed. HIGH_VOL still isn't `ready` (only 3 days of history). No code/restart needed — the flag is read live from the DB on every call, so it's already active in production. The mechanism will only start doing something the moment a regime with enough history drops below 0.55 AUC, which is exactly the point of it.

- **Score & signal table consolidation, phased execution of the previously-deferred "Phase 3" governance work (2026-08-03).** User asked to consolidate the 3 score tables + 6 (really 5 -- signals was already dropped) signal tables flagged in the gap-analysis roadmap; scoped via plan mode into a Score track (S1/S2) and a Signal track ("Cluster B-lite", continuing 2026-06's Cluster A) after a full consumer inventory (3 parallel research passes) found the naive interpretation was architecturally wrong -- `stock_scores`/`quant_scores` are `unified_ranker.py`'s *inputs*, not duplicates of `unified_recommendations`, so they cannot be physically merged into it.
  - **S1 (real correctness bug, not just cleanup):** `quant_scores` had three writers with a confirmed-live ordering conflict, verified directly against `queues.ts`'/`screeners.jobs.ts` cron patterns (not just claimed) -- `institutional_quant_engine.py` (ml-daily-ops, 7:30 PM IST, full `DELETE`+re-`INSERT`) was always clobbered same-night by `quantScoringService.ts`'s own upsert (11 PM IST, identical columns); live-verified via a seed-then-rerun smoke test that a real sibling column (`mf_quality_score`) survived a second engine run only after the fix. Repointed all 3 non-UI readers, dropped the table (177 live rows, all pre-migrated) via a new SQLite migration `074_drop_technical_analysis_signals` + live `DROP TABLE` on the Postgres container. **End-to-end live-verified with a real production run, not just a dry pass:** `technical_analysis_engine.py` actually executed against all 2,450 stocks, wrote 2,429 real `signal_source='technical'` rows, and all three repointed readers were confirmed reading real data afterward.

Renamed the misleadingly-named `getUnifiedSignals` tRPC procedure (it read `technical_signals`+`confluence_signals`, never `unified_signals`) to `getTechnicalConfluenceSignals`.

This mirrors the project's own established discipline (see the 2026-07-30 audit's repeated “deserves its own pass with a full consumer inventory” language) of not doing high-blast-radius consolidations as a drive-by.

- **signal_outcomes signal_source fix, closing the real "mixed label definition" mechanism (2026-08-03).** User asked to fix the full "Cluster B" (technical_signals rename + signal_outcomes drop + its data-quality issue) flagged as future work in the prior session. Investigated via plan mode rather than assuming the earlier framing was correct — and it was NOT: the "mixed label definition across horizons" finding from the 2026-07-30 audit undersold the real mechanism. `signal_outcomes` had no `signal_source` column, so THREE independent writers — `outcome_resolver.py` (h1/5/15, grades `technical_signals`), `confluence_outcome_tracker.py` (h1/3/7/14/30, grades the unrelated `confluence_signals` table), and a genuinely undiscovered third writer found only by grepping every remaining `signal_outcomes` reference at the end of the sweep, `signalOutcomesService.ts` (its own separately-scheduled `signal-outcomes-daily` cron, 9 AM IST weekdays, also grading `technical_signals` with yet a different methodology) — all guarded their dedup checks against "does ANY row exist for this key", regardless of writer/source. Whichever wrote first silently won a given `(symbol, signal_date, horizon_days)` forever; the horizon split the audit observed (h1/5/15 vs h3/7/14/30) was a byproduct of this collision-avoidance, not a designed labeling scheme. Verified this empirically against live data (NEUTRAL-band width per horizon, `computed_at` distribution) before designing the fix — an early hypothesis ("the resolver code was rewritten since the audit, historical rows are just stale") was tested against real data and refuted; the real mechanism was confirmed by tracing the actual cron schedules in `queues.ts`.
  - **Fix**: widened the key to `(symbol, signal_date, horizon_days, signal_source)`, mirroring `unified_signal_outcomes` (which already had exactly this column for exactly this reason — direct precedent, not a new pattern) and reusing Cluster A's exact SQLite rename→recreate→copy→drop migration dance. Stamped all three writers; `technicalSignalsService.ts`'s PENDING-seed row also needed the same stamp — without it, a seeded row at the column default (`unknown`) would never be matched by any resolver's `ON CONFLICT` target and would orphan permanently while a second row got created alongside it (found by reasoning through the seed/resolve lifecycle, not by a test failure). Backfilled 267,976 existing rows by inferring source from the already-correct `label_definition` split (sound, not a guess — the one-writer-per-horizon-bucket mechanism made the inference exact: 145,412 → technical, 122,564 → confluence, zero remaining unclassified). Audited and fixed ~20 consumer files across the ML stack — `ml_ensemble.py`'s core training query (both dialect branches, both label modes) was the single most important fix, arguably more consequential than anything else in this pass, since it's the platform's primary model. Two files needed the OPPOSITE filter (`confluence_ml_engine.py`, `confluence.router.ts`'s `getConfluenceOutcomes`) — confirmed by reading each query's actual JOIN logic rather than assuming every consumer wants 'technical'.
  - **Scope decisions, both confirmed with the user via AskUserQuestion before proceeding**: fix `signal_outcomes` now (real, active, ongoing correctness bug — not deferred); skip the `technical_signals`→`technical_features` rename (confirmed via exhaustive grep to touch 141 files, not the ~35/~69 originally estimated — purely cosmetic, zero functional benefit, not worth 141-file mechanical risk alongside a real bug fix). Also investigated and REJECTED merging `signal_outcomes`/`unified_signal_outcomes` into one table (the original "collapse to one outcome table" target) — `unified_signal_outcomes` is FK'd to a specific `unified_signals` row instance; `signal_outcomes` grades events that mostly have no corresponding `unified_signals` row at all. These answer genuinely different questions; forcing a merge would require dropping the FK or writing a `unified_signals` row for every graded event. A closed question, not a deferred one.
  - **Live-verified, not just unit-tested**: ran BOTH `outcome_resolver.py` and `confluence_outcome_tracker.py` for real against production (43 + 510,754 outcomes respectively); confirmed real symbols (e.g. `ADANIPORTS`/2026-08-02/h1) now genuinely carry both a technical- and confluence-sourced row simultaneously — structurally impossible before this fix; spot-checked `ml_calibration.per_regime_auc` (plausible AUCs 0.56-0.81) and `confluence_ml_engine.build_training_data` against the fresh data. New regression test for the `signalOutcomesService.ts` writer gap, negative-controlled: reverting its `ON CONFLICT` target throws `SQLITE_ERROR` against the now-widened schema (a harder failure than the original silent-collision bug, since the schema itself changed underneath the old target).
  - **Process note**: the Agent tool hit a session usage limit mid-investigation (three parallel research agents all failed simultaneously) — confirmed direct tool calls (Grep/Read/Bash) still worked, and completed the rest of the investigation via direct greps/reads instead of retrying the Agent tool, rather than blocking on it.

Full Python suite after both additions: 981 passed, 85 skipped, 0 failed. **Still open, not attempted this session** (each needs its own dedicated pass, not a drive-by): the remaining ~115 untested fetchers (`nt_change_oi_fetcher.py`/`nt_oi_snapshot_fetcher.py`/`nt_pcr_ts_fetcher.py`/`nt_vix_fetcher.py` checked and confirmed NOT to need the DB-stored NiftyTrader Bearer token this file previously flagged as blocking — they use plain public endpoints — but their `execute()`/`executemany()` calls route through `db_compat.get_engine()` directly with no DB-injection point, unlike the `con`-parameter style `mc_earnings_fetcher.py`/`mc_pricefeed_fetcher.py` use, so testing them safely needs a `monkeypatch` on the module-level DB functions rather than the pattern used here); actually retraining the LSTM (still rolled back to v3, 6+ weeks stale); the 3 unbuilt endpoint-corpus fetchers (superstar investors, event calendar, the `fno/market/filter` options surface beyond `so_option_chain_fetcher.py`); `screener_appearances` bitemporality (won't-fix, mitigated forward via `screener_membership_snapshot`).
- **GNews integrated as a news source tier, then redesigned twice in the same session based on live results + user feedback (2026-08-04).** User asked to integrate GNews for stock/market/global news. First cut was one `runGNewsCycle()` (3 calls, flat 3h cadence) gated only on `GNEWS_API_KEY` being present. User then gave a real key (added to `.env`, gitignored) and asked to keep interval choices appropriate per use-case and to also cover per-stock news specifically. Final shipped design in `newsSentimentService.ts`:
  - Split into 3 differentiated cycles instead of one flat job: `runGNewsMarketCycle()` (India business headlines + NSE/BSE/Nifty/Sensex search, every 2h), `runGNewsStocksCycle()` (one OR-joined search over a rotating batch of 5 names from the top-60-by-market-cap universe, cursor persisted in `app_settings.gnews_stocks_rotation_offset`, full universe rotates every 12h, every 1h), `runGNewsGlobalCycle()` (world business headlines, every 6h) — combined 52 req/day against the 100/day free-tier cap, weighted toward the cycles that actually feed trading decisions.
  - **Live-verified against the real endpoint and found a real bug before shipping**: 3 concurrent GNews calls via `Promise.all` got 2× HTTP 429 (a burst/concurrency limit on the free tier, not the 100/day quota — a lone call immediately after succeeded with real articles). Fixed by pacing `runGNewsMarketCycle`'s 2 calls 1.2s apart instead of running them concurrently.
  - **Added an explicit `app_settings.gnews_enabled` gate, default OFF, on top of the API-key gate** — the live response itself carries `information.realTimeArticles: "Real-time news data is only available on paid plans. Free plan has a 12-hour delay."`, a materially different freshness guarantee than every other source in this pipeline (RSS ~15-30min, MC/BSE genuinely live); shipping it enabled-by-default would have silently mixed 12h-stale data into what's presented as live market/stock news. Flip on with `UPDATE app_settings SET value='true' WHERE key='gnews_enabled'` once that tradeoff is accepted for a given use case.
  - **Added `runMcStockNewsCycle()`** — a genuinely live per-stock source (no delay caveat) reusing `fetchMcStockNews()`/`resolveMoneycontrolSymbol()` already proven working for the per-stock news tab (`McNewsCard.tsx`, 2026-07-31). No metered quota (`mcFetchJson`'s own `Semaphore` throttles concurrency platform-wide already), so it runs denser than Google News: top-100 by market cap, every 2h. Live-verified: 10 companies → 61 real, correctly-tagged articles in one run.
  - **While live-verifying, found (not fixed) a real pre-existing bug**: `mcApiService.ts`'s `decodeMangledEscapes()` requires a non-alphanumeric character immediately before a mangled `uXXXX` sequence to repair it, so `"Coforge's"` mangled to `"Coforgeu2019s"` is NOT repaired (the `e` before `u` blocks the match) while `"...Pick u2013 Why..."` (space before `u`) is. Live-confirmed via a real ingested MC headline stored uncorrected. The function's other two guards (requires a digit in the 4 hex chars; requires a non-ASCII decoded codepoint) likely already provide enough disambiguation that the prefix-boundary requirement is over-cautious and blocks the single most common real case (mangled possessive apostrophes) — flagged as a follow-up, not fixed this session (found via a different task than what was asked).
  - **Adding the new job blocks in `queues.ts` broke 2 unrelated tests**: `jobRegistryGraceMinutesConsistency.test.ts`/`monitorScriptsGraceMinutesConsistency.test.ts` scan `queues.ts` source for a `lockDuration` value within `MAX_LOOKAHEAD` (4000 chars) of each job's registration marker; the new blocks pushed `news-sentiment`'s distance to 5378 chars. Fixed by switching both tests' marker for this job to `newsSentimentWorker = new Worker`, the same override pattern already established for `intraday-fetcher`.
  - Also added `news-sentiment-freshness` to `dataQualityChecks.ts` — `news_sentiment_items` had never had a freshness check at all (the 2026-08-03 sweep only covered Python `runPython()` call sites; every writer into this table is TS), found while wiring this in.
  - 4 new job names on the existing `news-sentiment` BullMQ queue (`gnews-market-refresh`/`gnews-stocks-refresh`/`gnews-global-refresh`/`mc-stock-news-refresh`) — no new queue, no new monitoring registration needed. 16 new/updated unit tests total (`newsSentimentGNews.test.ts`, `mcStockNewsCycle.test.ts`, plus the 2 fixed consistency tests). Full suite 2,936/2,936 passed, `tsc --noEmit` clean — both verified after every redesign, not just at the end.
